<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Pain_au_chocolat_Luc_Viatour.jpg/480px-Pain_au_chocolat_Luc_Viatour.jpg" alt="Photo of Pain au Chocolat">

#### 🥐 [Pain au Chocolat](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-pain-au-chocolat/)
French · breakfast · hard  
**$0.21/serving** · 56 kcal · P2 · C6 · F3 · ⏱ 300 min · 🍽 8  

Classic French laminated viennoiserie: flaky, buttery croissant dough wrapped around two batons of dark chocolate, folded and proofed slowly, then baked until deep golden and shatteringly crisp. A real one-day project, but the payoff is bakery-quality pastries from your own oven.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — bread flour (high-protein) preferred for structure
- **Butter** — European-style 82%+ butterfat; ~140g goes into the butter block for lamination, rest in dough
- **Whole milk** — lukewarm, ~140ml for the dough
- **Sugar** — 0.15 tbsp
- **Instant yeast** — about 1 tbsp
- **Salt** — 0.1 tsp
- **Dark chocolate** — cut into 16 batons (~10g each), or use bakery chocolate sticks
- **Eggs** — for egg wash, beaten with a splash of milk

**Steps**

1. Make the detrempe (dough): Mix 250g bread flour, 30g sugar, 1 tbsp instant yeast, 1 tsp salt, 140ml lukewarm milk, and 20g soft butter into a shaggy dough. Knead 5-7 minutes until smooth but not over-developed. Shape into a rectangle, wrap, and chill at least 1 hour (or overnight).
2. Make the butter block: Pound 140g cold butter between parchment and roll into a 12x16cm rectangle, then chill until firm but pliable.
3. Encase the butter: Roll the chilled dough into a rectangle twice the size of the butter block. Lay the butter on one half and fold the dough over to seal it completely, pinching the edges.
4. First fold: Roll the package into a long rectangle and fold in thirds like a letter. Wrap and chill 30-45 minutes to keep the butter cold and the layers distinct.
5. Second and third folds: Repeat the roll-and-letter-fold twice more, chilling 30-45 minutes between each fold. You now have hundreds of butter layers, the key to a flaky crumb.
6. Cut and shape: Roll the laminated dough to about 4mm thick and cut into 8 rectangles (~8x14cm). Lay a chocolate baton near one end, roll once, add a second baton, then roll up snugly and place seam-side down.
7. Proof: Arrange on parchment-lined trays, cover loosely, and proof at warm room temperature (24-26C) for 2-3 hours until jiggly, puffy, and nearly doubled. Do not rush this.
8. Bake: Brush gently with egg wash. Bake at 200C (400F) for 18-22 minutes until deep golden brown and the layers are crisp. Cool on a rack at least 15 minutes so the chocolate sets and the crumb finishes.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Luc Viatour — CC BY-SA 3.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-pain-au-chocolat/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/d/dc/Paris-Brest_pastry_%28large%29.png" alt="Photo of Paris-Brest">

#### 🥮 [Paris-Brest](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-paris-brest/)
French · snack · hard  
**$1.38/serving** · 558 kcal · P9 · C40 · F40 · ⏱ 180 min · 🍽 8  

The classic French pâtisserie created for the 1910 Paris-Brest-Paris bicycle race: a ring of pâte à choux, baked until deeply golden and crisp, split and piped full of silky praline mousseline cream — a buttercream-lightened pastry cream flavored with hazelnut-almond praline. Crowned with sliced almonds and a snow of powdered sugar. Nutty, airy, and unmistakably Parisian.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Water** — 1/2 cup water (for the choux)
- **Whole milk** — 1/2 cup whole milk (for the choux)
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, cubed — for the choux
- **Sugar** — 1 tsp sugar (for the choux)
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **All-purpose flour** — 1 cup all-purpose flour, sifted
- **Eggs** — 4 large eggs, plus 1 more beaten for egg wash
- **Slivered almonds** — 1/2 cup sliced/slivered almonds, for topping
- **Whole milk** — 2 cups whole milk (for the pastry cream base of the mousseline)
- **Sugar** — 1/2 cup sugar (for the pastry cream)
- **Eggs** — 4 large egg yolks (for the pastry cream)
- **Cornstarch** — 1/4 cup cornstarch (for the pastry cream)
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks) unsalted butter, softened — for the mousseline
- **Almonds** — 1 cup hazelnuts (or almonds) toasted, for homemade praline paste
- **Sugar** — 1/2 cup sugar, for the praline caramel
- **Powdered sugar** — 2 tbsp powdered sugar, for dusting

**Steps**

1. Make the praline paste: Heat 1/2 cup sugar in a dry pan over medium until it melts into an amber caramel (no stirring; swirl gently). Stir in the toasted hazelnuts to coat, pour onto a parchment-lined tray, and cool until hard. Break up and blitz in a food processor for several minutes, scraping down, until it becomes a smooth, oily paste. Set aside.
2. Make the pastry cream: Warm 2 cups whole milk in a saucepan. In a bowl, whisk 4 egg yolks with 1/2 cup sugar and 1/4 cup cornstarch until pale. Temper by whisking in the hot milk, return to the pan, and cook over medium heat, whisking constantly, until it boils and thickens (about 2 minutes). Off heat, whisk in vanilla and the praline paste. Press plastic wrap onto the surface and chill cold, at least 2 hours.
3. Make the choux: Preheat oven to 400F (200C). Bring 1/2 cup water, 1/2 cup milk, the cubed butter, 1 tsp sugar, and 1/2 tsp salt to a rolling boil. Dump in the sifted flour all at once and stir vigorously with a wooden spoon until a smooth ball forms, then keep cooking and stirring 2 minutes to dry the paste so it films the pan bottom.
4. Transfer the paste to a bowl and beat 1 minute to cool slightly. Beat in 4 eggs one at a time, fully incorporating each, until the dough is glossy and falls from the spoon in a thick ribbon (a 'V' shape). Spoon into a piping bag fitted with a large star tip.
5. Trace an 8-inch circle on parchment as a guide (pencil-side down). Pipe a ring of choux along the circle, then a second ring just outside it, and a third ring on top straddling the seam — three connected rings forming one thick wheel. Brush with beaten egg and scatter sliced almonds over the top.
6. Bake at 400F for 15 minutes, then lower to 350F (175C) and bake 25-30 minutes more until deeply golden and firm — do not open the oven early or the ring will collapse. Turn off the oven, crack the door, and let it dry 10 minutes. Cool completely on a rack.
7. Finish the mousseline: Beat the softened 1 cup butter until light and fluffy. With the mixer running, add the cold praline pastry cream a spoonful at a time until smooth, thick, and pipeable. (If it looks split, keep beating — it comes together.) Load into a piping bag with a star tip.
8. Assemble: Slice the cooled ring in half horizontally with a serrated knife. Pipe generous rosettes of praline mousseline over the bottom ring, set the top back on, and dust heavily with powdered sugar. Chill 30 minutes before slicing into wedges to serve.

</details>

<sub>📷 Loneshieling, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-paris-brest/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/e/ea/Religieuse_%C3%A0_la_vanille.jpg" alt="Photo of Religieuse au Chocolat">

#### 🍫 [Religieuse au Chocolat](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-religieuse/)
French · snack · hard  
**$1.81/serving** · 393 kcal · P8 · C28 · F27 · ⏱ 180 min · 🍽 8  

The classic Parisian "little nun" — two stacked choux puffs, the large body and small head, each filled with chocolate crème pâtissière, glazed in glossy chocolate fondant, and joined by a piped buttercream collar. This is the real pâtisserie version: a proper pâte à choux baked until hollow and crisp, a from-scratch pastry cream thickened with egg yolks and cornstarch, and a fondant-style glaze. It takes patience but every component is achievable in a home kitchen, and the result looks and tastes like it came from a Left Bank patisserie.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Water** — 1/2 cup water (for the choux)
- **Whole milk** — 1/2 cup whole milk (for the choux)
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, cubed, for the choux
- **Sugar** — 1 tbsp sugar (for the choux)
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt (for the choux)
- **All-purpose flour** — 1 cup all-purpose flour (for the choux)
- **Eggs** — 4 large eggs, plus 1 more if needed, for the choux
- **Whole milk** — 2 cups whole milk (for the chocolate crème pâtissière)
- **Eggs** — 4 large egg yolks (for the crème pâtissière)
- **Sugar** — 1/2 cup sugar (for the crème pâtissière)
- **Cornstarch** — 1/4 cup cornstarch (for the crème pâtissière)
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract (for the crème pâtissière)
- **Dark chocolate** — 5 oz dark chocolate (about 70%), chopped, for the crème pâtissière
- **Butter** — 2 tbsp unsalted butter (for the crème pâtissière)
- **Powdered sugar** — 1 cup powdered sugar (for the chocolate fondant glaze)
- **Cocoa powder** — 2 tbsp cocoa powder (for the chocolate fondant glaze)
- **Corn syrup** — 1 tbsp corn syrup, for glossy fondant glaze
- **Water** — about 2 tbsp hot water, to loosen the glaze
- **Butter** — 6 tbsp unsalted butter, softened, for the buttercream collar
- **Powdered sugar** — 1/2 cup powdered sugar (for the buttercream collar)
- **Cocoa powder** — 1 tbsp cocoa powder (for the buttercream collar)

**Steps**

1. Make the crème pâtissière first so it can chill. Warm 2 cups whole milk in a saucepan until steaming. In a bowl, whisk 4 egg yolks, 1/2 cup sugar, and 1/4 cup cornstarch to a smooth paste. Slowly pour the hot milk into the yolks while whisking, then return everything to the pan. Cook over medium heat, whisking constantly, until it boils and thickens to a pudding (about 1-2 minutes of bubbling). Off the heat, whisk in the 5 oz chopped dark chocolate, 2 tbsp butter, and 2 tsp vanilla until smooth. Press plastic wrap directly onto the surface and refrigerate at least 2 hours.
2. Make the choux. Preheat the oven to 425F and line two baking sheets with parchment. In a saucepan, bring 1/2 cup water, 1/2 cup milk, 1 stick cubed butter, 1 tbsp sugar, and 1/2 tsp salt to a full boil. Dump in 1 cup flour all at once and stir hard with a wooden spoon until it forms a smooth ball that pulls from the sides, then keep stirring 2 more minutes to dry the dough out (a thin film should form on the pan bottom).
3. Transfer the dough to a bowl and let it cool 5 minutes. Beat in the eggs one at a time, fully incorporating each before adding the next. Stop when the dough is glossy and falls from the spoon in a slow V-shaped ribbon — you may not need the 5th egg. Spoon into a piping bag fitted with a plain round tip.
4. Pipe 8 large mounds about 2 inches wide for the bodies, and 8 small mounds about 1 inch wide for the heads, spacing them apart. Wet a fingertip and gently pat down any peaks so they bake evenly. Bake at 425F for 10 minutes, then drop the oven to 375F and bake 15-20 more minutes until deeply golden and firm — do not open the door early or they will collapse. The small heads bake faster, so pull them when set.
5. Cool the puffs completely, then pierce a small hole in the base of each with the piping tip. Whisk the chilled chocolate crème pâtissière smooth and transfer to a piping bag. Fill each body and each head from the bottom until heavy, then set aside.
6. Make the fondant glaze. Sift 1 cup powdered sugar with 2 tbsp cocoa into a bowl. Stir in 1 tbsp corn syrup and just enough hot water (start with 1 tbsp, add by the teaspoon) to make a thick but pourable, shiny glaze that coats a spoon. Dip the top of each puff into the glaze, letting excess drip off, and set right-side up.
7. Make the buttercream collar. Beat 6 tbsp softened butter with 1/2 cup powdered sugar and 1 tbsp cocoa until light and fluffy. Spoon into a piping bag fitted with a small star tip.
8. Assemble each religieuse: set a small glazed head on top of a large glazed body, then pipe a ring of little buttercream rosettes around the seam where they meet to form the nun's collar. Chill 20 minutes to set before serving the same day.

</details>

<sub>📷 Thomon, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-religieuse/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Tarte_au_citron%2C_vendue_%C3%A0_Lannion.jpg/480px-Tarte_au_citron%2C_vendue_%C3%A0_Lannion.jpg" alt="Photo of Tarte au Citron">

#### 🍋 [Tarte au Citron](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-tarte-au-citron/)
French · snack · medium  
**$1.20/serving** · 417 kcal · P6 · C37 · F27 · ⏱ 300 min · 🍽 8  

The classic French lemon tart: a crisp, buttery pâte sucrée shell filled with a silky, intensely tangy lemon cream baked just until set. No meringue, no fillers — just real lemon, eggs, butter, sugar and cream, the way a Parisian pâtissier would make it. Bright, smooth, and elegant.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 1 1/4 cups all-purpose flour (for the pâte sucrée shell)
- **Butter** — 1 1/8 cups (2 1/4 sticks) unsalted butter total — 7 tbsp cold and cubed for the crust, 11 tbsp softened for the lemon cream
- **Powdered sugar** — 1/2 cup powdered sugar (for the crust)
- **Sugar** — 3/4 cup granulated sugar (for the lemon cream)
- **Eggs** — 4 large eggs — 1 for the crust, 3 whole eggs plus context below for the filling
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp fine salt, split between crust and filling
- **Lemon** — 4 lemons — for about 2/3 cup fresh juice and the zest of 3
- **Lemon zest** — 2 tsp finely grated lemon zest (use the zest from your fresh lemons; bottled zest only if needed)
- **Vanilla extract** — 1/2 tsp vanilla extract (for the crust)
- **Heavy cream** — optional: a splash of heavy cream to loosen the finished lemon cream if very thick _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Make the pâte sucrée: In a bowl, rub 7 tbsp cold cubed butter into the flour, powdered sugar, and 1/4 tsp salt until sandy. Mix in 1 egg and 1/2 tsp vanilla just until a dough forms. Flatten into a disc, wrap, and chill at least 1 hour.
2. Roll the chilled dough on a floured surface to about 1/8 inch thick and line a 9-inch tart pan, pressing into the corners and trimming the edges. Prick the base all over with a fork and freeze 20 minutes. Preheat the oven to 350°F (175°C).
3. Blind bake: Line the shell with parchment and fill with dried beans or pie weights. Bake 18 minutes, then remove the weights and parchment and bake 8–10 minutes more until the base is dry and pale gold. Set aside; lower the oven to 300°F (150°C).
4. Make the lemon cream: Zest 3 lemons and juice all 4 to get about 2/3 cup juice. In a heatproof bowl, whisk together 3 whole eggs, 3/4 cup granulated sugar, the lemon zest, the lemon juice, and a pinch of salt.
5. Set the bowl over a pan of barely simmering water and whisk constantly until the mixture thickens to a custard that coats the back of a spoon, about 8–10 minutes (170°F / 77°C). Do not let it boil.
6. Off the heat, cool the curd to about 140°F (60°C), then blend in 11 tbsp softened butter a little at a time — using an immersion blender if you have one — until completely smooth and glossy. Loosen with a splash of cream only if it seems too stiff.
7. Pour the warm lemon cream into the baked tart shell and smooth the top. Bake at 300°F (150°C) for 8–10 minutes, just until the filling is set at the edges with a faint wobble in the center.
8. Cool the tart completely at room temperature, then refrigerate at least 3 hours until fully set. Serve chilled or barely cool, optionally dusted with a little powdered sugar at the very last moment.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-tarte-au-citron/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/Tarte_aux_Pommes_-_Julien_Plumart_2025-07-30.jpg/480px-Tarte_aux_Pommes_-_Julien_Plumart_2025-07-30.jpg" alt="Photo of Tarte aux Pommes (Classic French Apple Tart)">

#### 🥧 [Tarte aux Pommes (Classic French Apple Tart)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-tarte-aux-pommes/)
French · snack · medium  
**$1.11/serving** · 322 kcal · P4 · C45 · F15 · ⏱ 120 min · 🍽 8  

The real French apple tart: a crisp, buttery pâte brisée shell spread with a thin layer of homemade apple compote, topped with paper-thin apple slices fanned into rosettes, baked until the edges caramelize, then finished with a glossy apricot glaze. Rustic precision, no shortcuts.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 1 1/2 cups all-purpose flour, for the pâte brisée
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) cold unsalted butter, cubed, for the crust
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp fine salt
- **Sugar** — 1 tbsp sugar for the dough
- **Eggs** — 1 large egg yolk (reserve white) to bind the dough
- **Water** — 3 tbsp ice water, added gradually
- **Apple** — 5 firm-tart apples total (e.g. Granny Smith / Honeycrisp): 2 for the compote, 3 for the topping
- **White sugar** — 1/3 cup sugar, split between the compote and sprinkling on top
- **Butter** — 2 tbsp unsalted butter: 1 tbsp for the compote, 1 tbsp dotted over the topping
- **Lemon** — 1 lemon, juiced, to keep apples from browning and brighten the compote
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract for the compote
- **Cinnamon** — 1/2 tsp cinnamon (optional, lightly used in the compote) _(optional)_
- **Apricot jam** — 3 tbsp apricot jam, warmed and strained, for the glaze

**Steps**

1. Make the pâte brisée: In a bowl, whisk the flour, salt, and 1 tbsp sugar. Cut in the cold cubed butter with your fingertips or a pastry cutter until the mixture looks like coarse crumbs with some pea-sized bits remaining. Whisk the egg yolk into the ice water, then drizzle it in, tossing with a fork, until the dough just holds together when squeezed. Do not overwork. Pat into a disk, wrap, and chill at least 30 minutes.
2. Make the apple compote: Peel, core, and dice 2 apples. Cook in a saucepan with 1 tbsp butter, about 2 tbsp of the sugar, half the lemon juice, the vanilla, and optional cinnamon over medium heat, stirring, for 10-12 minutes until soft. Mash to a chunky purée and let cool. It should be thick, not watery.
3. Roll and blind-prep the crust: On a floured surface, roll the chilled dough into a 12-inch round about 1/8 inch thick. Drape into a 9-inch tart pan, press into the edges, and trim. Prick the base all over with a fork and chill 15 minutes while the oven heats to 375°F (190°C).
4. Slice the topping apples: Peel, halve, and core the remaining 3 apples. Slice into very thin half-moons (about 1/8 inch). Toss with the remaining lemon juice to prevent browning.
5. Assemble: Spread the cooled compote in an even layer over the chilled crust. Arrange the apple slices in tight, overlapping concentric circles, fanning them like rose petals from the outer edge inward. Pack them snugly, as they shrink while baking.
6. Finish the top: Sprinkle the remaining sugar evenly over the apples and dot with the remaining 1 tbsp butter, broken into small pieces, so the slices caramelize and the tips brown.
7. Bake at 375°F (190°C) for 45-55 minutes, until the crust is deep golden, the apple edges are tinged brown, and the slices are tender. If the rim browns too fast, shield it loosely with foil.
8. Glaze and serve: Warm the apricot jam with a splash of water until fluid, strain out any chunks, and brush a thin, even coat over the warm apples for a bakery shine. Cool to just warm or room temperature before slicing. Serve plain or with crème fraîche.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-tarte-aux-pommes/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/e/ea/Tarte_Bourdaloue.jpg" alt="Photo of Tarte Bourdaloue (Almond Pear Tart)">

#### 🍐 [Tarte Bourdaloue (Almond Pear Tart)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-tarte-bourdaloue/)
French · snack · medium  
**$1.54/serving** · 367 kcal · P5 · C30 · F24 · ⏱ 180 min · 🍽 8  

The classic Parisian almond-pear tart, named for the rue Bourdaloue where it was created in the 1850s. A crisp pâte sucrée shell is filled with rich almond frangipane, crowned with fanned poached pear halves, then baked until the cream sets golden and souffléd around the fruit. Finished with a glossy apricot glaze and toasted sliced almonds. Faithful to the original: real almond flour in the frangipane, a touch of rum and almond extract, and pears that sink slightly into the cream as it bakes.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 1 1/4 cups all-purpose flour (for the pâte sucrée)
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) cold unsalted butter, cubed, for the crust
- **Powdered sugar** — 1/2 cup powdered sugar (for the crust)
- **Almond flour** — 1/4 cup almond flour (adds tenderness to the crust)
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt
- **Eggs** — 3 large eggs total: 1 for the crust, 2 for the frangipane
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) softened unsalted butter, for the frangipane
- **Sugar** — 1/2 cup granulated sugar (for the frangipane)
- **Almond flour** — 1 cup almond flour (the heart of the frangipane)
- **All-purpose flour** — 2 tbsp all-purpose flour (to stabilize the frangipane)
- **Almond extract** — 1/2 tsp almond extract
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Rum** — 1 oz (2 tbsp) dark rum, traditional in frangipane
- **Pear** — 3 ripe but firm pears (Bartlett or Bosc), peeled, halved, cored
- **Lemon** — 1 lemon, juiced, to keep the pears from browning
- **Apricot jam** — 3 tbsp apricot jam, warmed, for the finishing glaze
- **Slivered almonds** — 1/4 cup slivered or sliced almonds, for the top

**Steps**

1. Make the pâte sucrée: Pulse 1 1/4 cups flour, the powdered sugar, 1/4 cup almond flour, and salt in a food processor. Add the cold cubed butter and pulse to coarse crumbs. Add 1 egg and pulse just until the dough clumps. Turn out, press into a disk, wrap, and chill at least 1 hour.
2. Roll the chilled dough on a floured surface to about 1/8 inch and line a 9-inch tart pan, pressing into the corners and trimming the edges. Prick the base with a fork and freeze 15 minutes. Meanwhile, preheat the oven to 350°F (175°C).
3. Blind-bake the shell: line with parchment and pie weights (or dried beans), bake 15 minutes, remove the weights and parchment, and bake 5 minutes more until the base is pale and dry. Set aside to cool slightly. Leave the oven at 350°F.
4. Make the frangipane: cream the softened butter and granulated sugar until light, 2-3 minutes. Beat in the 2 remaining eggs one at a time, then mix in 1 cup almond flour, 2 tbsp flour, the almond extract, vanilla, and rum until smooth and fluffy.
5. Toss the pear halves with the lemon juice. Spread the frangipane evenly into the cooled shell. Slice each pear half crosswise into thin segments, keeping the shape, then gently fan and press them cut-side down into the cream, stems pointing toward the center, arranged like spokes.
6. Scatter the slivered almonds over the exposed frangipane between the pears. Bake at 350°F for 45-55 minutes, until the frangipane is puffed, deep golden, and set in the center (a knife inserted in the cream comes out clean). Tent with foil if the almonds brown too fast.
7. Cool the tart 20 minutes, then warm the apricot jam with a splash of water until fluid and brush it over the pears and frangipane for a glossy finish. Cool completely before unmolding so the frangipane fully sets. Serve at room temperature, plain or with crème fraîche.

</details>

<sub>📷 traaf, CC BY-SA 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-tarte-bourdaloue/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Vanilla_bean_souffle_at_Emerils.jpg/480px-Vanilla_bean_souffle_at_Emerils.jpg" alt="Photo of Vanilla Souffle">

#### 🍮 [Vanilla Souffle](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-vanilla-souffle/)
French · snack · hard  
**$0.87/serving** · 164 kcal · P11 · C10 · F9 · ⏱ 40 min · 🍽 4  

A classic French vanilla souffle: a silky vanilla pastry-cream base lightened with billowing whipped egg whites, baked until it crowns proud and golden over the rim. Airy, just-set in the center, and fragrant with real vanilla. Serve the instant it leaves the oven, dusted with powdered sugar.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Butter** — plus extra, softened, for greasing the ramekins
- **Sugar** — granulated; some for coating ramekins, rest split between base and whites
- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose, ~3 tbsp for the thickened base
- **Milk** — whole milk, ~1 cup, warmed
- **Eggs** — 4 yolks + 5 whites (save 1 extra white for volume)
- **Vanilla extract** — real vanilla extract, ~2 tsp (or seeds of 1 vanilla bean)
- **Cream of tartar** — 1/4 tsp, to stabilize the whipped whites
- **Salt** — pinch, in the base
- **Powdered sugar** — for dusting before serving _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 400F (200C) with a rack in the lower third. Generously brush four 6-8oz ramekins with softened butter using upward strokes, then coat the insides fully with granulated sugar, tapping out the excess. This grip lets the souffle climb. Chill the ramekins while you work.
2. Make the base: melt 2 tbsp butter in a saucepan over medium heat, whisk in the flour, and cook 1 minute to a pale roux. Gradually whisk in the warm milk and cook, whisking constantly, until very thick and smooth, 2-3 minutes. Off heat, whisk in a pinch of salt, half the sugar, and the vanilla.
3. Whisk the 4 egg yolks into the warm base one at a time until glossy and uniform. Scrape into a large bowl and let cool to just warm (it should fall in thick ribbons).
4. In a spotlessly clean bowl, beat the 5 egg whites with the cream of tartar on medium until foamy, then raise the speed and rain in the remaining sugar. Beat to firm, glossy peaks that hold their shape but still look moist, not dry or clumpy.
5. Whisk a third of the whites into the yolk base to loosen it. Then gently fold in the rest in two additions with a spatula, cutting down and lifting over, until just combined with no white streaks, keeping as much air as possible.
6. Divide the batter among the ramekins, filling to the rim. Smooth the tops level with an offset spatula, then run your thumb around the inside edge to wipe a clean moat, which helps an even rise.
7. Place on a baking sheet and bake 12-16 minutes, until the souffles have risen 1-2 inches above the rim, are golden on top, and just jiggle slightly in the center. Do not open the oven for the first 10 minutes.
8. Dust immediately with powdered sugar and serve at once, before they deflate.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Coastie Wife 2008 — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-vanilla-souffle/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7a/B%C3%BBche_de_No%C3%ABl_chocolat_framboise_maison.jpg/480px-B%C3%BBche_de_No%C3%ABl_chocolat_framboise_maison.jpg" alt="Photo of Yule Log (Bûche de Noël)">

#### 🪵 [Yule Log (Bûche de Noël)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-yule-log/)
French · snack · hard  
**$1.24/serving** · 125 kcal · P4 · C19 · F4 · ⏱ 120 min · 🍽 10  

A classic French Christmas dessert: a feather-light cocoa sponge rolled around lightly sweetened vanilla whipped cream, blanketed in glossy dark chocolate ganache, then forked into bark and dusted with powdered-sugar "snow." Showy but very achievable.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Eggs** — separated; whole-egg foam method for the cocoa génoise
- **White sugar** — about 2/3 cup, split between the sponge and the whipped cream
- **All-purpose flour** — about 1/2 cup all-purpose; sift with cocoa
- **Dutch process cocoa** — about 1/3 cup Dutch-process cocoa for the sponge and to dust the towel
- **Baking powder** — 1 tsp
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp total, split between sponge and cream
- **Heavy cream** — 1 1/2 cups: ~1 cup whipped for filling, 1/2 cup heated for ganache
- **Powdered sugar** — for sweetening the whipped cream and dusting as snow
- **Dark chocolate** — about 8 oz, finely chopped, for the ganache coating
- **Butter** — 1 tbsp, stirred into ganache for shine
- **Instant coffee** — 1/2 tsp dissolved into the warm cream to deepen the chocolate _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat oven to 375F (190C). Line a rimmed half-sheet pan with parchment and lightly grease it. Sift together the flour, Dutch cocoa, baking powder, and salt.
2. Separate the eggs. Whip the whites to soft peaks, then rain in half the white sugar and beat to stiff, glossy peaks. In a second bowl, beat the yolks with the remaining sugar and 1 tsp vanilla until pale, thick, and ribboning, about 3 minutes.
3. Fold the sifted cocoa-flour into the yolks just until combined, then fold in the whites in three additions, keeping the batter airy. Spread evenly into the pan and bake 10-12 minutes, until the top springs back when pressed.
4. While it bakes, dust a clean kitchen towel generously with cocoa. Invert the hot cake onto the towel, peel off the parchment, and starting from a short end roll the cake up inside the towel. Let it cool fully rolled so it sets into a curl without cracking.
5. Whip the cream for the filling with the powdered sugar and remaining vanilla to firm peaks. Gently unroll the cooled cake, spread the whipped cream to the edges, and re-roll snugly (without the towel) into a log.
6. Make the ganache: heat 1/2 cup heavy cream (with the instant coffee, if using) until steaming, pour over the chopped dark chocolate, let sit 2 minutes, then stir smooth and stir in the butter. Let it thicken to a spreadable consistency.
7. Trim the ends on a diagonal and set one trimmed piece on the log as a knot/branch. Place the log on a platter and frost completely with ganache, including the stub.
8. Drag the tines of a fork lengthwise through the ganache to make bark, leaving the cut spiral ends exposed. Chill at least 1 hour to set.
9. Just before serving, dust with powdered sugar for snow. Slice with a warm knife to reveal the spiral.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-yule-log/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/77/Pastry-Turnover-Apple.jpg/480px-Pastry-Turnover-Apple.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Apple Turnovers">

#### 🥧 [Classic Apple Turnovers](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-apple-turnovers/)
French-American · snack · medium  
**$0.95/serving** · 418 kcal · P4 · C45 · F24 · ⏱ 120 min · 🍽 8  

Flaky, golden puff pastry pockets filled with cinnamon-spiced apples cooked down with brown sugar and a touch of lemon. Egg-washed, sugar-dusted, and baked until shatteringly crisp — the real bakery-style turnover a skilled home baker makes from scratch-rolled pastry and ripe apples.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 cups all-purpose flour, plus more for dusting
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks) cold unsalted butter, cubed
- **Water** — 1/2 cup ice water
- **Apple** — 3 firm-tart apples (Granny Smith), peeled and diced
- **Brown sugar** — 1/3 cup packed brown sugar
- **Sugar** — 2 tbsp granulated sugar, plus extra for dusting
- **Cinnamon** — 1 tsp ground cinnamon
- **Nutmeg** — 1/4 tsp ground nutmeg
- **Bottled lemon juice** — 1 tbsp lemon juice
- **Cornstarch** — 1 tbsp cornstarch
- **Butter** — 1 tbsp butter (for cooking the apples)
- **Eggs** — 1 egg, beaten with 1 tsp water (egg wash)

**Steps**

1. Make the rough puff pastry: In a bowl, whisk together the 2 cups flour and 1/2 tsp salt. Add the cubed cold butter and toss to coat, then press each cube flat with your fingers, leaving large flakes of butter visible (do not fully rub in). Drizzle in the ice water and gently bring together into a shaggy dough — it should look streaky with butter, not smooth.
2. Turn the dough onto a floured surface and pat into a rectangle. Roll it out to about 1/2 inch thick, then fold in thirds like a letter. Rotate 90 degrees and repeat the roll-and-fold two more times. Wrap and chill at least 1 hour (this builds the flaky layers).
3. Make the filling: Melt 1 tbsp butter in a skillet over medium heat. Add the diced apples, brown sugar, 2 tbsp granulated sugar, cinnamon, nutmeg, and lemon juice. Cook 6 to 8 minutes until the apples soften and release their juices. Stir the cornstarch into 1 tbsp water, add to the pan, and cook 1 minute until thickened and glossy. Spread on a plate and cool completely.
4. Preheat the oven to 400 F (200 C) and line a baking sheet with parchment. On a floured surface, roll the chilled dough into a 14-inch square and cut into eight 4 to 5 inch squares.
5. Place a heaping spoonful of cooled filling on the center of each square, leaving a clean border. Brush two edges of each square with egg wash, then fold over into a triangle. Press the edges firmly and crimp with a fork to seal tightly.
6. Transfer turnovers to the baking sheet, spacing them apart. Brush the tops with egg wash, cut 2 or 3 small steam vents in each, and sprinkle generously with granulated sugar.
7. Bake 22 to 25 minutes, until deeply golden brown and puffed. If any filling leaks, that's normal — it caramelizes.
8. Cool on the sheet 5 minutes, then transfer to a rack. Let cool at least 15 minutes before eating so the filling sets and you don't burn your mouth.

</details>

<sub>📷 Public Domain · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-apple-turnovers/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/12/Bienenstich_2014.jpg/480px-Bienenstich_2014.jpg" alt="Photo of Bienenstich (German Bee Sting Cake)">

#### 🐝 [Bienenstich (German Bee Sting Cake)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-bee-sting-cake/)
German · snack · hard  
**$0.72/serving** · 300 kcal · P5 · C40 · F13 · ⏱ 240 min · 🍽 12  

The authentic German Bee Sting Cake: a tender enriched yeast dough baked under a crackly honey-almond caramel, split and filled with a vanilla pastry cream lightened with whipped cream. The crunchy caramelized almond top against the silky custard is what earns it the name.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/2 cups all-purpose flour, for the yeast dough
- **Instant yeast** — 2 1/4 tsp instant yeast (1 standard packet)
- **Sugar** — 1/4 cup granulated sugar, for the dough
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Whole milk** — 1/2 cup whole milk, lukewarm
- **Butter** — 6 tbsp unsalted butter, softened (for the dough)
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs (1 for the dough, 1 yolk for the pastry cream)
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 1/2 tsp vanilla (1/2 tsp dough, 2 tsp pastry cream)
- **Butter** — 6 tbsp unsalted butter, for the honey-almond topping
- **Honey** — 3 tbsp honey, for the topping
- **Sugar** — 1/4 cup granulated sugar, for the topping caramel
- **Heavy cream** — 1 1/4 cups heavy cream (2 tbsp in topping, rest whipped into filling)
- **Slivered almonds** — 1 cup sliced/slivered almonds, for the topping
- **Milk** — 1 1/2 cups whole milk, for the pastry cream
- **Sugar** — 1/3 cup granulated sugar, for the pastry cream
- **Cornstarch** — 3 tbsp cornstarch, for the pastry cream

**Steps**

1. Make the dough: In a bowl whisk 2 1/2 cups flour, the yeast, 1/4 cup sugar, and salt. Add the lukewarm milk, 1 whole egg, 1/2 tsp vanilla, and 6 tbsp softened butter. Mix, then knead 8-10 minutes until smooth and elastic (it will be soft and slightly sticky). Cover and let rise in a warm spot 60-90 minutes until doubled.
2. Press the dough into a parchment-lined and buttered 9-inch springform or square pan, pushing it evenly to the edges. Cover loosely and let it rise again while you make the topping, about 20-30 minutes.
3. Make the honey-almond topping: In a saucepan melt 6 tbsp butter with the honey and 1/4 cup sugar. Bring to a simmer and cook 2-3 minutes until bubbling and pale gold, then stir in 2 tbsp of the heavy cream and the sliced almonds. Let cool 5 minutes so it thickens slightly. Preheat the oven to 350F (175C).
4. Gently spoon the warm almond mixture over the risen dough and spread to the edges. Bake 25-30 minutes until the top is deep golden and caramelized and the dough is cooked through. Cool completely in the pan, then release.
5. Make the pastry cream: Heat 1 1/2 cups milk until steaming. In a bowl whisk 1 egg yolk, 1/3 cup sugar, and the cornstarch until smooth. Slowly whisk in the hot milk, return everything to the pan, and cook over medium heat, whisking constantly, until thick and bubbling, 2-3 minutes. Off heat whisk in 2 tsp vanilla. Press plastic wrap onto the surface and chill until cold, at least 2 hours.
6. Whip the remaining cream (about 1 cup) to stiff peaks. Whisk the cold pastry cream smooth, then fold in the whipped cream in two additions to keep it light. This is the diplomat-style filling.
7. Assemble: With a serrated knife, slice the cooled cake horizontally into two layers. To make slicing easy, pre-cut the caramelized top layer into 12 wedges or squares before placing it back on (the caramel shatters if cut later).
8. Spread all the filling over the bottom layer in a thick, even cushion. Set the pre-cut almond top gently over the cream. Chill at least 1 hour to set, then serve cold.

</details>

<sub>📷 Xocolatl, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-bee-sting-cake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0f/Streuselkuchen.JPG/480px-Streuselkuchen.JPG" alt="Photo of Classic German Streuselkuchen">

#### 🍰 [Classic German Streuselkuchen](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-streuselkuchen/)
German · snack · medium  
**$0.55/serving** · 393 kcal · P6 · C54 · F17 · ⏱ 130 min · 🍽 12  

An authentic German crumb cake: a soft, lightly sweet yeast dough baked under a thick blanket of buttery cinnamon streusel that bakes up into golden, craggy crumbs. This is the real Blechkuchen (sheet cake) version sold in every German bakery — humble, comforting, and best with afternoon coffee. The trick is keeping the streusel butter cold so the crumbs stay clumpy rather than melting flat.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/2 cups all-purpose flour, for the yeast dough
- **Milk** — 1/2 cup whole milk, lukewarm (about 100F)
- **Active dry yeast** — 1 packet (2 1/4 tsp) active dry yeast
- **Sugar** — 1/4 cup granulated sugar, for the dough
- **Butter** — 5 tbsp unsalted butter, melted and cooled, for the dough
- **Eggs** — 1 large egg, room temperature
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Lemon zest** — 1 tsp finely grated lemon zest (optional, classic touch) _(optional)_
- **All-purpose flour** — 2 cups all-purpose flour, for the streusel topping
- **Sugar** — 3/4 cup granulated sugar, for the streusel
- **Butter** — 3/4 cup (1 1/2 sticks) cold unsalted butter, cubed, for the streusel
- **Cinnamon** — 1 1/2 tsp ground cinnamon, for the streusel
- **Powdered sugar** — 2 tbsp powdered sugar, for dusting (optional) _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Make the dough: Stir the yeast and a pinch of the dough sugar into the lukewarm milk and let it sit 5-10 minutes until foamy. In a large bowl, combine the 2 1/2 cups flour, remaining dough sugar, and salt. Add the foamy milk, melted-and-cooled butter, egg, vanilla, and lemon zest, then mix and knead 8-10 minutes by hand (or 5 minutes with a dough hook) until you have a smooth, soft, slightly tacky dough.
2. Cover the bowl with a damp towel and let the dough rise in a warm spot for about 1 to 1 1/2 hours, until doubled in size.
3. Make the streusel while the dough rises: In a separate bowl, combine the 2 cups flour, 3/4 cup sugar, and cinnamon. Add the cold cubed butter and rub it in with your fingertips until you have a mix of pea-sized to hazelnut-sized clumps. Do not overwork it into a paste — you want distinct crumbs. Chill the streusel in the fridge until needed.
4. Punch down the risen dough and press it evenly into a greased 9x13-inch baking pan (or a quarter sheet pan), pushing it into the corners. Cover and let it rest 15-20 minutes while the oven heats.
5. Preheat the oven to 350F (180C). Scatter the chilled streusel evenly over the entire surface of the dough in a thick, generous layer — the topping should be as deep as the cake itself.
6. Bake on the center rack for 30-35 minutes, until the streusel is golden and a skewer inserted into the dough comes out clean. The edges should be lightly browned and the crumbs set but not hard.
7. Let the cake cool in the pan for at least 20 minutes. Dust with powdered sugar if using, then cut into 12 squares and serve warm or at room temperature.

</details>

<sub>📷 Fiver, der Hellseher, CC BY-SA 3.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-streuselkuchen/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a0/Marmorkuchen.jpg/480px-Marmorkuchen.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Marble Cake (Marmorkuchen)">

#### 🍰 [Classic Marble Cake (Marmorkuchen)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-marble-cake/)
German · snack · medium  
**$0.65/serving** · 391 kcal · P6 · C52 · F18 · ⏱ 85 min · 🍽 12  

A tender, buttery vanilla pound-style cake swirled with rich cocoa batter for the signature marbled effect. This is the real German-style Marmorkuchen: one batter, split and tinted with cocoa, then layered and dragged with a knife so every slice shows ribbons of light and dark. Moist, fine-crumbed, and not too sweet, it keeps beautifully for days and needs no frosting.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks) unsalted butter, softened
- **Sugar** — 1 1/2 cups granulated sugar
- **Eggs** — 4 large eggs, room temperature
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **All-purpose flour** — 3 cups all-purpose flour
- **Baking powder** — 1 tbsp (3 tsp) baking powder
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Milk** — 1 cup whole milk, room temperature
- **Cocoa powder** — 3 tbsp unsweetened cocoa powder
- **Milk** — 3 tbsp whole milk (extra, to loosen the cocoa batter)

**Steps**

1. Preheat the oven to 350F (175C). Generously butter and flour a 10- to 12-cup Bundt pan (or a large tube pan), tapping out the excess so the marbled cake releases cleanly.
2. Whisk together the 3 cups flour, baking powder, and salt in a bowl and set aside. In a separate large bowl, beat the softened butter and sugar with an electric mixer on medium-high for 4 to 5 minutes until pale, light, and fluffy.
3. Beat in the eggs one at a time, scraping the bowl between additions, then beat in the vanilla. The batter should look smooth and creamy.
4. With the mixer on low, add the flour mixture in three additions alternating with the 1 cup milk in two additions, beginning and ending with flour. Mix just until no dry streaks remain; do not overbeat.
5. Scoop out about one-third of the batter (roughly 2 heaping cups) into a separate bowl. Sift the 3 tbsp cocoa over it and add the extra 3 tbsp milk, then fold until the cocoa batter is evenly dark and smooth.
6. Spoon half the plain vanilla batter into the prepared pan. Dollop the cocoa batter over it, then top with the remaining vanilla batter, spreading gently to roughly even.
7. Drag a butter knife or thin skewer through the batter in a slow figure-eight or zigzag, going twice around the pan, to swirl the two batters into a marble pattern. Don't overmix or you'll lose the contrast.
8. Bake 50 to 60 minutes, until the cake is golden, springs back when pressed, and a skewer inserted into the center comes out clean. Cool in the pan 15 minutes, then turn out onto a rack to cool completely before slicing. Dust with powdered sugar if you like.

</details>

<sub>📷 Public Domain · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-marble-cake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/Pfeffern%C3%BCsse_cocoa.jpg/480px-Pfeffern%C3%BCsse_cocoa.jpg" alt="Photo of Pfeffernüsse (German Spice Cookies)">

#### 🍪 [Pfeffernüsse (German Spice Cookies)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-pfeffern-sse/)
German · snack · medium  
**$0.25/serving** · 75 kcal · P1 · C14 · F2 · ⏱ 320 min · 🍽 36  

Classic German Christmas cookies — small, domed, and chewy-firm, built on a honey-and-molasses dough warmed with cinnamon, cloves, cardamom, nutmeg, and the signature pinch of black pepper that gives them their name ("pepper nuts"). The dough rests overnight so the spices bloom, then each cookie is dipped in a thin powdered-sugar glaze that dries to a crackly white shell. Faithful to the traditional Lebkuchen-family recipe a German baker would make for the holidays.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Honey** — 1/2 cup honey
- **Molasses** — 1/4 cup molasses
- **Brown sugar** — 1/2 cup packed dark brown sugar
- **Butter** — 1/4 cup (4 tbsp) unsalted butter
- **Eggs** — 1 large egg
- **All-purpose flour** — 2 3/4 cups all-purpose flour
- **Baking soda** — 1 tsp baking soda
- **Cinnamon** — 2 tsp ground cinnamon
- **Ground cloves** — 3/4 tsp ground cloves
- **Cardamom** — 1/2 tsp ground cardamom
- **Nutmeg** — 1/2 tsp ground nutmeg
- **Allspice** — 1/2 tsp ground allspice
- **Ground ginger** — 1/2 tsp ground ginger
- **Black pepper** — 1/2 tsp freshly ground black pepper
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt
- **Powdered sugar** — 1 1/2 cups powdered sugar (for the glaze)
- **Milk** — 2 tbsp milk (for the glaze)

**Steps**

1. Make the syrup base: In a small saucepan over medium-low heat, combine the honey, molasses, brown sugar, and butter. Stir until the butter melts and the sugar dissolves into a smooth syrup, about 3-4 minutes. Do not boil. Remove from heat and let cool until just warm to the touch, about 10 minutes.
2. Whisk the dry ingredients: In a large bowl, whisk together the flour, baking soda, cinnamon, cloves, cardamom, nutmeg, allspice, ginger, black pepper, and salt until evenly combined.
3. Combine: Whisk the egg into the cooled (not hot) syrup so it doesn't scramble. Pour the wet mixture into the dry ingredients and stir, then knead briefly by hand, until a stiff, slightly sticky dough forms. It will feel firm like a gingerbread dough.
4. Rest the dough: Wrap the dough tightly in plastic and refrigerate at least 4 hours, ideally overnight. This rest lets the spices bloom and firms the dough for rolling — it is traditional and worth it.
5. Shape: Preheat the oven to 350F (175C) and line baking sheets with parchment. Pinch off pieces and roll into smooth balls about 1 inch (the size of a large marble). Space them about 1.5 inches apart — they spread little and dome as they bake.
6. Bake: Bake one sheet at a time for 12-14 minutes, until the cookies are puffed, set, and lightly cracked on top. They should still feel slightly soft; they firm up as they cool. Let them cool on the sheet for 5 minutes, then transfer to a rack to cool completely.
7. Make the glaze: Whisk the powdered sugar with the milk until smooth and pourable — it should coat the back of a spoon and slowly drip off. Add milk a few drops at a time if too thick.
8. Glaze and dry: Dip the top of each fully cooled cookie into the glaze (or dunk whole and lift out with a fork), letting the excess drip off. Set on a rack over parchment and let the glaze dry to a firm, opaque white shell, about 1 hour. Store in an airtight tin — they improve after a day or two as they soften and the flavors meld.

</details>

<sub>📷 Lucky's andere mutti, CC BY 3.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-pfeffern-sse/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/da/Auntie_Anne%27s_Classic_Soft_Frozen_Pretzels_-_Sarah_Stierch.jpg/480px-Auntie_Anne%27s_Classic_Soft_Frozen_Pretzels_-_Sarah_Stierch.jpg" alt="Photo of Soft Pretzels">

#### 🥨 [Soft Pretzels](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-soft-pretzels/)
German · snack · medium  
**$0.36/serving** · 321 kcal · P8 · C55 · F7 · ⏱ 90 min · 🍽 8  

Chewy, golden Bavarian-style soft pretzels with a deeply browned crust and tender interior. A baking-soda bath gives them that signature mahogany color and pretzel-shop flavor, finished with a sheen of egg wash and a shower of coarse salt.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose or bread flour (cups)
- **Instant yeast** — 1 packet / 2.25 tsp
- **Water** — warm, ~110F (cups for dough)
- **Brown sugar** — 1 tbsp to feed the yeast
- **Butter** — 4 tbsp melted, in dough
- **Salt** — 2 tsp fine salt in dough
- **Baking soda** — 2/3 cup for the boiling bath
- **Eggs** — beaten with 1 tbsp water for wash
- **Flaky sea salt (Maldon-style)** — coarse pretzel salt for topping

**Steps**

1. Stir the warm water, brown sugar, and yeast together and let sit 5 minutes until foamy. Add the melted butter, fine salt, and flour, then mix and knead 8-10 minutes (by hand or stand mixer) until you have a smooth, slightly tacky, elastic dough.
2. Cover and let rise in a greased bowl 45-60 minutes until doubled. Meanwhile heat the oven to 450F and line two sheet pans with parchment.
3. Punch down and divide the dough into 8 equal pieces. Roll each into a 22-24 inch rope, thinner at the ends and a bit thicker in the middle.
4. Shape each rope into a U, cross the ends twice to make a twist, then fold the ends down onto the bottom curve and press to seal into the classic pretzel shape.
5. Bring 9 cups water to a gentle boil in a wide pot and carefully whisk in the baking soda (it will fizz). Boil each pretzel 30 seconds, lift out with a slotted spoon, and drain.
6. Set the boiled pretzels back on the parchment, brush all over with the beaten egg wash, and sprinkle generously with coarse salt.
7. Bake 12-15 minutes until deeply golden brown and glossy, rotating the pans halfway through.
8. Cool a few minutes on a rack and serve warm, ideally with mustard or cheese sauce.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-soft-pretzels/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d6/Bougatsa_%28with_vannila_custard%29_-_IL.FOXO_2026-02-13.jpg/480px-Bougatsa_%28with_vannila_custard%29_-_IL.FOXO_2026-02-13.jpg" alt="Photo of Bougatsa (Greek Semolina Custard Phyllo Pie)">

#### 🥮 [Bougatsa (Greek Semolina Custard Phyllo Pie)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-bougatsa/)
Greek · snack · medium  
**$1.15/serving** · 194 kcal · P2 · C17 · F13 · ⏱ 90 min · 🍽 8  

The classic Thessaloniki-style bougatsa: a creamy semolina custard scented with vanilla and lemon zest, wrapped in butter-brushed phyllo and baked until shatteringly golden, then dusted with powdered sugar and cinnamon. Cut into squares and serve warm for breakfast or as a sweet snack.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Phyllo dough** — 10 sheets phyllo pastry, thawed (about 2/3 of a 1 lb box)
- **Whole milk** — 4 cups whole milk
- **Semolina** — 2/3 cup fine semolina
- **Sugar** — 10 tbsp granulated sugar (about 2/3 cup)
- **Eggs** — 3 large eggs
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, melted, for brushing and the custard
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Lemon zest** — 1 tsp finely grated lemon zest
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt
- **Powdered sugar** — 3 tbsp powdered sugar, for dusting
- **Cinnamon** — 1 tsp ground cinnamon, for dusting

**Steps**

1. Make the custard base: In a heavy saucepan, warm the milk with the sugar and salt over medium heat until steaming but not boiling. While whisking constantly, rain in the semolina in a thin stream to avoid lumps, then cook, stirring continuously, for 5-7 minutes until the mixture thickens to a loose pudding that coats the spoon.
2. Off the heat, whisk in 2 tbsp of the melted butter, the vanilla, and the lemon zest. Let cool for about 5 minutes so it won't scramble the eggs. In a bowl, beat the eggs, then temper them by whisking in a ladleful of the warm semolina, then whisk the egg mixture back into the saucepan. The custard should be thick and glossy. Press plastic wrap directly onto the surface and let it cool to just warm.
3. Heat the oven to 350F (180C). Brush a roughly 9x13-inch baking dish with melted butter. Keep the phyllo covered with a damp towel while you work so it doesn't dry out.
4. Lay 6 sheets of phyllo into the dish one at a time, brushing each lightly with melted butter and letting the excess drape over the edges. Let the overhang hang outside the dish for now.
5. Spread the cooled semolina custard evenly over the phyllo base. Fold the draped overhang back over the filling to partially enclose it.
6. Top with the remaining 4 phyllo sheets, brushing each with melted butter, and tuck the edges down into the sides of the dish to seal in the custard. Brush the top generously with the last of the butter.
7. With a sharp knife, score the top phyllo layers into 8 squares (cutting only through the top, not into the custard) to make serving easier and to let steam escape. Bake on the middle rack for 40-45 minutes, until the top is deeply golden and crisp.
8. Let the bougatsa rest 15 minutes so the custard sets. Dust the whole top heavily with powdered sugar and a sifting of cinnamon, cut along the scored lines, and serve warm.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-bougatsa/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/28/Karydopita_%28walnut_pie%29.jpg/480px-Karydopita_%28walnut_pie%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Karidopita (Greek Walnut Syrup Cake)">

#### 🍰 [Karidopita (Greek Walnut Syrup Cake)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-karidopita/)
Greek · snack · medium  
**$1.20/serving** · 404 kcal · P6 · C49 · F22 · ⏱ 90 min · 🍽 16  

A classic Greek walnut cake — a moist, lightly spiced crumb dense with toasted walnuts, breadcrumbs and warm cinnamon and clove, baked in a pan and drenched while warm in a lemon-and-cinnamon syrup. The syrup soaks into every bite, leaving the cake tender, glistening and deeply nutty. A staple of village kitchens and name-day tables across Greece.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Walnuts** — 3 cups walnuts, coarsely chopped (toasted)
- **Italian breadcrumbs** — 1 1/2 cups fine dry breadcrumbs
- **All-purpose flour** — 1/2 cup all-purpose flour
- **Eggs** — 6 large eggs, separated
- **Sugar** — 1 cup granulated sugar (for the batter)
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup unsalted butter, melted
- **Cinnamon** — 4 tsp ground cinnamon (1 1/3 tbsp)
- **Ground cloves** — 1/2 tsp ground cloves
- **Baking powder** — 3 tsp baking powder (1 tbsp)
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Orange** — 1 orange, zested (zest only, for the batter)
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt
- **Sugar** — 2 cups granulated sugar (for the syrup)
- **Water** — 2 cups water (for the syrup)
- **Honey** — 2 tbsp honey (for the syrup)
- **Lemon** — 1 lemon — 1 strip of peel plus 1 tbsp juice (for the syrup)
- **Cinnamon** — 1 cinnamon stick or 1 tsp ground cinnamon (for the syrup)

**Steps**

1. Make the syrup first so it cools completely (it must be cold when it hits the warm cake). Combine the 2 cups sugar, 2 cups water, honey, the strip of lemon peel and the cinnamon stick in a saucepan. Bring to a boil, then simmer 8-10 minutes until very slightly syrupy. Stir in the lemon juice, take off the heat, and let it cool fully — chill it if you have time.
2. Preheat the oven to 350F (175C) and butter a 9x13-inch baking pan. Spread the chopped walnuts on a tray and toast 7-8 minutes until fragrant, then cool. Reserve a small handful for garnish and combine the rest with the breadcrumbs, flour, baking powder, cinnamon, cloves and salt in a bowl.
3. Beat the egg yolks with the 1 cup sugar until pale and thick, 3-4 minutes. Beat in the melted butter, vanilla and orange zest until smooth and emulsified.
4. Fold the dry walnut mixture into the yolk mixture. It will be very stiff and sandy — that's correct; it loosens once the whites go in.
5. In a clean bowl, whip the egg whites to stiff, glossy peaks. Stir a third of the whites into the heavy batter to slacken it, then gently fold in the rest in two additions, keeping as much air as possible.
6. Scrape into the prepared pan and level the top. Bake 35-40 minutes, until deep golden and a skewer comes out clean. The cake should spring back lightly when pressed.
7. As soon as the cake comes out of the oven, score it into 16 squares or diamonds with a sharp knife. Slowly ladle all of the cold syrup evenly over the hot cake — it will sizzle and absorb. Let it sit at least 3-4 hours (ideally overnight) so the syrup soaks all the way through.
8. Scatter the reserved walnuts over the top, cut along the scored lines, and serve at room temperature. It keeps, covered, for up to 5 days and only gets better.

</details>

<sub>📷 Dianeira, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-karidopita/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3f/Koulourakia.jpg/480px-Koulourakia.jpg" alt="Photo of Koulourakia (Greek Easter Butter Cookies)">

#### 🍪 [Koulourakia (Greek Easter Butter Cookies)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-koulourakia/)
Greek · snack · medium  
**$0.22/serving** · 134 kcal · P2 · C18 · F6 · ⏱ 75 min · 🍽 36  

Classic Greek braided butter cookies — tender, lightly sweet, and scented with vanilla and orange, finished with a glossy egg wash and a sprinkle of sesame. Twisted into the traditional coils and ropes, they bake up golden with a delicate crisp edge and a soft crumb that's made for dunking in coffee or tea.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 4 1/2 cups all-purpose flour, plus more for dusting
- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks) unsalted butter, softened
- **Sugar** — 1 cup granulated sugar
- **Eggs** — 3 large eggs (2 for the dough, 1 for the egg wash)
- **Baking powder** — 1 tbsp baking powder
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Orange** — zest of 1 orange, plus 3 tbsp fresh orange juice
- **Milk** — 3 tbsp whole milk, for the dough and to loosen the egg wash
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Sesame seeds** — 2 tsp sesame seeds, for sprinkling _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Bring the butter and eggs to room temperature before starting — this is essential for a dough that creams properly and doesn't crack. Whisk together the flour, baking powder, and salt in a bowl and set aside.
2. In a large bowl, beat the softened butter and sugar with a mixer on medium-high until pale, light, and fluffy, about 4 to 5 minutes. Don't rush this; the air you beat in gives the cookies their tender, slightly crumbly texture.
3. Beat in 2 of the eggs one at a time, fully incorporating each before adding the next. Mix in the vanilla, orange zest, and orange juice. The batter may look slightly curdled from the juice — that's fine, it comes together with the flour.
4. Add the flour mixture in two or three additions on low speed, alternating with the 2 tbsp milk, mixing just until a soft, smooth dough forms. Stop as soon as no dry streaks remain; overmixing makes tough cookies. The dough should be soft but not sticky — knead in a little extra flour by hand only if it clings to your fingers.
5. Cover the dough and let it rest at room temperature for 20 to 30 minutes. Meanwhile, position racks in the upper and lower thirds of the oven and preheat to 350F (175C). Line two baking sheets with parchment.
6. Pinch off about 1 1/2 tablespoons of dough and roll it on a clean surface into a rope about 8 inches long and pencil-thick. Fold the rope in half and twist the two halves around each other into a braid, or coil it into a spiral. Place shaped cookies about 1 inch apart on the lined sheets.
7. Beat the remaining egg with the remaining 1 tbsp milk and brush each cookie generously with the wash for a deep golden shine. Sprinkle with sesame seeds if using.
8. Bake for 18 to 22 minutes, rotating the sheets top-to-bottom halfway through, until the cookies are golden brown and firm. Let them cool on the sheet for 5 minutes, then transfer to a rack. They keep in an airtight tin for up to 2 weeks and only get better with a dunk in coffee.

</details>

<sub>📷 foodistablog, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-koulourakia/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ee/Melomakarona_%28Greek_Xmas_Sweets%29.jpg/480px-Melomakarona_%28Greek_Xmas_Sweets%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Melomakarona (Greek Honey Cookies)">

#### 🍯 [Melomakarona (Greek Honey Cookies)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-melomakarona/)
Greek · snack · medium  
**$0.51/serving** · 246 kcal · P2 · C34 · F12 · ⏱ 90 min · 🍽 30  

Classic Greek Christmas honey cookies: tender, lightly spiced olive-oil and orange dough baked deep gold, then soaked in warm honey-cinnamon syrup and topped with crushed walnuts.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Olive oil** — 1 cup Greek extra-virgin olive oil (dough)
- **Vegetable oil** — 1/2 cup neutral vegetable oil (dough)
- **Sugar** — 1/2 cup granulated sugar (dough)
- **Orange juice** — 3/4 cup fresh orange juice
- **Lemon zest** — zest of 1 orange (about 1 tsp packed)
- **Bottled lemon juice** — 1 tbsp lemon juice
- **All-purpose flour** — 4 1/2 cups all-purpose flour
- **Semolina** — 1/2 cup fine semolina
- **Baking powder** — 2 tsp baking powder
- **Baking soda** — 1/2 tsp baking soda
- **Cinnamon** — 1 1/2 tsp ground cinnamon (dough)
- **Whole cloves** — 1/2 tsp ground cloves (dough)
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt
- **Honey** — 1 cup Greek honey (syrup)
- **Sugar** — 1 cup granulated sugar (syrup)
- **Water** — 1 1/2 cups water (syrup)
- **Cinnamon** — 1 tsp ground cinnamon or 1 stick (syrup)
- **Lemon** — 1 lemon, peel and a squeeze of juice (syrup)
- **Walnuts** — 1 cup walnuts, finely chopped (topping)

**Steps**

1. Make the syrup first so it has time to cool. In a saucepan combine 1 cup honey, 1 cup sugar, 1 1/2 cups water, the cinnamon, a few strips of lemon peel, and a squeeze of lemon juice. Bring to a boil, then simmer gently for 5 minutes, skimming any foam. Remove from heat and cool completely. Hot cookies go into cool syrup (or cool cookies into warm syrup) — never hot into hot, or they fall apart.
2. Make the dough: in a large bowl whisk the olive oil, vegetable oil, and 1/2 cup sugar until blended. Whisk in the orange juice, orange zest, lemon juice, and brandy if using.
3. In a separate bowl whisk the flour, semolina, baking powder, baking soda, 1 1/2 tsp cinnamon, ground cloves, and salt. Add the dry to the wet and fold gently just until a soft, slightly oily dough forms. Do NOT knead — overworking makes them tough. Rest 10 minutes.
4. Preheat oven to 350°F (180°C) and line two sheets with parchment. Pinch off walnut-sized pieces (about 1 1/2 tbsp), shape into smooth ovals, place 1 inch apart, and flatten the tops slightly. Press a fork or fine grater over each for the traditional texture.
5. Bake 22-28 minutes until deeply golden and firm — they should sound hollow when tapped. Underbaked cookies turn gummy in syrup, so let them get real color.
6. While still warm, drop the cookies a few at a time into the fully cooled syrup. Soak 10-15 seconds per side, spooning syrup over them, then lift out with a slotted spoon onto a rack or platter.
7. Immediately sprinkle each soaked cookie with finely chopped walnuts (a pinch of cinnamon mixed in is traditional) so they stick to the wet surface.
8. Rest at least a few hours, ideally overnight, so the syrup distributes and the centers turn soft. Store loosely covered at room temperature; they keep up to 2 weeks and improve with time.

</details>

<sub>📷 Alexander Baxevanis, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-melomakarona/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Portokalopita1.jpg/480px-Portokalopita1.jpg" alt="Photo of Portokalopita (Greek Orange Phyllo Cake)">

#### 🍊 [Portokalopita (Greek Orange Phyllo Cake)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-portokalopita/)
Greek · snack · medium  
**$0.73/serving** · 373 kcal · P4 · C57 · F16 · ⏱ 240 min · 🍽 12  

The real Greek portokalopita: scrunched, dried phyllo torn into a custardy orange-yogurt batter, baked until golden, then drenched while hot in an orange-cinnamon syrup. The phyllo soaks up the syrup and turns into a moist, sponge-like cake with a faint chew — fragrant with fresh orange zest and juice. A beloved Greek café classic, best the day after.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Phyllo dough** — 1 lb (450 g) phyllo pastry, ~16-18 sheets, thawed
- **Greek yogurt** — 1 cup full-fat Greek yogurt
- **Vegetable oil** — 3/4 cup neutral oil (sunflower or vegetable)
- **Sugar** — 1 cup granulated sugar (for the batter)
- **Eggs** — 4 large eggs
- **Orange** — 3 oranges — zest of all 3 plus 1/2 cup fresh juice
- **Baking powder** — 1 tbsp baking powder
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Sugar** — 2 cups granulated sugar (for the syrup)
- **Water** — 2 cups water (for the syrup)
- **Cinnamon** — 1 cinnamon stick (or 1 tsp ground cinnamon), for the syrup _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Open and unfold the phyllo, separate the sheets, and lay them out loosely (draped over chair backs or spread on the counter) for 2-3 hours until dry and brittle. This is the defining step — dry phyllo crumbles into the batter and gives portokalopita its sponge-like crumb instead of a gummy paste. (Short on time: tear it up and bake the pieces at 300F/150C for 8-10 minutes until dry.)
2. Make the syrup first so it can cool completely. Combine the 2 cups sugar, 2 cups water, the cinnamon stick, and a few strips of orange peel in a saucepan. Bring to a boil, then simmer 8-10 minutes until lightly syrupy. Turn off the heat, stir in a splash of the orange juice, and leave to cool fully — a hot cake must always meet cold syrup, never the reverse.
3. Preheat the oven to 350F (175C) and grease a 9x13 inch baking pan. Zest all 3 oranges and squeeze 1/2 cup juice; set aside.
4. Whisk the batter: beat the 4 eggs with 1 cup sugar until pale and frothy, about 2-3 minutes. Whisk in the oil in a steady stream, then the Greek yogurt, orange zest, orange juice, and vanilla until smooth. Sprinkle the baking powder over the top and whisk it in.
5. Tear the dried phyllo into small shreds (roughly 1-2 inch pieces) directly into the batter, fluffing and separating as you go so no clumps stay dry. Fold gently with your hands until every shred is coated and the mixture looks like a loose, ragged custard.
6. Tip the mixture into the prepared pan and spread level. Bake on the middle rack for 40-45 minutes, until deep golden brown on top and set in the center (a skewer should come out with no wet batter).
7. As soon as the cake comes out of the oven, ladle the cold syrup evenly over the hot surface, going slowly and letting each addition soak in before adding more. Use all of it — it will look like too much, but the phyllo drinks it up.
8. Let the portokalopita rest at least 2-3 hours (ideally overnight) so the syrup distributes and the cake firms into clean, moist squares. Cut into 12 pieces and serve at room temperature, optionally with a dusting of cinnamon or a spoon of yogurt.

</details>

<sub>📷 El Mono Español, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-portokalopita/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Slices_of_butter_mochi.jpg/480px-Slices_of_butter_mochi.jpg" alt="Photo of Hawaiian Butter Mochi">

#### 🍮 [Hawaiian Butter Mochi](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-butter-mochi/)
Hawaiian · snack · easy  
**$0.46/serving** · 143 kcal · P1 · C18 · F8 · ⏱ 150 min · 🍽 24  

The real deal: a dense, chewy, golden-edged Hawaiian potluck classic made by whisking glutinous (mochiko) rice flour with coconut milk, evaporated milk, eggs, melted butter, and sugar, then baking it low and slow until it sets into squares that are crisp at the edges and gloriously chewy in the middle. No yeast, no kneading, no fancy equipment — just one bowl, a whisk, and a 9x13 pan.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Glutinous rice flour** — 3 cups mochiko (glutinous/sweet rice flour) — about one 16 oz box
- **Sugar** — 2 cups granulated sugar
- **Baking powder** — 2 tsp baking powder
- **Coconut milk** — 1 can (13.5 oz) full-fat coconut milk
- **Evaporated milk** — 1 can (12 oz) evaporated milk
- **Eggs** — 4 large eggs
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, melted and cooled slightly
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt

**Steps**

1. Preheat the oven to 350F (175C). Grease a 9x13-inch metal baking pan generously with butter or cooking spray, making sure to coat the corners well so the chewy edges release cleanly.
2. In a large bowl, whisk together the dry ingredients: mochiko (glutinous rice flour), sugar, baking powder, and salt. Break up any lumps so the batter stays smooth.
3. In a separate bowl, whisk the eggs until uniform, then whisk in the coconut milk, evaporated milk, vanilla, and the melted (slightly cooled) butter. If the butter is too hot it can scramble the eggs, so let it sit a minute first.
4. Pour the wet mixture into the dry ingredients and whisk until completely smooth with no dry pockets. The batter will be thin and pourable like a pancake batter — that is correct.
5. Pour the batter into the prepared pan. Tap the pan gently on the counter a few times to release large air bubbles and even out the surface.
6. Bake on the center rack for 60 to 70 minutes, until the top is golden brown, the edges have pulled slightly from the sides, and the center is set (a knife inserted in the middle comes out clean, not wet).
7. Let the butter mochi cool completely in the pan on a rack, at least 1 to 2 hours. This is essential — cutting it warm gives gummy, ragged squares, while fully cooled mochi slices into clean, chewy pieces.
8. Run a buttered knife or plastic knife around the edges, then cut into 24 squares. Wipe the blade between cuts for clean edges. Store covered at room temperature.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-butter-mochi/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ad/Hong_Kong_Sweet_Dynasty_egg_tarts.jpg/480px-Hong_Kong_Sweet_Dynasty_egg_tarts.jpg" alt="Photo of Hong Kong Egg Tarts">

#### 🥧 [Hong Kong Egg Tarts](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-hong-kong-egg-tarts/)
Hong Kong · snack · medium  
**$0.35/serving** · 59 kcal · P4 · C4 · F3 · ⏱ 75 min · 🍽 10  

Bakery-style HK dan tat: a tender, buttery shortcrust shell cradling a silky, wobbly egg custard with a glassy golden top. Built on the classic evaporated-milk-and-egg ratio so the custard sets smooth and tender, never rubbery.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose, ~250g for the tart shells
- **Butter** — softened, ~115g for the pastry
- **Powdered sugar** — ~55g, for a tender shortcrust
- **Eggs** — 1 for the pastry, ~4 for the custard
- **Evaporated milk** — ~180ml, the defining HK custard dairy
- **White sugar** — ~110g dissolved into hot water for the custard syrup
- **Vanilla extract** — tiny splash, traditional tarts often skip it _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Make the pastry: beat softened butter with powdered sugar until pale and creamy, then beat in 1 egg until fully combined. Mix in the flour just until a soft dough forms, then wrap and chill 30 minutes.
2. Make the custard syrup: dissolve the white sugar in about 180ml just-boiled water, stir until clear, then let it cool to lukewarm so it won't scramble the eggs.
3. Whisk the remaining 4 eggs lightly with the evaporated milk and vanilla, then pour in the cooled sugar syrup and whisk gently — avoid creating foam. Strain the custard twice through a fine sieve for a glassy finish.
4. Heat oven to 200C / 400F. Divide the chilled dough into 10 pieces and press each thinly and evenly up the sides of a tart tin or muffin cups, keeping the rim level with the top.
5. Skim off any surface bubbles, then pour the strained custard into each shell until about 90 percent full.
6. Bake at 200C / 400F for 10 minutes, then lower to 180C / 350F and bake another 10-15 minutes, until the pastry is golden and the custard is just set with a slight wobble in the center — a toothpick should stand upright.
7. If the custard puffs and domes, prop the oven door open slightly to release heat; this keeps the surface flat and silky.
8. Cool in the tins for 5 minutes, then carefully unmold. Serve warm for the classic just-baked dan tat experience.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: chinnian — CC BY-SA 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-hong-kong-egg-tarts/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/46/Pineapple_buns.jpg/480px-Pineapple_buns.jpg" alt="Photo of Hong Kong Pineapple Buns (Bo Lo Bao)">

#### 🍞 [Hong Kong Pineapple Buns (Bo Lo Bao)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-pineapple-bun/)
Hong Kong · snack · medium  
**$0.50/serving** · 306 kcal · P7 · C43 · F11 · ⏱ 180 min · 🍽 8  

The bakery classic from Hong Kong cha chaan tengs: a pillowy, lightly sweet milk-bread bun crowned with a crackly, golden cookie-paste topping that splits into the signature pineapple-rind pattern as it bakes. There's no pineapple inside — the name is all about that crunchy-sweet crust over a soft, fluffy interior. A tangzhong (cooked flour-milk paste) keeps the crumb tender for days.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2.5 cups bread flour, for the bun dough (plus tangzhong)
- **Whole milk** — 3/4 cup whole milk, warm — split between tangzhong and dough
- **Instant yeast** — 2 1/4 tsp instant yeast (one packet)
- **Sugar** — 1/4 cup granulated sugar, for the dough
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp salt
- **Eggs** — 2 eggs — 1 in the dough, 1 beaten for the egg wash
- **Butter** — 3 tbsp unsalted butter, softened, for the dough
- **Powdered sugar** — 1/2 cup powdered sugar, for the topping
- **Butter** — 1/4 cup unsalted butter, softened, for the topping
- **All-purpose flour** — 1/2 cup all-purpose flour, for the topping
- **Custard powder** — 1 tbsp custard powder for color and flavor — substitute is fine _(optional)_
- **Vanilla extract** — 1/2 tsp vanilla extract, for the topping
- **Baking powder** — 1/4 tsp baking powder, for the topping crackle

**Steps**

1. Make the tangzhong: In a small saucepan, whisk 2 tbsp of the bread flour with 1/4 cup of the milk until smooth. Cook over medium-low heat, stirring constantly, until it thickens into a soft paste that leaves lines when you drag the whisk (about 65C / 1-2 minutes). Scrape into a bowl and let it cool to lukewarm.
2. Make the dough: In a large bowl, combine the remaining bread flour, the sugar, salt, and instant yeast. Add 1 egg, the remaining 1/2 cup warm milk, and the cooled tangzhong. Mix to a shaggy dough, then knead 10-12 minutes by hand (or 8 minutes in a stand mixer) until smooth. Work in the 3 tbsp softened butter a little at a time, then keep kneading until the dough is glossy and passes the windowpane test — it should stretch thin without tearing.
3. First rise: Shape into a ball, place in a lightly oiled bowl, cover, and let rise in a warm spot until doubled, about 1 to 1.5 hours.
4. Make the pineapple topping while the dough rises: Cream the softened 1/4 cup butter with the powdered sugar until pale. Beat in 1 tbsp of the beaten egg (reserve the rest for wash), the vanilla, and custard powder if using. Stir in the 1/2 cup all-purpose flour and 1/4 tsp baking powder to form a soft, slightly sticky cookie dough. Wrap and chill until firm enough to handle, about 20 minutes.
5. Shape the buns: Punch down the dough and divide into 8 equal pieces. Roll each into a tight, smooth ball and place on a parchment-lined baking sheet, spaced apart. Cover and let proof 40-45 minutes until puffy.
6. Apply the topping: Divide the chilled topping into 8 pieces. Roll each between parchment (or press by hand) into a thin round and drape over a proofed bun, covering the top and upper sides. Brush the topping lightly with the reserved beaten egg, then score a shallow crosshatch with a knife to encourage the classic pineapple crackle.
7. Bake: Bake in a preheated oven at 350F (175C) for 15-18 minutes, until the topping is golden and cracked and the buns sound hollow when tapped. Rotate the pan halfway through for even color.
8. Cool and serve: Let cool on a rack at least 10 minutes. Serve warm — and for the true cha chaan teng experience (bo lo yau), split a warm bun and tuck in a thick cold slab of butter.

</details>

<sub>📷 Party Lin, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-pineapple-bun/)</sub>
