<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/46/Pineapple_buns.jpg/480px-Pineapple_buns.jpg" alt="Photo of Pineapple Buns (Bo Lo Bao)">

#### 🍍 [Pineapple Buns (Bo Lo Bao)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-pineapple-buns/)
Hong Kong · breakfast · hard  
**$0.17/serving** · 59 kcal · P3 · C7 · F2 · ⏱ 200 min · 🍽 8  

The Hong Kong bakery classic — pillowy tangzhong milk bread crowned with a sweet, crackly cookie crust that crazes into a golden pineapple-skin pattern as it bakes. No actual pineapple inside; just buttery, fragrant rolls best eaten warm, optionally split around a cold slab of butter.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — bread flour preferred for the dough, plus extra for the cookie topping (~430g total)
- **Instant yeast** — 2 tsp instant yeast
- **Whole milk** — warm, for dough + 3 Tbsp reserved for the tangzhong roux
- **Eggs** — 1 in dough, 1 split between cookie topping and egg wash
- **Sugar** — split: ~50g in dough, ~70g in cookie topping
- **Butter** — softened; ~40g in dough, ~60g in cookie topping
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp
- **Baking powder** — 1/2 tsp, for the cookie topping
- **Milk** — stand-in for milk powder — 2 Tbsp dry milk powder in the topping adds authentic aroma if you have it _(optional)_
- **Vanilla extract** — 1/2 tsp, in the cookie topping _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Make the tangzhong: whisk 3 Tbsp milk, 3 Tbsp water, and 2 Tbsp flour in a small pan over low heat, stirring constantly until it thickens into a smooth paste (about 65C/lines form on the bottom). Scrape into a bowl and let cool to lukewarm.
2. Make the dough: combine 2 cups bread flour, the yeast, 1/4 cup sugar, and salt. Add the cooled tangzhong, 1/2 cup warm milk, 1 egg, and mix into a shaggy dough, then knead 8-10 minutes until smooth and elastic. Knead in 3 Tbsp softened butter until fully absorbed and the dough is silky.
3. Cover and let rise in a warm spot until doubled, about 60-90 minutes.
4. Meanwhile make the cookie topping: cream 4 Tbsp softened butter with 1/3 cup sugar until light, beat in half a beaten egg, the milk powder (if using) and vanilla, then fold in 3/4 cup flour and the baking powder to form a soft dough. Chill until firm enough to handle.
5. Punch down the risen dough, divide into 8 pieces, roll each into a tight smooth ball, and space them on a lined baking sheet. Cover and proof 40-45 minutes until puffy.
6. Divide the cookie topping into 8, flatten each between plastic wrap into a thin disc, and drape one over each proofed bun so it covers the top and upper sides; brush the topping with the remaining beaten egg.
7. Score a crosshatch into the topping with a knife or bench scraper so it cracks into the signature pineapple pattern as it bakes.
8. Bake at 375F (190C) for 15-18 minutes until the crust is deep golden and crackled and the buns sound hollow when tapped.
9. Cool 5-10 minutes; serve warm, and for the classic bo lo yau, split a hot bun and tuck in a cold pat of butter.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Party Lin — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-pineapple-buns/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Two_Egg_Tarts_CC_02_HK.jpg/480px-Two_Egg_Tarts_CC_02_HK.jpg" alt="Photo of Hong Kong Egg Tarts (港式蛋撻)">

#### 🥧 [Hong Kong Egg Tarts (港式蛋撻)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-hong-kong-egg-tart/)
Hong Kong / Cantonese · snack · medium  
**$0.57/serving** · 197 kcal · P5 · C23 · F10 · ⏱ 85 min · 🍽 12  

Classic cha chaan teng egg tarts: a tender, slightly sweet shortcrust shell holding a silky, just-set egg custard with a glossy, wobble-when-warm center. The custard is built from whole eggs and a touch of evaporated milk, baked low enough to stay smooth and free of bubbles. Faithful to the Hong Kong style — flaky-pastry versions exist, but the cookie-crust custard tart is the one most home bakers master and serve with pride.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 1 3/4 cups all-purpose flour, for the pastry shell
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, softened
- **Powdered sugar** — 1/4 cup powdered sugar, for the pastry
- **Eggs** — 5 large eggs total — 1 for the pastry dough, 4 for the custard
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt, for the pastry
- **Vanilla extract** — 1/2 tsp vanilla extract, for the pastry
- **Sugar** — 1/2 cup granulated sugar, for the custard syrup
- **Water** — 3/4 cup hot water, to dissolve the custard sugar
- **Evaporated milk** — 1/3 cup evaporated milk (about 3 tbsp from a 12 oz can)

**Steps**

1. Make the pastry: Beat the softened butter with the powdered sugar and salt until smooth and pale, about 1 minute. Beat in 1 egg and the vanilla until fully combined. Add the flour and mix just until a soft dough comes together — do not overwork it or the shells will turn tough. Wrap and chill for 30 minutes.
2. Shape the shells: Divide the dough into 12 equal pieces (about 30 g each). Press each piece evenly into the bottom and up the sides of a 12-cup standard muffin tin or fluted tart molds, keeping the walls thin and even with a clean rim. Chill the lined tin while you make the custard.
3. Make the sugar syrup: Dissolve the granulated sugar in the 3/4 cup hot water, stirring until completely clear. Let it cool to lukewarm — adding hot syrup to the eggs would scramble them.
4. Mix the custard: In a separate bowl, gently whisk the 4 remaining eggs with the evaporated milk — whisk just to combine, avoiding excess foam. Pour in the cooled sugar syrup and stir gently.
5. Strain twice: Pass the custard through a fine-mesh sieve at least twice into a measuring jug. This removes chalazae and air bubbles and is the single most important step for the signature glass-smooth top. Skim off any surface foam.
6. Fill and bake: Preheat the oven to 400 F (200 C). Carefully pour the strained custard into the chilled shells, filling them to just below the rim (about 90%). Bake on a lower rack for 10 minutes, then reduce the heat to 325 F (165 C) and bake another 10–15 minutes.
7. Judge doneness: The tarts are done when the custard is just set with a slight wobble in the center and a toothpick inserted at an angle stands upright on its own. Do not let the custard puff or dome — that means it is overbaking. If the tops brown too fast, crack the oven door for the last few minutes.
8. Cool and serve: Let the tarts rest in the tin for 5 minutes, then gently lift them out. Serve warm, when the custard is at its silkiest and the shell is at its most tender.

</details>

<sub>📷 CCheungimm, CC BY-SA 3.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-hong-kong-egg-tart/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Bejgli1.jpg/480px-Bejgli1.jpg" alt="Photo of Hungarian Walnut Beigli (Diós Bejgli)">

#### 🥖 [Hungarian Walnut Beigli (Diós Bejgli)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-beigli/)
Hungarian · snack · hard  
**$1.16/serving** · 411 kcal · P9 · C35 · F27 · ⏱ 170 min · 🍽 16  

The classic Hungarian Christmas roll: a tender, lightly sweetened yeast dough wrapped snugly around a dense, fragrant ground-walnut filling, baked into a marbled, crackle-topped log. Brushing the rolls first with egg yolk and then, once dried, with egg white is the traditional trick that gives beigli its signature glossy, marbled crust. Sliced thin, it shows off a tight spiral of dark walnut against pale dough.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 4 cups all-purpose flour, for the dough
- **Butter** — 3/4 cup (1.5 sticks) cold unsalted butter, cubed
- **Sugar** — 1/4 cup granulated sugar, for the dough
- **Active dry yeast** — 1 packet (2 1/4 tsp) active dry yeast
- **Milk** — 1/2 cup lukewarm whole milk, to bloom the yeast
- **Eggs** — 3 large eggs — 2 yolks for the dough, plus 1 whole egg separated for the glaze
- **Sour cream** — 2 tbsp sour cream, for the dough
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Lemon zest** — 1 tsp grated lemon zest, for the dough
- **Walnuts** — 3 1/2 cups (about 14 oz) walnuts, finely ground, for the filling
- **Powdered sugar** — 3/4 cup powdered sugar, for the filling
- **Honey** — 2 tbsp honey, for the filling
- **Raisins** — 1/2 cup golden raisins, for the filling
- **Milk** — 1/2 cup whole milk, to scald and moisten the filling
- **Cinnamon** — 1/2 tsp ground cinnamon, for the filling _(optional)_
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract, for the filling
- **Lemon zest** — 1 tsp grated lemon zest, for the filling

**Steps**

1. Make the dough: Stir the yeast into the 1/2 cup lukewarm milk with a pinch of the sugar and let it foam for 8-10 minutes. Meanwhile, rub the cold cubed butter into the flour, remaining sugar, and salt with your fingertips until it looks like coarse crumbs. Add the bloomed yeast, 2 egg yolks, the sour cream, and lemon zest, then bring together into a smooth dough — knead just 2-3 minutes until it comes together (this is a short, rich dough, not a bread dough; do not over-knead). Divide into 2 pieces, wrap, and chill 1 hour (or overnight).
2. Make the walnut filling: Scald the 1/2 cup milk and pour it over the ground walnuts, powdered sugar, honey, raisins, vanilla, cinnamon, and lemon zest. Stir into a thick, spreadable paste and let cool completely. It should be moist but hold its shape — if it's stiff, add a splash more warm milk; if loose, a spoonful more ground walnuts.
3. Roll out: On a lightly floured surface, roll each dough half into a rectangle roughly 10 by 14 inches and about 1/8 inch thick. Keep the dough cool and the thickness even — thin dough is what gives beigli its high filling-to-dough ratio.
4. Fill and roll: Spread half the walnut filling over each rectangle, leaving a 1/2-inch border. Roll up snugly from the long side into a tight log, pinching the seam and tucking the ends. Place seam-side down on a parchment-lined baking sheet, leaving space between the two logs.
5. First glaze and rest: Lightly beat the reserved egg yolk and brush it all over both logs. Let them sit uncovered at cool room temperature for 30-40 minutes until the yolk glaze dries to a tacky film — this drying step is the key to the marbled, cracked top.
6. Second glaze and prick: Lightly beat the reserved egg white and brush it over the dried yolk layer. Let dry another 10 minutes, then prick each log every inch or so with a toothpick or skewer, going down to the filling. This vents steam so the rolls don't burst at the seam.
7. Bake: Bake in a preheated 350°F (180°C) oven for 30-35 minutes, until deep golden and the surface has developed its characteristic marbled cracks. If the tops brown too fast, tent loosely with foil for the last 10 minutes.
8. Cool and slice: Let the logs cool completely on a rack before slicing — beigli is always cut cold, in thin 1/2-inch slices, to show the tight walnut spiral. It keeps, wrapped, for over a week and the flavor deepens by day two.

</details>

<sub>📷 Hu Totya, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-beigli/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4d/Kremes.jpg/480px-Kremes.jpg" alt="Photo of Krémes (Hungarian Vanilla Cream Slice)">

#### 🍰 [Krémes (Hungarian Vanilla Cream Slice)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-kr-mes/)
Hungarian · snack · hard  
**$1.09/serving** · 431 kcal · P8 · C47 · F23 · ⏱ 360 min · 🍽 9  

The classic Hungarian café pastry: two sheets of crisp, golden baked puff pastry sandwiching a tall, trembling layer of vanilla custard cream. The custard is a proper crème pâtissière, cooked thick on the stove then folded with whipped cream and set with a little gelatin so it stands tall yet quivers when you cut it. Finished simply with a snowfall of powdered sugar — no frills, just butter, vanilla, eggs, and milk done right.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 cups all-purpose flour (for the rough-puff pastry)
- **Butter** — 1 cup + 2 tbsp cold unsalted butter (16 tbsp for laminating, 2 tbsp for the custard)
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp salt (for the pastry dough)
- **Water** — 1/2 cup ice-cold water (to bring the dough together)
- **Whole milk** — 3 cups whole milk (for the custard)
- **Eggs** — 6 large eggs, separated (yolks for custard, whites whipped in)
- **Sugar** — 1 cup granulated sugar (for the custard)
- **Cornstarch** — 6 tbsp cornstarch (to thicken the pastry cream)
- **All-purpose flour** — 1/4 cup all-purpose flour (with the cornstarch, for a sturdy custard)
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp pure vanilla extract (or seeds of 1 vanilla bean)
- **Gelatin (unflavored)** — 1 packet (about 2 1/2 tsp) unflavored gelatin, bloomed
- **Heavy cream** — 1 cup cold heavy cream, whipped (folded into the custard)
- **Powdered sugar** — 1/4 cup powdered sugar, for dusting the top

**Steps**

1. Make the rough-puff pastry: Whisk the flour and salt. Cut 16 tbsp cold butter into the flour, leaving some pieces walnut-sized, then add the ice water and bring it together into a shaggy dough. Pat into a rectangle, then roll out, fold in thirds (like a letter), and rotate 90 degrees. Repeat this roll-and-fold 4 to 5 times, chilling 20 minutes whenever the butter softens. Wrap and rest in the fridge at least 1 hour.
2. Bake the two pastry sheets: Preheat the oven to 400 F (200 C). Divide the dough in half and roll each into a 9x9-inch square, about 1/8 inch thick. Transfer each to a parchment-lined sheet, prick all over with a fork, and lay a second sheet of parchment plus a light second tray on top to keep one sheet flatter for the base. Bake 20 to 25 minutes until deep golden and crisp. Cool completely. Trim both to match a 8x8 or 9x9 pan; reserve the prettier sheet for the top.
3. Start the custard: Bloom the gelatin in 3 tbsp cold water and set aside. Warm the milk with the vanilla in a saucepan until steaming. In a bowl, whisk the 6 egg yolks with the granulated sugar, cornstarch, and 1/4 cup flour until smooth and pale.
4. Temper and cook: Slowly pour about a third of the hot milk into the yolk mixture while whisking, then return everything to the saucepan. Cook over medium heat, whisking constantly, until it thickens to a stiff pudding and bubbles slowly, 3 to 5 minutes. Off heat, whisk in the bloomed gelatin and the remaining 2 tbsp butter until glossy. Press plastic wrap onto the surface and cool to just-warm.
5. Whip and fold: Beat the 6 egg whites to stiff, glossy peaks. Separately whip the cold heavy cream to soft peaks. Gently fold the whipped cream into the warm custard, then fold in the egg whites in two additions, keeping it airy — this is what gives Krémes its tall, trembling body.
6. Assemble: Set the base pastry sheet in a lined square pan. Pour the custard over it and spread level — it should be a thick, generous layer at least 1 1/2 inches tall. Set the reserved top sheet gently on the custard, pressing very lightly so it adheres without crushing the cream.
7. Chill and finish: Refrigerate at least 4 hours, ideally overnight, until the custard is fully set and sliceable. Dust the top sheet generously with powdered sugar. Using a serrated knife dipped in hot water and wiped between cuts, slice into 9 squares and serve cold.

</details>

<sub>📷 Illustratedjc, CC BY-SA 3.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-kr-mes/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/K%C3%BCrt%C5%91skal%C3%A1cs%2C_2.jpg/480px-K%C3%BCrt%C5%91skal%C3%A1cs%2C_2.jpg" alt="Photo of Kürtőskalács (Hungarian Chimney Cake)">

#### 🥐 [Kürtőskalács (Hungarian Chimney Cake)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-k-rt-skal-cs/)
Hungarian · snack · hard  
**$0.83/serving** · 544 kcal · P10 · C91 · F15 · ⏱ 135 min · 🍽 6  

The classic Transylvanian-Hungarian "chimney cake": an enriched, lightly sweet yeast dough wound in a spiral, rolled in granulated sugar, and baked until the sugar caramelizes into a glassy, crackly amber shell over a soft, pull-apart crumb. Finished warm with a roll in cinnamon sugar and toasted walnuts the moment it comes out of the oven. Authentically baked on a wooden or foil-wrapped roller — here adapted faithfully for a standard home oven.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 3 1/2 cups all-purpose flour (plus a little for dusting)
- **Whole milk** — 1 cup whole milk, warmed to about 110F
- **Active dry yeast** — 1 packet (2 1/4 tsp) active dry yeast
- **Sugar** — 1/4 cup granulated sugar (for the dough)
- **Butter** — 6 tbsp unsalted butter, melted (plus extra melted butter for brushing the rolls)
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs (1 whole egg + 1 yolk in the dough; reserve the spare white only if you like)
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp salt
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Lemon zest** — 1 tsp finely grated lemon zest _(optional)_
- **White sugar** — 3/4 cup granulated sugar for rolling/coating the dough (this caramelizes into the shell)
- **Cinnamon** — 2 tsp ground cinnamon (for the finishing cinnamon sugar)
- **Walnuts** — 1/2 cup walnuts, finely chopped and lightly toasted (classic topping) _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Bloom the yeast: stir the active dry yeast and a pinch of the dough sugar into the warm milk (about 110F) and let stand 8-10 minutes until foamy. If it doesn't foam, the milk was too hot or the yeast is dead - start over.
2. Make the dough: in a large bowl combine the flour, remaining 1/4 cup sugar, and salt. Add the foamy milk, the whole egg plus the extra yolk, 6 tbsp melted butter, vanilla, and lemon zest. Mix to a shaggy dough, then knead 8-10 minutes (by hand or stand mixer with the hook) until smooth, supple, and elastic - it should pull away from the bowl but stay soft. Cover and let rise in a warm spot 1 to 1.5 hours, until doubled.
3. Build the rollers: traditional kürtőskalács bakes on wooden cylinders. To mimic this in a home oven, tightly wrap 6 cans (or cardboard tubes) in two layers of heavy foil to make smooth cylinders about 2-2.5 inches across, brush them well with melted butter, and set them on a foil-lined sheet. Preheat the oven to 375F.
4. Shape: punch down the dough and divide into 6 pieces. On a lightly floured surface roll each piece into a long rope about 1/2 inch thick and 30-35 inches long. Wind each rope in a spiral around a buttered cylinder, overlapping each turn slightly so the coils just touch. Press the ends gently to seal so they don't unravel.
5. Coat: brush each wound roll all over with melted butter, then roll it firmly in the 3/4 cup granulated sugar, pressing so a generous, even layer of sugar sticks to every surface - this sugar is what melts and caramelizes into the signature crackly shell.
6. Bake: stand or lay the sugared rolls on the sheet (rotate them halfway if lying down) and bake at 375F for 20-25 minutes, until deep golden brown and the sugar has melted into a glossy caramel crust. Rotate the pan once for even color; the cakes should sound hollow and the caramel should look glassy, not pale.
7. Finish: while baking, mix the cinnamon with about 1/4 cup of any leftover/extra granulated sugar and the toasted chopped walnuts. The instant the cakes come out, brush lightly with melted butter and roll them in the cinnamon-sugar-walnut mix so it sticks to the hot caramel.
8. Unmold and serve: let cool just 3-5 minutes, then carefully twist and slide each spiral off its cylinder (the caramel shell releases as it sets). Serve warm and fresh - kürtőskalács is at its best within an hour or two, pulled apart in soft, sweet, caramelized coils.

</details>

<sub>📷 Christo, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-k-rt-skal-cs/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/9/9b/Nankhatai_biscuits.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Nankhatai (Indian Cardamom Shortbread Cookies)">

#### 🍪 [Classic Nankhatai (Indian Cardamom Shortbread Cookies)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-nankhatai/)
Indian · snack · easy  
**$0.40/serving** · 133 kcal · P1 · C9 · F10 · ⏱ 75 min · 🍽 16  

Melt-in-your-mouth eggless Indian shortbread cookies made with ghee, all-purpose flour and a little semolina for the signature grainy-crumbly bite. Perfumed with green cardamom and nutmeg, crowned with pistachios, and baked until pale gold with the classic cracked tops. The real Diwali-tin tea-time treat.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 1 1/2 cups all-purpose flour (maida)
- **Semolina** — 1/4 cup fine semolina (sooji), for the signature grainy crumb
- **Ghee** — 3/4 cup ghee, soft and at room temperature (not melted)
- **Powdered sugar** — 3/4 cup powdered sugar
- **Green cardamom** — 1 tsp freshly ground green cardamom
- **Nutmeg** — 1/4 tsp freshly grated nutmeg
- **Baking soda** — 1/4 tsp baking soda
- **Baking powder** — 1/4 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — pinch of salt (about 1/8 tsp)
- **Pistachios** — 1 oz pistachios, slivered, for topping

**Steps**

1. Whisk together the all-purpose flour, semolina, baking soda, baking powder and salt in a bowl, then set aside for 15-20 minutes so the semolina can begin to soften slightly. In a separate large bowl, sift the powdered sugar over the soft (not melted) ghee.
2. Beat the ghee and powdered sugar together with a spoon or hand mixer for 4-5 minutes until pale, light and creamy. This aeration is what gives Nankhatai its tender, melt-in-the-mouth texture, so don't rush it. Beat in the ground cardamom and grated nutmeg.
3. Add the dry ingredients to the creamed ghee in two additions, mixing gently with your hand until a soft, smooth, pliable dough forms. Do not knead or overwork it; stop as soon as it comes together. If it feels too crumbly to shape, work in 1-2 teaspoons more soft ghee.
4. Cover the dough and rest it 15 minutes at room temperature. Meanwhile, position a rack in the center of the oven and preheat to 325F (165C). Line two baking sheets with parchment.
5. Divide the dough into 16 equal pieces (about 1 to 1 1/4 inches each). Roll each into a smooth ball between your palms, then place on the trays spaced 2 inches apart and flatten very gently into thick discs. Press a few slivered pistachios into the top of each, then press a light cross or single line into the surface with a knife to encourage the classic cracked top.
6. Bake for 16-20 minutes, until the cookies are set, the bottoms are lightly golden, and the tops have cracked but are still very pale on top. Nankhatai should stay pale, not browned, so pull them the moment the bases color.
7. Let the cookies rest on the hot tray for 10 minutes; they will be extremely fragile while warm and firm up as they cool. Transfer carefully to a rack to cool completely. Store airtight at room temperature for up to 2 weeks.

</details>

<sub>📷 Yuyutzu, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-nankhatai/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/58/Karachi_fruit_biscuits_2013-12-03_12-20.jpg/480px-Karachi_fruit_biscuits_2013-12-03_12-20.jpg" alt="Photo of Karachi Biscuits (Hyderabadi Tutti-Frutti Cookies)">

#### 🍪 [Karachi Biscuits (Hyderabadi Tutti-Frutti Cookies)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-karachi-biscuits/)
Indian (Hyderabadi) · snack · easy  
**$0.18/serving** · 92 kcal · P1 · C8 · F6 · ⏱ 70 min · 🍽 24  

The iconic eggless cookies from Hyderabad's Karachi Bakery — a buttery, almost melt-in-the-mouth shortbread perfumed with cardamom, faintly tinted gold with custard powder, and freckled with chewy tutti-frutti. Crisp at the edge, sandy and tender at the heart, with that unmistakable bakery-counter aroma. Naturally eggless, the way the originals are made.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 cups all-purpose flour (maida)
- **Cornstarch** — 1/4 cup custard powder (use cornstarch + a few drops vanilla and a pinch of turmeric as the closest stand-in)
- **Butter** — 3/4 cup (1.5 sticks) unsalted butter, softened
- **Powdered sugar** — 3/4 cup powdered (icing) sugar
- **Milk** — 3 tbsp whole milk, to bind
- **Cardamom** — 3/4 tsp green cardamom powder (seeds of about 6-7 pods, ground)
- **Vanilla extract** — 1/2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Baking powder** — 1/2 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt
- **Turmeric** — 1/8 tsp turmeric, for the classic pale-gold tint (or a drop of yellow food color) _(optional)_
- **Dried cranberries** — 1/2 cup tutti-frutti (candied papaya); dried cranberries are the closest catalog swap
- **Cashews** — 1/4 cup cashews, chopped _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. In a bowl, whisk together the flour, custard powder (cornstarch stand-in), baking powder, salt, cardamom powder, and turmeric. Set this dry mix aside.
2. In a separate large bowl, beat the softened butter and powdered sugar together until pale, light, and fluffy — about 3-4 minutes by hand or 2 minutes with a mixer. This aeration is what gives the biscuits their tender melt.
3. Beat in the vanilla. Add the dry mix in two batches, folding gently. Add the milk a little at a time and bring everything together into a soft, non-sticky dough — do not overwork it or the biscuits turn hard.
4. Fold in the tutti-frutti (or dried cranberries) and chopped cashews, distributing them evenly through the dough.
5. Shape the dough into a log about 2 inches thick (or press into a small parchment-lined loaf tin and chill), wrap in parchment, and refrigerate for at least 30 minutes until firm. Chilling is essential for clean slices and prevents spreading.
6. Preheat the oven to 340°F (170°C) and line two baking sheets with parchment. Slice the chilled log into rounds about 1/3 inch (8 mm) thick and arrange them an inch apart on the trays.
7. Bake for 15-18 minutes, until the bottoms are set and the edges are just barely golden — the tops should stay pale, not browned. They will feel soft when hot.
8. Let the biscuits rest on the tray for 5 minutes (they are fragile while warm), then transfer to a rack to cool completely. They crisp up and develop their signature sandy crumb as they cool.

</details>

<sub>📷 Randhirreddy, CC BY-SA 3.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-karachi-biscuits/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/23/Plum_cake._Goa.jpg/480px-Plum_cake._Goa.jpg" alt="Photo of Kerala Plum Cake (Christmas Fruit Cake)">

#### 🍰 [Kerala Plum Cake (Christmas Fruit Cake)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-kerala-plum-cake/)
Indian (Kerala) · snack · medium  
**$1.11/serving** · 298 kcal · P4 · C35 · F14 · ⏱ 95 min · 🍽 12  

The classic Syrian-Christian Christmas cake from Kerala, built on rum-soaked dried fruits and a deep burnt-sugar caramel that gives the crumb its signature dark color and bittersweet edge. Loaded with cashews and warm spices, it's dense, moist, and boozy in the best way. Soak the fruit ahead for the most authentic flavor.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Raisins** — 1/4 cup raisins (for soaking)
- **Dried cranberries** — 1/4 cup dried cranberries (stands in for tutti-frutti/candied peel)
- **Dried apricots** — 1/4 cup dried apricots, finely chopped
- **Rum** — 1/2 cup dark rum, for soaking the fruit
- **Sugar** — 1/2 cup white sugar, for the burnt-sugar caramel
- **Water** — 1/4 cup hot water, to deglaze the caramel
- **All-purpose flour** — 1 1/2 cups all-purpose flour
- **Butter** — 3/4 cup (1 1/2 sticks) unsalted butter, softened
- **Brown sugar** — 1/2 cup packed brown sugar
- **Eggs** — 3 large eggs, at room temperature
- **Cashews** — 1/4 cup cashews, roughly chopped
- **Baking powder** — 1 1/2 tsp baking powder
- **Cinnamon** — 1 tsp ground cinnamon
- **Ground cloves** — 1/2 tsp ground cloves
- **Nutmeg** — 1/2 tsp ground nutmeg
- **Ground ginger** — 1/2 tsp ground ginger
- **Cardamom** — 1/2 tsp ground cardamom
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Orange** — zest of 1 orange
- **Lemon** — zest of 1 lemon (plus 1 tbsp juice)

**Steps**

1. Soak the fruit: Combine the raisins, dried cranberries, and chopped dried apricots in a jar with the dark rum. Seal and let sit at least overnight (ideally 2-3 days, or up to weeks) at room temperature, shaking occasionally. The plumper and boozier, the better.
2. Make the burnt-sugar caramel: Add the white sugar to a small heavy saucepan over medium heat. Let it melt and cook, swirling (do not stir), until it turns a deep mahogany brown and just starts to smoke — this bitterness is the soul of the cake. Off the heat, carefully pour in the hot water (it will sputter violently), swirl to dissolve into a smooth dark syrup, and set aside to cool completely.
3. Prep: Preheat the oven to 325F (160C). Grease and line an 8-inch round or 9x5 loaf pan with parchment. Drain the soaked fruit, reserving any excess rum. Toss the fruit and chopped cashews with 1 tablespoon of the flour to keep them from sinking.
4. Whisk the remaining flour with the baking powder, cinnamon, cloves, nutmeg, ginger, cardamom, and salt in a bowl. Set aside.
5. Cream the softened butter and brown sugar with an electric mixer until pale and fluffy, 3-4 minutes. Beat in the eggs one at a time, then mix in the vanilla, orange zest, lemon zest and juice, and all of the cooled burnt-sugar caramel. The batter will be a rich brown.
6. Fold the dry ingredients into the wet just until combined — do not overmix. Gently fold in the floured fruit and cashews along with any reserved rum (1-2 tablespoons) for extra moisture and aroma.
7. Scrape the batter into the prepared pan and level the top. Bake for 55-70 minutes, until the cake is deeply set and a skewer inserted in the center comes out clean (a few moist crumbs are fine). Tent loosely with foil if the top darkens too fast.
8. Cool in the pan for 15 minutes, then turn out onto a rack. For the most authentic flavor, brush the warm cake with a little extra rum, wrap tightly, and let it mature at least a day before slicing. It keeps well for weeks.

</details>

<sub>📷 Joegoauk Goa, CC BY-SA 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-kerala-plum-cake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/Home_made_Mawa_Cake.jpg/480px-Home_made_Mawa_Cake.jpg" alt="Photo of Parsi Mawa Cake">

#### 🍰 [Parsi Mawa Cake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-mawa-cake/)
Indian (Parsi) · snack · medium  
**$1.10/serving** · 276 kcal · P4 · C30 · F15 · ⏱ 90 min · 🍽 10  

A classic Parsi tea-time cake built around mawa (reduced milk solids), recreated here with rich ricotta, evaporated and condensed milk for that signature dense, milky, melt-in-the-mouth crumb. Perfumed with green cardamom and a whisper of nutmeg, scattered with almonds, and baked low and slow until deep gold. This is the Irani-bakery cake Mumbai runs on — sturdy, fragrant, and not too sweet.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 1 1/2 cups all-purpose flour
- **Semolina** — 1/4 cup fine semolina (sooji), for a tender-dense crumb
- **Ricotta cheese** — 1/2 cup whole-milk ricotta, standing in for grainy mawa/khoya (this is two 1/2-cup units)
- **Evaporated milk** — 1/2 cup evaporated milk, for the reduced-milk richness of mawa
- **Sweetened condensed milk** — 1/4 cup sweetened condensed milk, deepens the mawa flavor
- **Butter** — 3/4 cup (1 1/2 sticks) unsalted butter, softened
- **Sugar** — 3/4 cup granulated sugar
- **Eggs** — 3 large eggs, at room temperature
- **Baking powder** — 1 1/2 tsp baking powder
- **Cardamom** — 1 1/2 tsp freshly ground green cardamom (the signature flavor)
- **Nutmeg** — 1/4 tsp freshly grated nutmeg
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Slivered almonds** — 1/4 cup slivered almonds, for scattering on top
- **Milk** — 1/4 cup whole milk, to loosen the batter as needed

**Steps**

1. Position a rack in the center of the oven and preheat to 325F (165C). Grease an 8-inch round or 8.5x4.5-inch loaf pan, line the bottom with parchment, and grease again. The low temperature is essential — mawa cake is baked slow so the milk solids set into a moist, even crumb without doming or cracking.
2. Whisk together the flour, semolina, baking powder, ground cardamom, nutmeg, and salt in a bowl. Let the semolina sit in this mix while you build the batter; it begins to hydrate and gives the cake its characteristic close, slightly grainy texture.
3. In a large bowl, beat the softened butter with the sugar until pale and fluffy, 4 to 5 minutes. This long creaming builds the structure that carries the heavy milk solids. Add the eggs one at a time, beating well after each so the batter stays emulsified and smooth.
4. Beat in the ricotta, evaporated milk, condensed milk, and vanilla. The batter may look slightly broken — that is fine; it comes together once the flour goes in. This trio of dairy is the stand-in for traditional mawa and gives the cake its deep, milky flavor.
5. Add the dry ingredients in two additions, folding gently with a spatula just until no streaks remain. Do not overmix. If the batter is stiffer than a thick ribbon, fold in the milk a tablespoon at a time until it slowly drops off the spatula.
6. Scrape the batter into the prepared pan and smooth the top. Scatter the slivered almonds evenly over the surface. Tap the pan once on the counter to release large air pockets.
7. Bake at 325F for 55 to 70 minutes (loaf pans run longer than rounds), until deep golden, the top springs back, and a skewer inserted in the center comes out with just a few moist crumbs. If the top browns too fast past the 40-minute mark, tent loosely with foil.
8. Cool in the pan on a rack for 15 minutes, then turn out, peel off the parchment, and cool completely right-side up. Mawa cake firms and deepens in flavor as it sits — it is best after a few hours and even better the next day, sliced thick alongside hot chai.

</details>

<sub>📷 Sanika Dhumal, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-mawa-cake/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/Irish_soda_bread_%2825292114472%29.jpg/480px-Irish_soda_bread_%2825292114472%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Irish Soda Bread">

#### 🍞 [Irish Soda Bread](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-irish-soda-bread/)
Irish · snack · easy  
**$0.34/serving** · 293 kcal · P7 · C53 · F5 · ⏱ 55 min · 🍽 8  

A rustic, no-yeast quick bread leavened by buttermilk reacting with baking soda. The crust is crackly and the crumb is tender and slightly tangy, studded with sweet raisins and aromatic caraway. Mix it fast, cut the cross, and bake — it goes from bowl to oven in minutes.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose flour, about 4 cups / 500g
- **Baking soda** — 1 tsp
- **Salt** — 1 tsp
- **Sugar** — 2 tbsp, optional for a slightly sweeter loaf _(optional)_
- **Butter** — 3 tbsp cold, cut into cubes
- **Buttermilk** — about 1.75 cups / 420ml
- **Eggs** — 1 large, lightly beaten
- **Raisins** — about 1 cup
- **Caraway seeds** — 1 tbsp _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Preheat the oven to 425F (220C) and line a baking sheet with parchment, or dust a cast-iron skillet with flour.
2. In a large bowl, whisk together the flour, baking soda, salt, and sugar so the leavening is evenly distributed.
3. Add the cold cubed butter and rub it into the flour with your fingertips until the mixture looks like coarse crumbs, then stir in the raisins and caraway seeds.
4. In a separate bowl, whisk the buttermilk with the egg, then pour into the dry ingredients and stir with a wooden spoon just until a shaggy, sticky dough forms — do not overmix or knead, as that toughens the crumb.
5. Turn the dough onto a lightly floured surface and gently shape it into a round about 8 inches wide with as few movements as possible; the surface will be rough, which is correct.
6. Set the round on the baking sheet and, with a sharp knife, cut a deep cross about three-quarters into the top — this lets heat penetrate and helps it bake through.
7. Bake for 15 minutes at 425F, then lower the heat to 400F (200C) and bake another 25 to 30 minutes, until deep golden and hollow-sounding when tapped on the bottom.
8. Cool on a wire rack for at least 30 minutes before slicing; soda bread is best eaten the day it is baked, ideally with butter.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Jim Lukach — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-irish-soda-bread/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/I_cantuccini.jpg/480px-I_cantuccini.jpg" alt="Photo of Almond Biscotti">

#### 🍪 [Almond Biscotti](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-almond-biscotti/)
Italian · snack · medium  
**$0.27/serving** · 170 kcal · P4 · C34 · F2 · ⏱ 75 min · 🍽 12  

Classic twice-baked Italian almond cookies — crisp, dry, and made for dunking in coffee or vin santo. A simple egg-and-flour dough studded with toasted whole almonds, scented with vanilla and almond extract, then baked as a log and sliced.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose flour, ~2 cups
- **Sugar** — ~1 cup granulated
- **Almonds** — 1 cup whole, toasted
- **Eggs** — 3 egg
- **Baking powder** — 1.5 tsp
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp
- **Almond extract** — 3/4 tsp — defines the flavor
- **Lemon zest** — zest of 1 lemon, brightens the dough _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat oven to 350F (175C) and line a baking sheet with parchment. Spread the whole almonds on a separate tray and toast 8-10 minutes until fragrant, then let cool and roughly chop about half, leaving the rest whole.
2. In a bowl, whisk together flour, sugar, baking powder, and salt. Stir in the toasted almonds so they are evenly distributed.
3. In a separate bowl, beat the eggs with the vanilla, almond extract, and lemon zest. Reserve about 1 tablespoon of beaten egg for an egg wash.
4. Pour the egg mixture into the dry ingredients and mix with a spatula, then your hands, until a stiff, slightly sticky dough forms. Do not add extra flour unless it is unworkable.
5. Turn dough onto the parchment and shape into 2 logs about 3 inches wide and 3/4 inch tall, spaced apart. Brush the tops with the reserved egg wash for shine.
6. Bake 25-30 minutes until firm, pale gold, and dry on top. Remove and lower the oven to 300F (150C). Let the logs cool 10-15 minutes — slicing hot causes crumbling.
7. Transfer logs to a cutting board and, using a serrated knife, slice on a slight diagonal into 1/2-inch thick pieces with a firm sawing motion.
8. Lay the slices cut-side down on the sheet and bake 10 minutes, then flip each one and bake 8-10 more minutes until crisp and dry. Cool completely on a rack — they harden as they cool. Store airtight up to 2 weeks.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Matteo Bertini — CC BY-SA 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-almond-biscotti/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/Amaretti_gobeirne.JPG/480px-Amaretti_gobeirne.JPG" alt="Photo of Classic Italian Amaretti">

#### 🍪 [Classic Italian Amaretti](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-amaretti/)
Italian · snack · easy  
**$0.16/serving** · 34 kcal · P0 · C9 · F0 · ⏱ 60 min · 🍽 24  

Traditional Italian almond cookies — chewy and tender inside with a crackly, powdered-sugar-dusted crust. Made the authentic way with just almond flour, sugar, egg whites, and almond extract, these naturally gluten-free morsels deliver intense marzipan-like flavor. They keep beautifully and are wonderful alongside espresso or a glass of Vin Santo.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Almond flour** — 2 cups (200g) blanched almond flour
- **Sugar** — 1 cup (200g) granulated sugar
- **Liquid egg whites** — 2 large egg whites (about 1/2 cup), room temperature
- **Almond extract** — 1 tsp almond extract
- **Vanilla extract** — 1/2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp fine salt
- **Lemon zest** — zest of 1/2 lemon (about 1/2 tsp), optional _(optional)_
- **Powdered sugar** — 1/2 cup (60g) powdered sugar, for rolling

**Steps**

1. Position a rack in the center of the oven and preheat to 325°F (160°C). Line two baking sheets with parchment paper.
2. In a medium bowl, whisk together the almond flour, granulated sugar, and salt, breaking up any lumps so the mixture is even.
3. In a separate clean bowl, whisk the egg whites by hand just until frothy and loose — you want them aerated but NOT whipped to peaks. Whisk in the almond extract, vanilla, and lemon zest if using.
4. Pour the egg white mixture into the dry ingredients and stir with a spatula until a stiff, sticky, marzipan-like dough comes together. If it feels too dry to hold together, add a teaspoon of egg white at a time; if too wet to roll, rest it 10 minutes to let the almond flour absorb moisture.
5. Place the powdered sugar in a shallow bowl. Scoop level tablespoons of dough and roll each into a smooth 1-inch ball with your palms, then roll generously in powdered sugar to coat completely. The thick sugar coating is what creates the signature crackled top.
6. Arrange the coated balls about 1.5 inches apart on the prepared sheets. For the classic cracked look, pinch each ball gently at the top or leave round — do not flatten. Let them sit uncovered at room temperature for 15 to 20 minutes so a light skin forms.
7. Bake one sheet at a time for 18 to 22 minutes, until the tops are crackled and pale golden but the centers are still soft — they firm up as they cool. Avoid over-baking or they lose their chewy interior.
8. Cool on the baking sheet for 5 minutes, then transfer to a rack to cool completely. Store airtight at room temperature for up to 1 week; the texture actually improves after a day.

</details>

<sub>📷 Greg O'Beirne, CC BY-SA 3.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-amaretti/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/EWEN-Ladyfingers_%281%29.jpg/480px-EWEN-Ladyfingers_%281%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Italian Ladyfingers (Savoiardi)">

#### 🍰 [Classic Italian Ladyfingers (Savoiardi)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-ladyfingers/)
Italian · snack · medium  
**$0.12/serving** · 48 kcal · P2 · C8 · F1 · ⏱ 45 min · 🍽 24  

Light, crisp-edged, tender-centered sponge biscuits piped into elegant fingers and dusted with sugar for a delicate crackle. Made the authentic way: yolks and whites whipped separately, then folded with twice-sifted flour for maximum lift and the classic pale-gold, slightly domed look. Perfect on their own with coffee or as the backbone of tiramisu and charlottes.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Eggs** — 4 large eggs, separated, at room temperature
- **Sugar** — 1/2 cup (100g) granulated sugar, divided
- **All-purpose flour** — 1 cup (130g) all-purpose flour, sifted twice
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Cream of tartar** — 1/4 tsp cream of tartar (helps stabilize the whites)
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp fine salt
- **Powdered sugar** — 1/4 cup powdered sugar, for dusting before baking

**Steps**

1. Position a rack in the center of the oven and preheat to 350F (175C). Line two baking sheets with parchment. If you want uniform fingers, draw 3.5-inch (9 cm) guide lines spaced an inch apart on the underside of the parchment, then flip it ink-side down.
2. Separate the eggs while cold, then let them come to room temperature. In a clean, grease-free bowl, beat the egg whites with the cream of tartar and salt on medium speed until foamy. Increase to medium-high and gradually rain in half the granulated sugar (4 tbsp). Whip to firm, glossy peaks that hold their shape but are not dry or clumpy.
3. In a separate bowl, beat the egg yolks with the remaining granulated sugar (4 tbsp) and the vanilla on high speed for 3 to 4 minutes, until the mixture is pale, thick, and falls in a slowly dissolving ribbon when you lift the beaters.
4. Scoop about a third of the whipped whites into the yolks and stir to lighten. Then gently fold in the remaining whites in two additions with a large spatula, cutting down through the center and sweeping up the sides, until just combined and still airy.
5. Sift the twice-sifted flour over the batter in two or three additions, folding delicately each time. Stop folding the moment no dry streaks remain. The batter should be thick enough to pipe and hold a soft ribbon. Do not overmix or it will deflate and bake flat.
6. Transfer the batter to a piping bag fitted with a 1/2-inch (12 mm) plain round tip. Pipe 3.5-inch fingers onto the parchment, spacing them an inch apart. Sift a generous, even layer of powdered sugar over the tops, wait 2 minutes, then sift a second light layer, this double dusting creates the signature pearled, crackly crust.
7. Bake one sheet at a time for 12 to 15 minutes, rotating halfway, until the fingers are pale golden, set, and spring back lightly when touched. The bottoms should be firm and lightly colored.
8. Slide the parchment onto a wire rack and let the ladyfingers cool completely before peeling them off. They firm up as they cool. Store airtight at room temperature for up to a week, or use immediately in tiramisu.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-ladyfingers/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/7/73/Crostata_di_mele_di_Santin_spolverata_con_zucchero_a_velo.jpg" alt="Photo of Crostata di Marmellata (Italian Jam Tart)">

#### 🥧 [Crostata di Marmellata (Italian Jam Tart)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-crostata/)
Italian · snack · medium  
**$0.87/serving** · 300 kcal · P4 · C37 · F15 · ⏱ 95 min · 🍽 8  

The classic Italian jam tart every nonna knows by heart: a buttery, lightly sweet pasta frolla shortcrust filled with thick apricot jam and finished with a hand-laid lattice top. Tender and crumbly rather than flaky, it keeps for days and is just as good with morning coffee as it is after dinner.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 cups all-purpose flour (about 250g 00 or AP flour)
- **Sugar** — 1/2 cup granulated sugar (100g)
- **Butter** — 10 tbsp cold unsalted butter, cubed (about 140g)
- **Eggs** — 1 whole egg, plus 1 yolk for the dough
- **Lemon zest** — 1 tsp finely grated lemon zest
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt
- **Baking powder** — 1/2 tsp baking powder
- **Apricot jam** — 1 cup good apricot jam (about 320g)

**Steps**

1. Make the pasta frolla: In a bowl, whisk together the flour, sugar, salt, and baking powder. Add the cold cubed butter and rub it into the flour with your fingertips (or pulse in a food processor) until the mixture looks like coarse damp sand with no butter pieces larger than a pea.
2. Add the whole egg, the extra yolk, lemon zest, and vanilla. Mix just until the dough comes together into a ball — work it as little as possible to keep it tender. Flatten into a disc, wrap, and chill at least 30 minutes (up to overnight).
3. Heat the oven to 350F (180C). Cut off about one-third of the dough and keep it cold for the lattice. On a lightly floured surface, roll the larger piece into a circle about 1/8 inch thick and line a 9-inch tart pan, pressing it into the corners and trimming the overhang flush with the rim.
4. Spread the apricot jam evenly over the base, leaving a thin border at the edge. If the jam is very stiff, stir it loose first; if very runny, simmer it 2-3 minutes to thicken so it doesn't boil over.
5. Roll the reserved dough and cut it into strips about 3/4 inch wide. Lay them across the tart in a lattice, pressing the ends onto the rim to seal. Re-roll any scraps if needed.
6. Bake on the middle rack for 30-35 minutes, until the pastry is firm and golden and the jam is bubbling gently. If the lattice browns too fast, tent loosely with foil for the last few minutes.
7. Cool the crostata completely in the pan on a rack — the jam is molten and the pastry is fragile while hot, and it sets as it cools. Slice into wedges and serve at room temperature.

</details>

<sub>📷 fugzu, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-crostata/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/db/Maritozzo_con_panna.jpg/480px-Maritozzo_con_panna.jpg" alt="Photo of Maritozzi (Roman Sweet Cream Buns)">

#### 🍞 [Maritozzi (Roman Sweet Cream Buns)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-maritozzi/)
Italian · breakfast · medium  
**$0.75/serving** · 324 kcal · P7 · C49 · F11 · ⏱ 300 min · 🍽 8  

The classic Roman breakfast pastry: soft, lightly sweet enriched buns scented with orange zest and a touch of honey, baked golden, then split and overstuffed with cold, barely-sweetened whipped cream. A faithful, traditional version with a proper overnight-friendly enriched dough and a glossy honey-water glaze.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 3 cups bread flour (or all-purpose), about 400g
- **Milk** — 1/2 cup whole milk, warmed to lukewarm
- **Instant yeast** — 2 1/4 tsp instant yeast (one packet)
- **Sugar** — 5 tbsp granulated sugar (about 1/3 cup), for the dough
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs, plus 1 yolk reserved for egg wash
- **Butter** — 5 tbsp unsalted butter, softened (about 70g)
- **Honey** — 1 tbsp honey, for the dough
- **Olive oil** — 1 tbsp mild olive oil (traditional Roman touch)
- **Orange** — zest of 1 orange (plus 1 tbsp juice)
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp fine salt
- **Heavy cream** — 1 1/2 cups cold heavy whipping cream, for the filling
- **Powdered sugar** — 1/4 cup powdered sugar (about 4 tbsp), divided for cream and dusting

**Steps**

1. Warm the milk to lukewarm (about 100F/38C) and stir in the yeast and 1 tsp of the sugar. Let sit 5-10 minutes until foamy. In a large bowl or stand mixer, combine the flour, remaining sugar, salt, and orange zest.
2. Add the foamy milk, 2 whole eggs, honey, olive oil, vanilla, and 1 tbsp orange juice. Mix until a shaggy dough forms, then knead 8-10 minutes (or 6-8 in a stand mixer with the dough hook) until smooth and elastic. With the mixer running, add the softened butter a little at a time and knead another 5-8 minutes until the dough is glossy, soft, and pulls cleanly from the bowl.
3. Shape into a ball, place in a lightly oiled bowl, cover, and let rise in a warm spot 1.5-2 hours until doubled. For best flavor, refrigerate the dough overnight after the first rise and continue the next day.
4. Punch down gently and divide into 8 equal pieces (about 100g each). Shape each into a tight ball, then roll into a smooth oval/log about 4 inches long. Place seam-side down on a parchment-lined baking sheet, spacing them well apart. Cover loosely and proof 1 to 1.5 hours until puffy and nearly doubled.
5. Preheat the oven to 375F (190C). Beat the reserved egg yolk with 1 tsp water and brush the buns gently. Bake 16-20 minutes until deep golden and an internal temp of about 190F (88C). Meanwhile, simmer 1 tbsp honey with 1 tbsp water for 30 seconds to make a glaze.
6. Brush the hot buns with the honey glaze for shine, then cool completely on a rack. The buns must be fully cold before filling or the cream will melt.
7. Whip the cold heavy cream with 3 tbsp of the powdered sugar (and a few drops of vanilla if you like) to firm peaks. Keep it cold and do not overwhip.
8. Slice each cooled bun lengthwise at a slight angle, cutting most of the way through but leaving a hinge. Pipe or spoon in a generous mound of whipped cream, then drag a flat spatula or offset knife across the open face to scrape the cream flush and smooth. Dust with the remaining powdered sugar and serve cold.

</details>

<sub>📷 Giacomo Alessandroni, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-maritozzi/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7c/Lemon_olive_oil_cake_-_3300260329.jpg/480px-Lemon_olive_oil_cake_-_3300260329.jpg" alt="Photo of Olive Oil Cake">

#### 🍰 [Olive Oil Cake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-olive-oil-cake/)
Italian · snack · easy  
**$0.97/serving** · 397 kcal · P4 · C44 · F23 · ⏱ 65 min · 🍽 10  

A moist, tender single-layer cake built on fruity extra-virgin olive oil instead of butter, perfumed with lemon and orange zest and a splash of vanilla. The olive oil keeps it soft for days and gives a subtle savory depth, while whipped eggs and sugar lend a light, open crumb. Finished simply with a dusting of powdered sugar — rustic, elegant, and genuinely Italian.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — about 1.75 cups all-purpose flour
- **Sugar** — about 1.25 cups granulated sugar
- **Extra virgin olive oil** — 1 cup good fruity extra-virgin olive oil
- **Eggs** — 3 large eggs, room temperature
- **Whole milk** — 3/4 cup whole milk
- **Lemon zest** — zest of 1 lemon
- **lemon-juice** — 2 tbsp fresh lemon juice
- **Orange** — zest of 1/2 orange
- **Vanilla extract** — 1.5 tsp vanilla extract
- **Baking powder** — 1.5 tsp baking powder
- **Baking soda** — 1/4 tsp baking soda
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp salt
- **Powdered sugar** — for dusting _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 350F (175C). Grease a 9-inch round cake pan with a little olive oil, line the bottom with parchment, and grease again. In a bowl, whisk together the flour, baking powder, baking soda, and salt; set aside.
2. In a large bowl, beat the eggs and sugar with a whisk or mixer for 3 to 4 minutes until pale, thick, and noticeably increased in volume — this aeration gives the cake its light crumb.
3. With the mixer running on low (or whisking steadily), pour in the extra-virgin olive oil in a slow, thin stream so it emulsifies smoothly into the eggs. Whisk in the milk, lemon juice, vanilla, and the lemon and orange zest.
4. Add the dry ingredients in two additions, folding gently with a spatula just until no streaks of flour remain. Do not overmix — a few lumps are fine and overworking makes the cake tough.
5. Pour the batter into the prepared pan and smooth the top. Tap the pan once on the counter to release large air bubbles.
6. Bake on the center rack for 40 to 45 minutes, until the top is deep golden, the cake springs back when lightly pressed, and a toothpick inserted in the center comes out clean or with a few moist crumbs.
7. Cool in the pan on a wire rack for 15 minutes, then run a knife around the edge, invert to release, peel off the parchment, and turn right-side up to cool completely.
8. Dust generously with powdered sugar just before serving. The cake keeps, well wrapped at room temperature, for up to 3 days and only gets more moist.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: stu_spivack — CC BY-SA 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-olive-oil-cake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Vegan_Pignoli_Cookie_%284874691874%29.jpg/480px-Vegan_Pignoli_Cookie_%284874691874%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Pignoli Cookies (Italian Pine Nut Cookies)">

#### 🍪 [Pignoli Cookies (Italian Pine Nut Cookies)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-pignoli-cookies/)
Italian · snack · medium  
**$0.97/serving** · 135 kcal · P3 · C14 · F8 · ⏱ 50 min · 🍽 24  

Chewy, marzipan-rich Italian cookies built on a base of pure almond paste, crusted all over with toasted pine nuts. Crackly on the outside, soft and almond-dense within, with no flour and no butter — just almond, sugar, and egg white. A staple of Sicilian-American bakeries, especially around the holidays.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Almond flour** — 1 1/2 cups almond flour (to build a homemade almond paste base)
- **Sugar** — 1 cup plus 2 tbsp granulated sugar (1 cup for the paste, 2 tbsp reserved)
- **Powdered sugar** — 1/2 cup powdered sugar
- **Liquid egg whites** — 3 large egg whites (about 3/4 cup), divided
- **Almond extract** — 1 tsp almond extract
- **Vanilla extract** — 1/2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt
- **Pine nuts** — 8 oz (about 1 1/2 cups) pine nuts, for coating

**Steps**

1. Preheat the oven to 325 F and line two baking sheets with parchment paper. In a food processor, combine the almond flour, 1 cup of the granulated sugar, and the powdered sugar. Pulse until very finely ground and uniform, about 30 seconds.
2. Add 2 of the egg whites, the almond extract, vanilla, and salt to the processor. Run continuously until the mixture comes together into a thick, sticky, smooth almond paste dough that pulls away from the sides, 1 to 2 minutes. It should be tacky but hold its shape when pinched.
3. Pour the pine nuts into a wide shallow bowl. In a separate small bowl, lightly whisk the remaining egg white with the reserved 2 tbsp sugar to make a loose glaze — this helps the pine nuts adhere.
4. Scoop level tablespoons of dough and roll each into a smooth ball with lightly dampened hands. Working one at a time, dip each ball into the egg white glaze, then roll and press firmly into the pine nuts so the nuts coat the entire surface.
5. Place the coated balls about 2 inches apart on the prepared sheets, gently flattening each into a thick disk. Press any stray pine nuts back into the surface so none are loose.
6. Optional but traditional: let the shaped cookies rest uncovered for 15 minutes so the tops set slightly, which encourages the classic crackled finish.
7. Bake one sheet at a time on the center rack for 16 to 20 minutes, until the pine nuts are golden and the edges are lightly browned but the centers are still soft. Do not overbake — they firm up as they cool.
8. Let the cookies cool completely on the sheet for 10 minutes before transferring to a rack. They will be delicate while warm and set into a chewy texture once cool. Store airtight at room temperature for up to a week.

</details>

<sub>📷 Veganbaking.net from USA, CC BY-SA 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-pignoli-cookies/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e7/Pizza_night-_dough_is_ready_%2833502053445%29.jpg/480px-Pizza_night-_dough_is_ready_%2833502053445%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Pizza Dough">

#### 🍕 [Pizza Dough](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-pizza-dough/)
Italian · dinner · medium  
**$0.22/serving** · 400 kcal · P11 · C83 · F1 · ⏱ 1500 min · 🍽 4  

A classic Neapolitan-style pizza dough made with just flour, water, yeast, and salt. A slow, cold ferment develops deep flavor and an airy, blistered crust with a chewy interior. Makes enough for two 12-inch pizzas.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 00 or bread flour preferred, ~500g / 3.5 cups
- **Water** — lukewarm, ~325ml (65% hydration)
- **Active dry yeast** — about 1/2 tsp for a slow ferment
- **Salt** — fine sea salt, ~12g / 2 tsp
- **Olive oil** — for oiling the bowl _(optional)_
- **Semolina** — for dusting the peel so dough slides _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Dissolve the yeast in the lukewarm water and let it sit 5 minutes until it looks cloudy. In a large bowl, whisk the flour and salt together, then pour in the yeast water.
2. Mix with a wooden spoon or your hand until no dry flour remains and a shaggy, sticky dough forms. Cover and rest 20 minutes to let the flour fully hydrate (autolyse).
3. Knead the dough on a lightly floured surface for 8-10 minutes, or stretch-and-fold in the bowl every 5 minutes for 20 minutes, until smooth, elastic, and slightly tacky. It should pass a windowpane test.
4. Lightly oil a bowl, add the dough, cover tightly, and let rise at room temperature for 1-2 hours until nearly doubled.
5. Divide into two equal balls, shaping each into a tight round by tucking the edges underneath. Place in oiled containers, cover, and cold-ferment in the fridge 24-72 hours for the best flavor.
6. Remove the dough 1-2 hours before baking and let it come to room temperature so it stretches easily.
7. Preheat the oven and a pizza stone or steel to its highest setting (260C/500F or hotter) for at least 45 minutes. Gently stretch each ball into a 12-inch round by hand, leaving a puffy rim and never using a rolling pin.
8. Dust a peel with semolina, top the pizza, and bake 6-10 minutes until the crust is blistered, golden, and charred in spots.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: terri_bateman — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-pizza-dough/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/Ricciarelli_from_Siena-2.jpg/480px-Ricciarelli_from_Siena-2.jpg" alt="Photo of Ricciarelli (Sienese Almond Cookies)">

#### 🍪 [Ricciarelli (Sienese Almond Cookies)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-ricciarelli/)
Italian · snack · medium  
**$0.28/serving** · 44 kcal · P0 · C11 · F0 · ⏱ 110 min · 🍽 20  

Soft, chewy diamond-shaped almond cookies from Siena, with a crackled powdered-sugar crust and a tender, marzipan-like interior. Built on almond flour, sugar, and whipped egg whites, scented with almond extract and citrus zest — no butter, no flour, naturally gluten-free.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Almond flour** — 2 1/2 cups (250g) finely ground almond flour
- **Sugar** — 1 cup (200g) granulated sugar
- **Powdered sugar** — 3/4 cup powdered sugar, divided (some for the dough, some for coating)
- **Liquid egg whites** — 2 large egg whites (about 1/2 cup), at room temperature
- **Almond extract** — 1 tsp almond extract
- **Vanilla extract** — 1/2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Orange** — zest of 1 orange
- **Baking powder** — 1/2 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt
- **Lemon zest** — 1/2 tsp finely grated lemon zest

**Steps**

1. In a large bowl, whisk together the almond flour, granulated sugar, 1/4 cup of the powdered sugar, baking powder, and salt until evenly combined and no lumps remain. Stir in the orange and lemon zest.
2. In a separate clean, grease-free bowl, beat the room-temperature egg whites with a pinch of the salt until they hold soft, billowy peaks — stop short of stiff and dry. Beat in the almond extract and vanilla.
3. Fold the whipped egg whites into the almond-sugar mixture a third at a time, working gently until a thick, sticky, cohesive dough forms. It should be moldable, not runny; if it feels too wet, work in a spoonful more almond flour.
4. Cover the dough and chill in the refrigerator for at least 1 hour (or up to overnight). This firms the dough so it can be shaped and helps the signature cracks form during baking.
5. Dust a work surface generously with the remaining powdered sugar. Roll the dough into a log about 1 inch thick, then cut on the diagonal into roughly 20 pieces. Shape each into the classic flattened diamond/lozenge with slightly tapered ends, about 1/2 inch thick.
6. Roll each cookie thoroughly in powdered sugar until heavily coated, then place on parchment-lined baking sheets, spacing them 1 1/2 inches apart. For the most authentic crackle, let the shaped, sugared cookies rest uncovered at room temperature for 1 to 2 hours (or overnight) so a skin forms on the surface.
7. Preheat the oven to 300F (150C). Bake one sheet at a time for 14 to 18 minutes, until the cookies have spread slightly, cracked across the powdered-sugar crust, and are barely set — they should stay pale, with no real browning, and feel soft in the center.
8. Let the ricciarelli cool completely on the sheet; they firm up as they cool but stay chewy inside. Dust with a little extra powdered sugar before serving. Store airtight for up to a week.

</details>

<sub>📷 Shaw from Shanghai, China; derivative work by Saibo, CC BY-SA 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-ricciarelli/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/d/d3/Sbrisolona_%28cropped%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Sbrisolona (Classic Mantovan Almond Crumble Cake)">

#### 🍪 [Sbrisolona (Classic Mantovan Almond Crumble Cake)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-sbrisolona/)
Italian · snack · easy  
**$0.58/serving** · 250 kcal · P4 · C22 · F17 · ⏱ 70 min · 🍽 12  

The authentic crumbly almond cake from Mantua, Lombardy. Equal parts all-purpose flour, cornmeal, and ground almonds bound with just enough butter, sugar, and egg yolk to form a dry, sandy dough that bakes into a thin, brittle slab. You never slice it — you smash it into irregular shards and eat it with coffee or a glass of sweet wine. The name comes from the dialect word for "crumb."

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 1 cup all-purpose flour (140g)
- **Cornmeal (polenta)** — 1 cup fine/medium cornmeal (about 140g) — the signature crunch
- **Almonds** — 1 cup whole almonds, ground coarse (about 130g); leave a handful chopped for texture
- **Sugar** — about 2/3 cup granulated sugar (130g)
- **Butter** — about 2/3 cup cold unsalted butter, cubed (150g)
- **Eggs** — 1 large egg yolk (white reserved or discarded)
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Lemon zest** — 1 tsp finely grated lemon zest (zest of about half a lemon)
- **Almond extract** — 1/2 tsp almond extract (stands in for traditional bitter almonds)
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp fine salt

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 350F (175C). Line the bottom of a 9-inch round tart or springform pan with parchment and lightly butter the sides. Pulse the almonds in a food processor (or chop by hand) until mostly coarse meal with a few bigger bits remaining — texture is the whole point, so do not grind to a smooth flour.
2. In a large bowl whisk together the flour, cornmeal, ground almonds, sugar, and salt until evenly combined.
3. Scatter the cold cubed butter over the dry mix. Rub it in with your fingertips, working quickly, until the mixture looks like damp sand with pea-sized clumps — no smooth dough should form.
4. Drizzle in the egg yolk, vanilla, almond extract, and lemon zest. Toss and rake with your fingers (do not knead) just until the crumbs clump when squeezed but still fall apart loosely. If it feels too dry to clump at all, add a teaspoon of cold water; you want rubble, not a ball.
5. Tip the loose crumbs into the pan and spread them in an even layer roughly 1/2 to 3/4 inch thick. Do NOT press or pack it down — leave the surface craggy and uneven. The gaps and lumps are what give Sbrisolona its shattery character.
6. Bake for 35 to 45 minutes, until the top is golden-brown and the edges are deeply golden and firm. It should smell toasty and nutty; the cornmeal and almonds darken noticeably when done.
7. Cool in the pan on a rack for at least 30 minutes — it firms up and crisps as it cools, so resist cutting it warm. It will be fragile.
8. Once fully cool, turn it out and break it into rough irregular shards with your hands (traditionally smacked in the center with a fist). Dust lightly with powdered sugar if you like, and serve with espresso, grappa, or a sweet wine like Moscato. Store airtight up to a week; it stays crisp.

</details>

<sub>📷 ho visto nina volare, CC BY-SA 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-sbrisolona/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/3/32/Torta_della_nonna.jpg" alt="Photo of Torta della Nonna">

#### 🥧 [Torta della Nonna](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-torta-della-nonna/)
Italian · snack · medium  
**$1.49/serving** · 392 kcal · P7 · C53 · F17 · ⏱ 160 min · 🍽 8  

The real Tuscan "grandmother's cake": a tender pasta frolla shortcrust shell filled with a thick lemon-and-vanilla pastry cream (crema pasticcera), blanketed with pine nuts, and dusted with powdered sugar. Crisp at the edges, creamy in the center, fragrant with lemon zest. This is the faithful nonna version, no shortcuts.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/4 cups all-purpose flour (for the pasta frolla)
- **Butter** — 10 tbsp (1 1/4 sticks) cold unsalted butter, cubed
- **Sugar** — scant 9 tbsp granulated sugar for the dough (about 1/2 cup + 1 tbsp)
- **Eggs** — 4 large eggs total: 1 whole egg + 1 yolk for the dough, 3 yolks for the cream
- **Lemon zest** — zest of 1 1/2 lemons, split between dough and cream (about 3 tsp)
- **Baking powder** — 1/2 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp fine salt
- **Whole milk** — 2 cups whole milk (for the pastry cream)
- **Sugar** — scant 7 tbsp granulated sugar for the cream (about 1/3 cup + 2 tbsp)
- **Cornstarch** — 4 tbsp cornstarch (to thicken the cream)
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 1/2 tsp vanilla extract (or 1 vanilla bean, seeds scraped)
- **Pine nuts** — 2 oz (about 1/3 cup) pine nuts for the top
- **Powdered sugar** — 2 tbsp powdered sugar, for dusting

**Steps**

1. Make the pasta frolla: In a bowl, whisk together the flour, sugar, baking powder, salt, and the zest of half a lemon. Add the cold cubed butter and rub it into the flour with your fingertips until the mixture looks like coarse crumbs. Make a well, add 1 whole egg plus 1 yolk, and bring the dough together quickly with your hands - do not knead. Flatten into a disk, wrap, and chill at least 1 hour (it can rest overnight).
2. Make the crema pasticcera: Warm the milk with the zest of 1 lemon in a saucepan until it just steams; do not boil. Meanwhile whisk the 3 egg yolks with the cream's sugar until pale, then whisk in the cornstarch until smooth. Pour the hot milk over the yolks in a slow stream, whisking constantly, then return everything to the pan.
3. Cook the cream over medium heat, whisking without stopping, until it thickens to a glossy, spoon-coating pudding and a few slow bubbles break the surface, about 3-4 minutes. Off the heat, stir in the vanilla. Scrape into a bowl, press plastic wrap directly onto the surface, and chill until cold and set, about 1 hour.
4. Heat the oven to 350F (180C). Butter and lightly flour a 9-inch tart pan or springform. Divide the chilled dough into two pieces, roughly two-thirds and one-third. On a floured surface, roll the larger piece into a circle and line the pan, pressing it up the sides to form a shell. Roll the smaller piece into a circle for the lid.
5. Fill and seal: Spread the cold pastry cream evenly into the shell. Lay the dough lid over the top, press the edges of the lid and shell together to seal, and trim any overhang. Tuck and crimp the rim so the cream is fully enclosed.
6. Top with pine nuts: Scatter the pine nuts generously over the lid and press them lightly so they adhere. For an even bake, prick the lid once or twice with a fork.
7. Bake on the middle rack for 35-45 minutes, until the crust is deep golden and the pine nuts are toasted. If the nuts brown too fast, tent loosely with foil for the last 10 minutes.
8. Cool the torta completely in the pan (the cream needs to set), then unmold. Just before serving, dust the top generously with powdered sugar. Serve at room temperature or lightly chilled.

</details>

<sub>📷 fugzu from Pistoia, Italia, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-torta-della-nonna/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Castagnaccio_with_pine_nuts.jpg/480px-Castagnaccio_with_pine_nuts.jpg" alt="Photo of Castagnaccio (Tuscan Chestnut Flour Cake)">

#### 🌰 [Castagnaccio (Tuscan Chestnut Flour Cake)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-castagnaccio/)
Italian (Tuscan) · snack · easy  
**$0.97/serving** · 238 kcal · P4 · C34 · F9 · ⏱ 70 min · 🍽 8  

The real Tuscan autumn classic: a thin, rustic unleavened cake made almost entirely from sweet chestnut flour, water, and good olive oil. No added sugar — the natural sweetness comes from the chestnut flour and plump raisins — finished with pine nuts, rosemary, and a cracked, crackly top. Dense, moist, faintly bitter-sweet, and unmistakably Tuscan.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/2 cups chestnut flour (farina di castagne) — the defining ingredient; catalog has no chestnut flour so logged under flour
- **Water** — about 2 1/4 cups lukewarm water, added gradually to make a smooth pourable batter
- **Extra virgin olive oil** — 5 tbsp extra-virgin olive oil (about 1/3 cup) — 3 tbsp in the batter, 2 tbsp to oil the pan and drizzle
- **Raisins** — 1/2 cup raisins, soaked 15 min in warm water then drained
- **Pine nuts** — 2 oz pine nuts (about 1/3 cup)
- **Walnuts** — 1/4 cup walnuts, roughly chopped (optional, traditional in some versions) _(optional)_
- **Fresh rosemary** — 1 sprig fresh rosemary, leaves picked
- **Orange** — zest of 1 orange (optional, common in Florentine versions) _(optional)_
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp fine salt

**Steps**

1. Preheat the oven to 375F (190C). Soak the raisins in a small bowl of warm water for 15 minutes, then drain and pat dry. Brush a roughly 10-inch round or rectangular baking pan generously with about 1 tbsp of the olive oil.
2. Sift the chestnut flour into a large bowl with the salt to break up its natural lumps — chestnut flour clumps easily and sifting is essential for a smooth batter.
3. Gradually whisk in the lukewarm water a little at a time, working out every lump, until you have a smooth, lump-free batter with the consistency of thin pancake batter or heavy cream. It should pour easily and ribbon off the whisk.
4. Whisk in 3 tbsp of the olive oil and the optional orange zest. Stir in about two-thirds of the soaked raisins and half the pine nuts, reserving the rest for the top.
5. Pour the batter into the oiled pan — it should sit only about 1/2 to 3/4 inch deep; castagnaccio is meant to be thin. Scatter the reserved raisins, the remaining pine nuts, the chopped walnuts, and the rosemary leaves evenly over the surface, then drizzle the last 1 tbsp olive oil over the top.
6. Bake for 30 to 40 minutes, until the surface is dry, deeply cracked all over like dried earth, and the edges pull slightly from the pan. The top should be set and the characteristic crazed crust formed; the inside stays moist and dense.
7. Let cool in the pan for at least 15 minutes — it firms up as it cools and is traditionally served at room temperature, cut into squares or wedges. It keeps well, covered, for 2 to 3 days.

</details>

<sub>📷 Gordon Joly, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-castagnaccio/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4d/2022-01-28_15_54_50_A_plate_of_garlic_knots_in_the_Dulles_section_of_Sterling%2C_Loudoun_County%2C_Virginia.jpg/480px-2022-01-28_15_54_50_A_plate_of_garlic_knots_in_the_Dulles_section_of_Sterling%2C_Loudoun_County%2C_Virginia.jpg" alt="Photo of Garlic Knots">

#### 🧄 [Garlic Knots](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-garlic-knots/)
Italian-American · snack · easy  
**$1.53/serving** · 191 kcal · P3 · C1 · F19 · ⏱ 75 min · 🍽 4  

Pillowy pizza-dough knots baked until golden, then tossed while hot in a garlicky butter loaded with fresh parsley and parmesan. The classic pizzeria side that disappears off the tray in minutes.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Refrigerated pizza dough** — 1 lb ball, store-bought or homemade, at room temperature
- **Butter** — about 5 tbsp, melted
- **Olive oil** — 1 tbsp, for the dough and pan
- **Garlic** — 5 cloves, finely minced or grated
- **Fresh parsley** — 3 tbsp finely chopped
- **Parmesan** — 1/3 cup finely grated, plus more to finish
- **Salt** — for the butter and to flour the surface
- **Red pepper flakes** — pinch, for heat _(optional)_
- **All-purpose flour** — for dusting the work surface

**Steps**

1. Let the pizza dough sit at room temperature for 30-45 minutes so it's relaxed and easy to stretch. Heat the oven to 425F (220C) and line a baking sheet with parchment.
2. Lightly flour your surface and pat the dough into a rough 8x10-inch rectangle. Cut it into 12 strips about 3/4 inch wide, then roll each strip into a 6-7 inch rope under your palms.
3. Tie each rope into a simple knot, tucking the ends under so they don't unravel. Arrange the knots on the sheet about an inch apart and brush lightly with olive oil. Let them rest 15 minutes to puff slightly.
4. Bake for 15-18 minutes, until the knots are golden brown and sound hollow when tapped.
5. While they bake, gently warm the melted butter with the minced garlic over low heat for 1-2 minutes just until fragrant (do not brown the garlic), then stir in the chopped parsley, salt, and red pepper flakes.
6. As soon as the knots come out, brush or toss them generously with the garlic-parsley butter so they soak it up while hot.
7. Shower with the grated parmesan, toss once more, and serve warm with extra parmesan and a side of marinara if you like.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Famartin — CC BY-SA 4.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-garlic-knots/)</sub>
