<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0f/Florentines.jpg/480px-Florentines.jpg" alt="Photo of Florentine Cookies">

#### 🍪 [Florentine Cookies](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-florentine-cookies/)
Italian-French · snack · medium  
**$0.09/serving** · 10 kcal · P0 · C1 · F1 · ⏱ 50 min · 🍽 12  

Crisp, lacy almond cookies set with a buttery caramel of honey, sugar, and cream, then finished with a swipe of dark chocolate. Delicate, golden, and elegant — the classic Italian-French tea cookie.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Slivered almonds** — finely chopped or sliced almonds
- **Butter** — 0.5 tbsp
- **White sugar** — 0.3 tbsp
- **Honey** — 0.15 tbsp
- **Heavy cream** — 0.2 cup
- **All-purpose flour** — 0.1 cup
- **Salt** — 0.02 tsp
- **Vanilla extract** — 0.05 tsp _(optional)_
- **Dark chocolate** — for coating the backs

**Steps**

1. Preheat the oven to 350F (175C) and line two baking sheets with parchment paper or silicone mats. Toss the chopped almonds with the flour and a pinch of salt in a bowl and set aside.
2. In a small saucepan, combine the butter, sugar, honey, and heavy cream. Bring to a gentle boil over medium heat, stirring, until the mixture reaches 238F (soft-ball stage) or coats the back of a spoon, about 2 to 3 minutes.
3. Remove from the heat and stir in the vanilla, then fold in the almond-flour mixture until evenly coated. Let the batter rest 2 to 3 minutes to thicken slightly.
4. Drop level teaspoons of batter onto the lined sheets, spacing them at least 3 inches apart since they spread a lot. Flatten each slightly with the back of a spoon.
5. Bake one sheet at a time for 8 to 10 minutes, until the cookies are bubbling, lacy, and deep golden-brown at the edges. Watch closely near the end to avoid burning.
6. Let the cookies cool on the sheet for 5 minutes to firm up, then slide the parchment onto a rack and cool completely before peeling them off.
7. Melt the dark chocolate gently (microwave in 20-second bursts, stirring). Spread a thin layer on the flat back of each cooled cookie.
8. Drag a fork through the chocolate to make wavy lines, or sandwich two cookies back-to-back. Set chocolate-side up until firm, about 30 minutes.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Tympan — CC BY-SA 4.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-florentine-cookies/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/11/Anpan.jpg/480px-Anpan.jpg" alt="Photo of Anpan (Japanese Sweet Red Bean Buns)">

#### 🍞 [Anpan (Japanese Sweet Red Bean Buns)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-anpan/)
Japanese · snack · medium  
**$0.51/serving** · 223 kcal · P6 · C34 · F7 · ⏱ 200 min · 🍽 8  

Pillowy-soft, milk-enriched Japanese sweet rolls filled with smooth sweet red bean paste (koshian). A tangzhong water-roux keeps the crumb cloud-like and fresh for days, while a glossy egg wash and a pinch of sesame on top give anpan their iconic bakery look. This is the classic version sold in Japanese bakeries since the 1870s — gently sweet dough, generous bean filling, no shortcuts.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/4 cups bread flour (about 280g), plus 3 tbsp held back for the tangzhong
- **Milk** — 2/3 cup whole milk, lukewarm (plus 1/4 cup water cooked into the tangzhong)
- **Water** — 1/4 cup water, for the tangzhong roux
- **Sugar** — 1/4 cup granulated sugar
- **Instant yeast** — 2 tsp instant yeast
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp salt
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs — 1 in the dough, 1 beaten for the egg wash
- **Butter** — 3 tbsp unsalted butter, softened
- **Red beans (canned)** — about 1 1/2 cups (480g) smooth sweet red bean paste / koshian, divided into 8 balls — or cook the canned red beans down with sugar to make your own
- **Sesame seeds** — 1 tsp black or white sesame seeds, for topping _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Make the tangzhong: In a small saucepan whisk the 3 tbsp reserved flour with the 1/4 cup water and 2 tbsp of the milk until smooth. Cook over medium-low, stirring constantly, until it thickens into a soft pudding-like paste that holds a line when you drag the whisk, about 2-3 minutes. Scrape into a bowl and let it cool to lukewarm.
2. Make the dough: In a large bowl combine the remaining bread flour, sugar, instant yeast, and salt. Add the cooled tangzhong, the remaining lukewarm milk, and 1 egg. Mix to a shaggy dough, then knead 8-10 minutes by hand (or 6-7 minutes in a stand mixer) until smooth. Add the softened butter a little at a time and keep kneading until the dough is glossy, elastic, and passes a thin windowpane, 6-8 more minutes.
3. First rise: Shape into a ball, place in a lightly oiled bowl, cover, and let rise in a warm spot until doubled, about 60-90 minutes.
4. Divide and fill: Punch down and divide into 8 equal pieces (about 70g each); roll each into a ball, cover, and rest 10 minutes. Flatten each ball into a 4-inch disk, thinner at the edges. Place a ball of red bean paste in the center, then pull the dough up and around the filling, pinching the seam firmly shut. Roll seam-side down to seal.
5. Shape and proof: Arrange the buns seam-side down on a parchment-lined baking sheet, spaced apart. Gently flatten each to the classic squat anpan dome. Cover loosely and proof until puffy and nearly doubled, 40-50 minutes. They should spring back slowly when poked.
6. Finish the tops: Heat the oven to 350F (175C). Beat the second egg with a splash of water and brush a thin, even coat over each bun. For the traditional look, press a shallow well into the center with a damp finger or the back of a chopstick and sprinkle sesame seeds into it.
7. Bake: Bake on the center rack for 14-17 minutes, until deeply golden and shiny and the buns sound hollow when tapped. If they color too fast, tent loosely with foil for the last few minutes.
8. Cool: Transfer to a rack and cool at least 20 minutes — the filling is molten hot straight from the oven. Eat the same day for peak softness; the tangzhong keeps them tender for 2-3 days stored airtight.

</details>

<sub>📷 ja:User:捕澤, CC BY-SA 3.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-anpan/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/1/1f/Japanese_castella_cake%3B_2018.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Nagasaki Castella (Japanese Honey Sponge Cake)">

#### 🍯 [Classic Nagasaki Castella (Japanese Honey Sponge Cake)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-castella-cake/)
Japanese · snack · medium  
**$0.44/serving** · 204 kcal · P5 · C39 · F3 · ⏱ 90 min · 🍽 10  

An authentic Nagasaki-style kasutera: a tall, tight-crumbed honey sponge built entirely on whipped whole eggs, sugar, bread flour, and honey — no butter, no oil, no baking powder. The whole-egg foam method and a warm honey-milk syrup give it its signature moist, fine texture and deep golden-brown top. Baked low and slow in a deep paper-lined frame, then wrapped and rested overnight so the crumb settles into its dense, springy perfection.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Eggs** — 6 large eggs, at room temperature
- **Sugar** — 1 cup (200g) granulated sugar
- **All-purpose flour** — 1 1/4 cups (150g) bread flour, sifted twice
- **Honey** — 3 tbsp honey
- **Whole milk** — 2 tbsp whole milk
- **Mirin** — 1 tbsp mirin (or substitute warm water)
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt

**Steps**

1. Line a deep 8x8-inch (or equivalent loaf-style) frame with two layers of parchment, building the walls a good inch above the rim so the cake can climb. Preheat the oven to 320F (160C). In a small bowl, gently warm the honey, milk, and mirin together (microwave 15 seconds or set over warm water) until fluid and combined; keep it warm but not hot.
2. Set a large heatproof bowl over a pot of barely simmering water. Crack in all 6 eggs, add the sugar and salt, and whisk constantly until the mixture reaches about 100F (38C) and the sugar has fully dissolved — it should feel just warm to a clean fingertip, with no grit. This warming step is essential for a stable, voluminous whole-egg foam.
3. Move the bowl to a stand mixer (or use a hand mixer) and whip on high for 6 to 8 minutes until the foam is very pale, tripled in volume, and thick enough to hold a ribbon that sits on the surface for several seconds before sinking. Reduce to low speed and whip 2 more minutes to break down large bubbles and tighten the foam — this gives castella its fine, even crumb.
4. Pour the warm honey mixture down the side of the bowl and fold it in gently with a spatula, just until streak-free. Sift the bread flour over the foam in three additions, folding from the bottom up after each — slow, deliberate strokes. Stop the moment no dry flour remains; overworking deflates the batter and toughens the crumb.
5. Pour the batter into the lined frame from about 8 inches up to help pop surface bubbles. Tap the frame firmly on the counter several times, then drag a chopstick or skewer back and forth through the batter in a zigzag to release trapped air. Smooth the top.
6. Bake at 320F (160C) for 12 minutes, then lower the oven to 300F (150C) and bake another 40 to 45 minutes, until the top is deep mahogany-brown and a skewer comes out clean. If the top colors too fast in the first 15 minutes, loosely tent with foil.
7. Immediately on removing it, drop the frame onto the counter once from a few inches up — this 'shock' keeps the cake from shrinking and wrinkling as it cools. Invert the hot cake onto a sheet of plastic wrap, peel off the parchment, and wrap the cake tightly while still warm to trap moisture.
8. Rest the wrapped cake at room temperature for at least 8 hours, ideally overnight — this is non-negotiable. The crumb settles, the moisture redistributes, and the flavor deepens. Trim the four edges flat, then slice into thick 1-inch bars with a clean, warm knife.

</details>

<sub>📷 Helen.Yang from China, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-castella-cake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b4/Cream_pan.jpg/480px-Cream_pan.jpg" alt="Photo of Cream Pan (Japanese Custard Cream Bread)">

#### 🍞 [Cream Pan (Japanese Custard Cream Bread)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-cream-pan/)
Japanese · snack · medium  
**$0.52/serving** · 278 kcal · P6 · C48 · F6 · ⏱ 200 min · 🍽 8  

Classic Japanese bakery cream pan: pillowy-soft, milky tangzhong-style enriched bread wrapped around a silky homemade vanilla pastry cream, folded into the traditional glove shape and baked to a glossy golden brown. Soft enough to tear by hand, with a custard center that's rich but not cloying.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/2 cups bread flour (for the dough), plus a little extra for dusting
- **Whole milk** — 1 1/2 cups whole milk total: ~2/3 cup warm for the dough, ~1 cup for the pastry cream
- **Sugar** — about 11 tbsp sugar total: 3 tbsp in the dough, 8 tbsp (1/2 cup) in the pastry cream
- **Butter** — 3 tbsp unsalted butter, softened (worked into the dough)
- **Eggs** — 3 large eggs: 1 whole in the dough, 2 yolks for the pastry cream, plus reserve a little white/whole egg for the egg wash
- **Instant yeast** — 2 tsp instant yeast
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp salt
- **Cornstarch** — 3 tbsp cornstarch (thickens the pastry cream)
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 1/2 tsp vanilla extract for the pastry cream

**Steps**

1. Make the pastry cream first so it can chill. In a saucepan, warm 1 cup whole milk until steaming. In a bowl, whisk 2 egg yolks with 8 tbsp (1/2 cup) of the sugar and the 3 tbsp cornstarch until pale and smooth. Slowly pour in about half the hot milk while whisking constantly to temper, then return everything to the saucepan.
2. Cook the custard over medium heat, whisking nonstop, until it thickens to a pudding and large bubbles burst on the surface, about 2-4 minutes. Remove from heat, whisk in the 1 1/2 tsp vanilla, then scrape into a shallow dish. Press plastic wrap directly onto the surface and refrigerate until cold and firm, at least 1 hour.
3. Make the dough. In a large bowl, combine 2 1/2 cups bread flour, 2 tsp instant yeast, 3 tbsp sugar, and 3/4 tsp salt. Add 1 whole egg and about 2/3 cup warm milk (around 100F). Mix to a shaggy dough, then knead 8-10 minutes until smooth and elastic.
4. Add the 3 tbsp softened butter a little at a time, kneading until fully absorbed and the dough is smooth, supple, and slightly tacky (it should pass a thin windowpane test). Shape into a ball, place in a greased bowl, cover, and let rise in a warm spot until doubled, about 1 to 1 1/2 hours.
5. Punch down the dough and divide into 8 equal pieces. Roll each into a ball, cover, and rest 10 minutes. Flatten each ball into a 4-5 inch oval. Spoon a heaping tablespoon of cold pastry cream onto one half, leaving a clean border.
6. Fold the dough over the filling into a half-moon and press the edges firmly to seal completely (a leaky seal lets cream burst out). For the traditional glove shape, cut 3-4 short slits into the rounded edge. Place on a parchment-lined tray, cover loosely, and proof until puffy, 40-50 minutes.
7. Heat the oven to 350F (175C). Beat the reserved egg with a splash of milk and brush the buns lightly for a glossy finish, avoiding the cut slits.
8. Bake 13-16 minutes until deep golden brown and the buns sound hollow when tapped. Cool on a rack at least 20 minutes so the custard sets before eating; they keep best in an airtight container and are wonderful slightly warmed.

</details>

<sub>📷 7'o'7, CC BY-SA 3.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-cream-pan/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Souffl%C3%A9-style_cheesecake_001.jpg/480px-Souffl%C3%A9-style_cheesecake_001.jpg" alt="Photo of Japanese Cotton Cheesecake">

#### 🍰 [Japanese Cotton Cheesecake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-japanese-cotton-cheesecake/)
Japanese · snack · hard  
**$0.46/serving** · 83 kcal · P5 · C4 · F5 · ⏱ 110 min · 🍽 8  

The famously light, jiggly Japanese soufflé cheesecake — a cream cheese custard folded into glossy meringue and baked gently in a water bath until it rises tall, then settles into a cloud-soft, melt-in-the-mouth crumb. Less sweet and far lighter than a New York cheesecake.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Cream cheese** — 250g, softened
- **Butter** — 60g unsalted
- **Whole milk** — 100ml
- **Eggs** — yolks and whites separated
- **White sugar** — 100g, for the meringue
- **All-purpose flour** — 60g cake/all-purpose, sifted
- **Cornstarch** — 20g, sifted
- **Bottled lemon juice** — 1 tbsp
- **Cream of tartar** — 1/4 tsp, stabilizes meringue
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp
- **Salt** — pinch

**Steps**

1. Heat oven to 320F (160C). Line the bottom and sides of an 8-inch round pan with parchment, then wrap the outside tightly in foil to keep water out during the bath. Bring a kettle of water to a boil for the bain-marie.
2. Gently melt the cream cheese, butter, and milk together in a heatproof bowl over a pan of barely simmering water, whisking until completely smooth with no lumps. Remove from heat and let cool until just warm.
3. Whisk the 6 egg yolks, vanilla, and lemon juice into the warm cheese mixture. Sift in the flour and cornstarch and whisk until silky and fully combined, then set the batter aside.
4. In a clean, grease-free bowl, beat the 6 egg whites with the cream of tartar and salt until foamy, then add the sugar in three additions, beating to soft, droopy peaks — do not go to stiff peaks or the cake will crack and dry out.
5. Fold one-third of the meringue into the cheese batter to lighten it, then gently fold in the rest in two batches with a spatula until just uniform, keeping as much air as possible.
6. Pour into the prepared pan and tap once on the counter to release large bubbles. Set the pan in a larger roasting tray and pour in the boiling water to come about 1 inch up the sides.
7. Bake 25 minutes at 320F, then lower to 250F (120C) and bake 55-65 minutes more, until risen, golden, and set with only a faint jiggle in the center.
8. Turn off the oven, crack the door open, and let the cake rest inside 15-20 minutes to prevent sudden collapse, then remove and cool slightly.
9. Invert onto a plate, peel the parchment, and turn right-side up. Serve warm for maximum jiggle or chill for a denser, sliceable crumb; dust with powdered sugar if desired.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-japanese-cotton-cheesecake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Japanese_Milk_Bread.jpg/480px-Japanese_Milk_Bread.jpg" alt="Photo of Japanese Milk Bread (Shokupan)">

#### 🍞 [Japanese Milk Bread (Shokupan)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-japanese-milk-bread-shokupan/)
Japanese · breakfast · medium  
**$0.09/serving** · 55 kcal · P1 · C11 · F1 · ⏱ 210 min · 🍽 12  

Cloud-soft, pillowy Japanese white bread made with a tangzhong (cooked flour-and-milk roux) that locks in moisture for a tender, feathery crumb that stays fresh for days. Lightly sweet, faintly milky, and perfect for toast, sandwiches, or pulling apart warm.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — bread flour preferred for chew and rise; ~360g total including the tangzhong portion
- **Whole milk** — warm; split between tangzhong and dough
- **Instant yeast** — about 7g / 2 tsp
- **White sugar** — about 50g / 1/4 cup
- **Salt** — about 6g / 1 tsp
- **Eggs** — 1 in dough + reserve a little beaten egg for the egg wash
- **Butter** — softened, unsalted; about 40g, added after the dough comes together

**Steps**

1. Make the tangzhong: whisk 20g of the flour with 50g milk and 50g water in a small saucepan over medium heat, stirring constantly until it thickens into a smooth pudding-like paste (about 65 C / 149 F). Scrape into a bowl and cool to room temperature.
2. In a large bowl or stand-mixer bowl, combine the remaining flour, sugar, instant yeast, and salt. Add the cooled tangzhong, the rest of the warm milk (about 120g), and 1 egg. Mix until a shaggy dough forms.
3. Knead on medium for 10-12 minutes (or 15-20 by hand) until smooth and elastic, then add the softened butter a little at a time and knead another 8-10 minutes until the dough is glossy and passes the windowpane test (stretches thin without tearing).
4. Shape into a ball, place in a greased bowl, cover, and let rise in a warm spot for 60-90 minutes until doubled.
5. Punch down gently, divide into 3 equal pieces, roll each into an oval, fold into thirds, then roll up into a tight cylinder. Place the three rolls seam-side down side by side in a greased loaf or pullman pan.
6. Cover and proof again for 45-60 minutes until the dough crests just below (open-top) or near the rim of the pan and springs back slowly when poked.
7. Brush the tops gently with the reserved beaten egg for a glossy crust. Bake in an oven preheated to 180 C / 350 F for 30-35 minutes until deep golden and the loaf sounds hollow when tapped.
8. Immediately turn the loaf out of the pan onto a rack and cool completely before slicing so the crumb sets and stays fluffy.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: ChrisHamby — CC BY-SA 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-japanese-milk-bread-shokupan/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/a/a6/Strawberry_short_cake%EF%BC%88%E3%82%B9%E3%83%88%E3%83%AD%E3%83%99%E3%83%AA%E3%83%BC%E3%82%B7%E3%83%A7%E3%83%BC%E3%83%88%E3%82%B1%E3%83%BC%E3%82%AD%EF%BC%89_%282874857378%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Japanese Strawberry Shortcake">

#### 🍰 [Japanese Strawberry Shortcake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-japanese-strawberry-shortcake/)
Japanese · snack · medium  
**$1.29/serving** · 207 kcal · P5 · C34 · F6 · ⏱ 120 min · 🍽 8  

The classic Japanese ichigo shortcake: a featherlight genoise sponge soaked in vanilla syrup, layered and masked with lightly sweetened whipped cream, and piled with fresh strawberries. Restrained sweetness, cloud-soft crumb, and a clean finish — exactly the cake you see in every Japanese bakery and on Christmas Eve tables across Japan.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Eggs** — 4 large eggs, room temperature
- **Sugar** — 1/2 cup (100g) granulated sugar, for the sponge
- **All-purpose flour** — 1 cup (120g) cake or all-purpose flour, sifted
- **Butter** — 2 tbsp (30g) unsalted butter, melted
- **Whole milk** — 2 tbsp whole milk, warm
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Heavy cream** — 2 cups (480ml) cold heavy whipping cream
- **Powdered sugar** — 1/4 cup (30g) powdered sugar, for the whipped cream
- **Strawberries** — 4 cups fresh strawberries (about 1.5 lb), hulled
- **Water** — 1/4 cup water, for the soaking syrup
- **White sugar** — 2 tbsp sugar, for the soaking syrup

**Steps**

1. Make the syrup first so it cools: combine 1/4 cup water and 2 tbsp sugar in a small pan, bring to a simmer until dissolved, remove from heat and stir in a few drops of the vanilla. Set aside. Preheat the oven to 340F (170C) and line the bottom of an 8-inch round cake pan with parchment; do not grease the sides so the sponge can climb.
2. Build the genoise: set a heatproof bowl over a pan of barely simmering water and whisk the 4 eggs with 1/2 cup granulated sugar until just warm to the touch (about 100F) and the sugar dissolves. Off the heat, beat with a hand or stand mixer on high for 6-8 minutes until pale, tripled in volume, and thick enough to leave a ribbon that holds for 3-4 seconds.
3. Warm the 2 tbsp butter with 2 tbsp milk and 1 tsp vanilla until the butter melts; keep it warm. Sift the cup of flour over the egg foam in three additions, folding gently with a spatula each time — cut down through the center and lift, rotating the bowl, just until no streaks remain. Stop the moment it is combined to keep the air in.
4. Temper the butter mixture: scoop a large spoonful of batter into the warm butter-milk, stir to lighten, then fold that back into the main batter quickly and thoroughly. Pour into the pan, tap once firmly on the counter to pop large bubbles, and bake 28-32 minutes until golden and springy and a skewer comes out clean.
5. Drop the pan from a few inches onto the counter to prevent shrinking, then invert onto a rack, peel the parchment, and cool completely. Meanwhile slice most of the strawberries about 1/4-inch thick for the filling, reserving the prettiest whole berries for the top. Chill the bowl and beaters for the cream.
6. Whip the cold heavy cream with the powdered sugar and remaining vanilla to soft-medium peaks — stop while it still looks glossy and pipeable; overwhipping turns it grainy. Level the domed top off the cooled sponge with a serrated knife and slice the cake horizontally into two even layers.
7. Assemble: place the bottom layer cut-side up on a plate and brush generously with the cooled syrup. Spread a layer of cream, cover with sliced strawberries in a single tight layer, then a thin layer of cream over the berries. Set the second layer on top, brush with syrup, and press gently to settle.
8. Mask the whole cake with a smooth coat of the remaining whipped cream, saving a little to pipe rosettes around the top edge. Crown with the reserved whole strawberries. Chill at least 1 hour before slicing with a hot, wiped knife so the layers stay clean. Best eaten the same day.

</details>

<sub>📷 Yusuke Kawasaki from Tokyo, Japan, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-japanese-strawberry-shortcake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Green_tea_ice_cream_and_tea_roll_cakes.jpg/480px-Green_tea_ice_cream_and_tea_roll_cakes.jpg" alt="Photo of Matcha Roll Cake (抹茶ロールケーキ)">

#### 🍵 [Matcha Roll Cake (抹茶ロールケーキ)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-matcha-roll-cake/)
Japanese · snack · medium  
**$0.77/serving** · 145 kcal · P5 · C20 · F5 · ⏱ 130 min · 🍽 8  

A tender Japanese matcha sponge roll: a light genoise-style biscuit sponge with stone-ground matcha folded into the flour, baked fast and hot, rolled while warm to set the curl, then unrolled and filled with softly whipped matcha cream. Earthy, barely sweet, and cloud-soft — the real teahouse version a careful home baker can absolutely nail.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Eggs** — 4 large eggs, at room temperature
- **Sugar** — 1/2 cup granulated sugar (for the sponge)
- **All-purpose flour** — 1/2 cup cake or all-purpose flour, sifted
- **Cornstarch** — 1 tbsp cornstarch, sifted with the flour for a tender crumb
- **Matcha powder** — about 1 1/2 tbsp culinary matcha for the sponge plus a little extra for dusting
- **Butter** — 2 tbsp unsalted butter, melted and kept warm
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt
- **Heavy cream** — 1 cup cold heavy whipping cream (for the filling)
- **Powdered sugar** — 3 tbsp powdered sugar (for the whipped cream)
- **Matcha powder** — 2 tsp matcha whisked into the cream

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 400°F (200°C). Line a 10x15-inch (quarter sheet) jelly-roll pan with parchment, leaving a small overhang on the long sides. Sift the flour, cornstarch, and 1 1/2 tbsp matcha together twice so the matcha is lump-free and evenly dispersed; set aside.
2. Make a hot-whip sponge base: combine the whole eggs and granulated sugar in a heatproof bowl and set it over a pan of barely simmering water. Whisk constantly until the mixture is just warm to the touch (about 100°F) and the sugar has dissolved, 1-2 minutes. This warmth lets the eggs whip to maximum volume.
3. Take the bowl off the heat and beat with a hand or stand mixer on high until the batter is pale, thick, and triples in volume — about 5-7 minutes. It should fall in a slowly dissolving ribbon when you lift the beaters. Beat in the vanilla and salt.
4. Sift the matcha-flour mixture over the eggs in two additions, folding with a spatula gently but thoroughly each time — cut down through the center, sweep around, and turn the bowl. Stop the moment no dry streaks remain. Stir a big spoonful of batter into the warm melted butter to loosen it, then fold that back in. Do not deflate the foam.
5. Pour into the prepared pan and spread level with an offset spatula, pushing batter fully into the corners. Tap the pan once on the counter to pop large bubbles. Bake 10-12 minutes, until the top springs back when lightly pressed and the edges just begin to pull away. Do not overbake or the sponge will crack when rolled.
6. Roll it warm to train the curl: lay a clean kitchen towel on the counter and dust it with a little sifted matcha (or powdered sugar). Invert the hot cake onto the towel, peel off the parchment, and starting from a short end, roll the cake up snugly inside the towel. Let it cool completely rolled, seam-side down, 30-45 minutes.
7. Whip the filling: with cold beaters and bowl, whip the heavy cream with the powdered sugar and 2 tsp sifted matcha until it holds firm, spreadable peaks — stop before it turns grainy. Keep cold.
8. Gently unroll the cooled cake (it will keep a gentle curve). Spread the matcha cream evenly over the surface, leaving a 1/2-inch border at the far end so it doesn't squeeze out. Re-roll snugly without the towel, finishing seam-side down. Wrap in plastic and chill at least 1 hour to set. Trim the ends, dust with matcha, and slice with a clean warm knife.

</details>

<sub>📷 Norton Ip, CC BY-SA 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-matcha-roll-cake/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/Melonpan.jpg/480px-Melonpan.jpg" alt="Photo of Melonpan">

#### 🍈 [Melonpan](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-melonpan/)
Japanese · snack · hard  
**$0.21/serving** · 75 kcal · P3 · C11 · F2 · ⏱ 210 min · 🍽 8  

Japanese bakery-style melonpan: pillowy, lightly sweet enriched milk-bread buns wrapped in a crisp sugar-cookie crust, scored in the signature crosshatch and sparkled with coarse sugar. Crackly on the outside, soft and fluffy within.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — bread flour for dough + all-purpose for cookie crust, ~400g total
- **Instant yeast** — 2 tsp / ~6g
- **Whole milk** — warm, ~140ml for the dough
- **Eggs** — 1 for dough, 1 for cookie crust
- **Butter** — 30g softened in dough + 50g softened in crust
- **White sugar** — 40g in dough + 70g in cookie crust
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp
- **Vanilla extract** — 1/2 tsp in cookie crust
- **Turbinado sugar** — coarse sugar for sprinkling the crust _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Make the dough: stir the instant yeast into warm milk and let it sit 5 minutes until foamy, then add 1 beaten egg, 40g sugar, and salt. Mix in roughly 250g bread flour to form a shaggy dough.
2. Knead the dough 8-10 minutes until smooth and elastic, working in the 30g softened butter halfway through; it should pass a thin windowpane test. Cover and let rise in a warm spot 60-90 minutes until doubled.
3. Make the cookie crust: cream 50g softened butter with 70g sugar until pale, beat in 1 egg and the vanilla, then fold in about 150g flour to form a soft cookie dough. Chill 20-30 minutes until workable.
4. Punch down the risen dough and divide into 8 equal pieces; roll each into a tight, smooth ball and rest 10 minutes under a towel.
5. Divide the chilled cookie dough into 8 pieces, flatten each between plastic wrap into a thin disc, and drape one over each bun, pressing gently so it covers the top two-thirds.
6. Roll the cookie-topped buns in coarse sugar, then score a crosshatch pattern into the crust with a bench scraper or knife to create the melon-rind look.
7. Arrange on lined trays with space to expand and proof uncovered 40-50 minutes until puffy (uncovered keeps the crust crackly).
8. Bake in a preheated 340F (170C) oven for 13-15 minutes until the cookie crust is set and pale golden; avoid over-browning so they stay tender. Cool on a rack at least 15 minutes before eating.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Sakurai Midori — CC BY-SA 3.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-melonpan/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Challah_Bread_Six_Braid_1.JPG/480px-Challah_Bread_Six_Braid_1.JPG" alt="Photo of Challah Bread">

#### 🍞 [Challah Bread](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-challah-bread/)
Jewish · snack · medium  
**$0.38/serving** · 259 kcal · P6 · C40 · F8 · ⏱ 220 min · 🍽 12  

A classic braided egg bread with a glossy mahogany crust and a soft, faintly sweet, pillowy crumb. Enriched with eggs and oil, it pulls apart in tender strands and is just as good warm with butter as it is sliced for French toast the next day.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — about 4 cups bread or all-purpose flour
- **Instant yeast** — 2 1/4 tsp (1 packet)
- **Eggs** — 3 for dough, 1 reserved for egg wash
- **Vegetable oil** — about 1/3 cup neutral oil
- **Honey** — about 1/4 cup; or substitute sugar
- **Sugar** — 2 tbsp to feed the yeast
- **Salt** — about 2 tsp
- **Water** — 3/4 cup warm water (100-110F)
- **Sesame seeds** — or poppy seeds, for topping _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. In a large bowl, stir the warm water (100-110F) with the sugar and yeast; let sit 5-10 minutes until foamy. If it does not foam, your yeast is dead-start over with fresh yeast.
2. Whisk in the honey, oil, and 3 of the eggs until smooth. Add the salt, then add the flour about a cup at a time, stirring until a shaggy dough forms.
3. Knead on a lightly floured surface 8-10 minutes (or 6-7 minutes with a stand mixer dough hook) until the dough is smooth, elastic, and slightly tacky but not sticky. Add flour only a tablespoon at a time if needed.
4. Place the dough in an oiled bowl, turn to coat, cover, and let rise in a warm spot 1.5 to 2 hours, until doubled in size.
5. Punch down the dough and divide it into 3 (or 6) equal ropes, rolling each into a strand about 16 inches long, slightly tapered at the ends. Pinch the tops together and braid snugly, then pinch the bottom ends and tuck both ends under.
6. Transfer the braid to a parchment-lined baking sheet, cover loosely, and let it proof 45-60 minutes until puffy and nearly doubled. Meanwhile, heat the oven to 350F.
7. Beat the reserved egg with a teaspoon of water and brush the loaf thoroughly, getting into the crevices. Sprinkle with sesame or poppy seeds if using.
8. Bake 25-32 minutes until deep golden brown and the loaf sounds hollow when tapped (internal temp 190F). Tent with foil if it browns too fast.
9. Cool on a rack at least 30 minutes before slicing so the crumb sets.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Aviv Hod — CC BY 3.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-challah-bread/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5e/Freshly_baked_chocolate_rugelach.jpg/480px-Freshly_baked_chocolate_rugelach.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Cream Cheese Rugelach">

#### 🥐 [Classic Cream Cheese Rugelach](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-rugelach/)
Jewish · snack · medium  
**$0.35/serving** · 130 kcal · P2 · C12 · F9 · ⏱ 200 min · 🍽 32  

Tender, flaky little crescent cookies built on the classic Ashkenazi-Jewish cream cheese dough, rolled around a buttery cinnamon-sugar, walnut, and raisin filling with a smear of apricot jam. The cream cheese makes the dough rich and shatteringly short, while a slow overnight chill keeps the rolls crisp and the filling from leaking. Brushed with egg and dusted with cinnamon sugar, they bake to a deep golden brown.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 cups all-purpose flour
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks) unsalted butter, cold and cubed
- **Cream cheese** — 8 oz cream cheese, cold and cubed
- **Apricot jam** — 1/2 cup apricot jam, for spreading
- **Brown sugar** — 1/2 cup packed light brown sugar, for filling
- **Cinnamon** — 2 tsp ground cinnamon, for filling
- **Walnuts** — 1 cup walnuts, finely chopped
- **Raisins** — 3/4 cup raisins, chopped
- **Eggs** — 1 large egg, beaten, for egg wash
- **White sugar** — 2 tbsp granulated sugar, for topping

**Steps**

1. Make the dough: In a food processor, pulse the flour and salt to combine. Add the cold cubed butter and cream cheese and pulse in short bursts just until the dough comes together in moist clumps, about 15-20 pulses. Do not overwork it; you want visible flecks of butter. Turn out, divide into 4 equal pieces, flatten each into a disk, wrap in plastic, and refrigerate at least 2 hours or overnight.
2. Make the filling: Stir together the brown sugar and cinnamon. In a separate bowl, combine the finely chopped walnuts and chopped raisins. Have the apricot jam ready, stirred smooth.
3. Roll out: Working with one disk at a time (keep the rest chilled), roll on a lightly floured surface into a 9-inch circle about 1/8 inch thick. Spread a thin layer of apricot jam over the entire circle, sprinkle evenly with a quarter of the cinnamon-brown-sugar, then a quarter of the walnut-raisin mixture. Press the filling gently with your hand so it adheres.
4. Shape: Using a pizza wheel or sharp knife, cut the circle into 8 equal wedges, like a pizza. Starting at the wide outer edge, roll each wedge up tightly toward the point to form a small crescent. Place point-side down on a parchment-lined baking sheet, curving the ends slightly inward.
5. Chill again: Repeat with the remaining 3 disks. Refrigerate the shaped rugelach on their sheets for at least 30 minutes (or freeze 15) so they hold their shape and stay flaky in the oven. Meanwhile, position a rack in the center and preheat the oven to 350F.
6. Top: Brush each chilled cookie with beaten egg. Stir together the 2 tbsp granulated sugar with a pinch of the leftover cinnamon sugar and sprinkle over the tops.
7. Bake: Bake at 350F for 22-26 minutes, rotating the sheet halfway, until the rugelach are deep golden brown and any leaked filling has caramelized. Some leakage is normal.
8. Cool: Let cool on the sheet for 5 minutes, then transfer to a wire rack (lift them off any hardened caramel while still warm so they don't stick). Cool completely before storing in an airtight container.

</details>

<sub>📷 Maor X, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-rugelach/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/Homemade_hamantaschen_%28cropped%29.jpg/480px-Homemade_hamantaschen_%28cropped%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Hamantaschen">

#### 🔺 [Classic Hamantaschen](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-hamantaschen/)
Jewish · snack · medium  
**$0.29/serving** · 144 kcal · P2 · C20 · F6 · ⏱ 120 min · 🍽 24  

Tender, lightly sweet triangular Purim cookies with a buttery citrus-scented dough folded around fruit jam and poppy-style filling. The dough is rolled thin, cut into rounds, dabbed with filling, and pinched into the signature three-cornered shape before baking until just golden at the edges.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 3 cups all-purpose flour, plus more for rolling
- **Baking powder** — 1 1/2 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Butter** — 3/4 cup (1 1/2 sticks) unsalted butter, softened
- **Sugar** — 3/4 cup granulated sugar
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs (plus 1 more for egg wash)
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Lemon zest** — zest of 1 lemon (about 2 tsp)
- **Orange juice** — 2 tbsp fresh orange juice
- **Apricot jam** — 1/2 cup apricot jam, for filling
- **Poppy seeds** — 2 tbsp poppy seeds, simmered with a little honey, for mohn-style filling _(optional)_
- **Honey** — 2 tbsp honey, to sweeten the poppy seed filling _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Make the dough: In a bowl, whisk together the flour, baking powder, and salt. In a separate large bowl, cream the softened butter and sugar with a hand mixer until pale and fluffy, about 2 to 3 minutes. Beat in the 2 eggs one at a time, then the vanilla, lemon zest, and orange juice until smooth.
2. Add the dry ingredients to the wet in two additions, mixing just until a soft dough forms and pulls together. Do not overwork it. Divide the dough in half, flatten each piece into a disk, wrap, and refrigerate at least 1 hour (or overnight) until firm. Chilled dough is essential for clean shaping.
3. Make the optional poppy filling: simmer the poppy seeds with the honey and a splash of water over low heat for 5 minutes until thickened to a spreadable paste, then cool. Keep the apricot jam ready as the second filling. Both should be thick, not runny, so they hold in the oven.
4. Preheat the oven to 350F (175C) and line two baking sheets with parchment. On a lightly floured surface, roll one disk of dough to about 1/8 inch thick. Using a 3-inch round cutter or glass, cut out circles, rerolling scraps once.
5. Place a scant teaspoon of filling in the center of each round. Do not overfill, or the cookies will burst open while baking.
6. Shape the triangles: lift three sides of the circle up and over the filling to form a triangle, pinching the three corners firmly to seal while leaving a small window of filling visible in the center. Pinch hard so the seams hold during baking.
7. Arrange the cookies about 1 inch apart on the prepared sheets. Beat the remaining egg and lightly brush the exposed dough for a glossy, golden finish.
8. Bake for 14 to 18 minutes, until the cookies are set and just golden at the edges and bottoms. Rotate the pans halfway through for even color.
9. Let the hamantaschen cool on the sheet for 5 minutes to firm up, then transfer to a rack to cool completely. Store in an airtight container for up to 5 days.

</details>

<sub>📷 Yoninah, CC BY 2.5, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-hamantaschen/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Cinnamon_Babka.jpg/480px-Cinnamon_Babka.jpg" alt="Photo of Cinnamon Babka">

#### 🍞 [Cinnamon Babka](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-cinnamon-babka/)
Jewish / Eastern European · snack · hard  
**$0.90/serving** · 420 kcal · P6 · C60 · F17 · ⏱ 240 min · 🍽 10  

A tender, enriched yeasted bread twisted around a buttery brown-sugar cinnamon filling, baked into a glossy laminated swirl and finished with a sticky vanilla sugar syrup. Rich, soft, and unmistakably bakery-quality.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — bread or all-purpose flour, about 3.75 cups for dough
- **Instant yeast** — 2 1/4 tsp
- **Whole milk** — 2/3 cup, warm, to bloom yeast and enrich dough
- **Sugar** — 1/3 cup granulated for the dough
- **Eggs** — 2 large, room temperature
- **Butter** — about 14 tbsp total: 6 tbsp softened into dough, 8 tbsp for filling
- **Salt** — 1 1/4 tsp fine salt in dough
- **Brown sugar** — 3/4 cup packed, for the cinnamon filling
- **Cinnamon** — 3 tbsp ground cinnamon for filling
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp, split between dough and syrup

**Steps**

1. Bloom the yeast: warm the milk to about 110F, stir in 1 tsp of the granulated sugar and the instant yeast, and let sit 5-8 minutes until foamy.
2. Make the dough: in a stand mixer with the dough hook, combine flour, remaining granulated sugar, and salt. Add the milk mixture, eggs, and 1 tsp vanilla, then mix on low until shaggy. Knead 5 minutes until cohesive.
3. With the mixer running, add the 6 tbsp softened butter one tablespoon at a time, kneading 8-10 minutes until the dough is smooth, elastic, and pulls cleanly from the bowl. Cover and proof 1.5-2 hours until doubled, then chill at least 1 hour (this makes it rollable).
4. Make the filling: stir together the brown sugar, cinnamon, and 8 tbsp softened butter into a spreadable paste.
5. Roll the cold dough on a floured surface into a roughly 12x18 inch rectangle. Spread the cinnamon filling evenly to the edges, then roll up tightly from a long side into a log.
6. Slice the log in half lengthwise to expose the layers. With cut sides facing up, twist the two halves over each other into a tight two-strand braid, keeping the cut layers showing.
7. Lay the braid into a parchment-lined 9x5 inch loaf pan, tucking the ends under. Cover and proof 45-60 minutes until puffy and risen near the rim.
8. Bake at 350F for 40-50 minutes until deep golden and the center reaches 190F; tent with foil if it browns too fast.
9. Meanwhile simmer 1/3 cup water with the remaining granulated sugar and 1 tsp vanilla into a syrup. Brush it generously over the hot babka and let cool in the pan 20 minutes before lifting out.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Hibachrach — CC BY-SA 4.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-cinnamon-babka/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4c/Ma%27amoul_Filled_with_Dates_and_Nuts.jpg/480px-Ma%27amoul_Filled_with_Dates_and_Nuts.jpg" alt="Photo of Ma'amoul (Date-Filled Semolina Cookies)">

#### 🍪 [Ma'amoul (Date-Filled Semolina Cookies)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-maamoul/)
Levantine (Lebanese/Syrian/Palestinian) · snack · medium  
**$0.12/serving** · 15 kcal · P0 · C2 · F1 · ⏱ 75 min · 🍽 12  

Tender, melt-in-the-mouth Levantine shortbread cookies made from a semolina-and-flour dough rich with ghee and perfumed with orange blossom and cardamom, stuffed with a spiced Medjool date paste. Traditionally pressed in carved wooden molds and baked pale, then dusted with powdered sugar — the iconic sweet of Eid and Easter across Syria, Lebanon, and Palestine.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Semolina** — fine semolina (smeed) — the backbone of the dough
- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose flour, for structure
- **Ghee** — or clarified butter (samneh), melted warm
- **Sugar** — just a touch in the dough — most sweetness comes from the dates
- **Cardamom** — ground; stands in for mahleb
- **Orange juice** — stands in for orange-blossom water; use 1 tbsp blossom water if you have it
- **Instant yeast** — tiny pinch, traditional for a lighter crumb
- **Milk** — warm, to bring the dough together
- **Medjool dates** — pitted, for the filling paste
- **Cinnamon** — ground, for the date filling
- **Nutmeg** — a pinch in the date paste _(optional)_
- **Powdered sugar** — for dusting the baked cookies
- **Walnuts** — optional — fold chopped walnuts into some for variety _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Make the dough: in a large bowl combine 1.5 cups fine semolina, 1/2 cup flour, 2 tbsp sugar, the ground cardamom, and a small pinch of instant yeast. Pour over the warm melted ghee and rub it in thoroughly with your fingertips until every grain is coated and the mixture feels like damp sand. Cover and rest at room temperature for at least 4 hours or overnight — this lets the semolina swell and is the secret to a tender cookie.
2. The next day, sprinkle the orange juice (or orange-blossom water) and just enough warm milk over the rested semolina, gathering gently into a soft, pliable dough. Do NOT knead — work it only until it just holds together, or the cookies will turn tough. Cover and let it relax 15 minutes.
3. Make the filling: blitz or mash the pitted Medjool dates with the cinnamon and a pinch of nutmeg into a smooth, sticky paste. If stiff, warm it briefly and work in a teaspoon of ghee so it rolls easily. Divide and roll into small marble-sized balls.
4. Shape: pinch off a walnut-sized piece of dough, flatten it in your palm into a cup, set a date ball in the center, and pull the dough up and over to fully enclose it. Pinch closed and smooth into a ball with no cracks.
5. Press each ball firmly into a floured ma'amoul mold (or shape by hand into a slightly flattened dome and mark the top with a fork or tweezers). Rap the mold once on the counter to release, and set the patterned cookie on a parchment-lined tray, pattern side up.
6. Heat the oven to 350F (175C). Bake the cookies on the middle rack for 15-20 minutes, JUST until the bottoms are lightly golden and the tops are still pale — ma'amoul should never brown on top or they go dry. Rotate the tray halfway through.
7. Let the cookies cool completely on the tray; they are fragile while warm and firm up as they cool. Once fully cool, dust generously with powdered sugar. Store in an airtight tin — they keep beautifully for up to two weeks and the flavor deepens by day two.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: روتانا — CC BY 4.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-maamoul/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/7/73/Kek_Lapis_Sarawak.jpg" alt="Photo of Kek Lapis Sarawak (Sarawak Layer Cake)">

#### 🍰 [Kek Lapis Sarawak (Sarawak Layer Cake)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-kek-lapis-sarawak/)
Malaysian · snack · hard  
**$0.99/serving** · 279 kcal · P5 · C19 · F20 · ⏱ 130 min · 🍽 16  

The classic spiced Sarawak layer cake from Malaysian Borneo: rich, buttery batter built one wafer-thin layer at a time, each grilled until golden before the next is poured on. The result is a tight stack of fragrant, custardy layers held together by their own caramelized tops, perfumed with cardamom, cinnamon and clove. This is the plain "lapis biasa" base layered into the signature horizontal stripes, finished with a slick of condensed-milk batter. Dense, tender and unmistakably festive.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Butter** — 1 1/2 cups (3 sticks) unsalted butter, softened
- **Sweetened condensed milk** — 1/2 cup (about half a 14 oz can) sweetened condensed milk
- **Eggs** — 10 large eggs, separated
- **Powdered sugar** — 3/4 cup powdered (icing) sugar, for whipping the whites
- **Sugar** — 3/4 cup granulated sugar, creamed with the butter
- **All-purpose flour** — 1 1/2 cups all-purpose (plain) flour, sifted
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Cardamom** — 1 tsp ground green cardamom
- **Cinnamon** — 1 tsp ground cinnamon
- **Ground cloves** — 1/2 tsp ground cloves
- **Nutmeg** — 1/4 tsp ground nutmeg
- **Baking powder** — 1 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Milk** — 2 tbsp whole milk, to loosen the batter if needed _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Line the base and sides of an 8-inch square pan with parchment, leaving a slight overhang. Position an oven rack in the upper third and set the oven to its top/grill (broiler) element. Have a flat-bottomed glass and a small offset spatula or back of a spoon ready for pressing each layer flat. Sift together the flour, cardamom, cinnamon, cloves, nutmeg, baking powder and salt; set aside.
2. Beat the softened butter with the granulated sugar until very pale and fluffy, 5 to 6 minutes. Add the egg yolks two at a time, beating well after each, then beat in the sweetened condensed milk and vanilla until the mixture is smooth, thick and glossy.
3. In a clean bowl, whip the egg whites until foamy, then add the powdered sugar in three additions, whipping to stiff, glossy peaks. The icing sugar gives the layers their fine, tight crumb.
4. Fold the sifted dry ingredients into the yolk-butter mixture in two additions just until combined. Then fold in the whipped whites in three additions, working gently to keep the volume. If the batter is stiffer than a thick ribbon, fold in the 2 tbsp milk. Aim for a batter that drops thickly from the spatula.
5. Spoon roughly 4 to 5 tablespoons of batter into the pan and spread it into a thin, even layer right to the corners, tapping the pan to level it. Grill (broil) on the upper rack until the top is evenly deep golden, about 4 to 6 minutes, rotating the pan once for even color. Watch constantly, as the thin layer can scorch fast.
6. Remove the pan, gently press the baked layer flat with the flat-bottomed glass to flatten any domes and pop large bubbles, and brush away nothing, this caramelized surface is the glue between layers. Spoon and spread the next 4 to 5 tablespoons of batter directly on top, then grill again until golden. Repeat, pressing flat between every layer, until all the batter is used, about 10 to 13 layers.
7. For the final layer, switch the oven to a standard bake at 325F (160C) and bake 8 to 10 minutes so the top sets through without over-darkening, then cool the cake in the pan for 15 minutes.
8. Lift the cake out by the parchment and cool completely on a rack. Wrap and rest at least a few hours, ideally overnight, so the layers settle and the spice deepens. Trim the four edges with a sharp knife to expose the stripes, then slice into neat fingers or squares to show off the layers.

</details>

<sub>📷 bob P-&-S, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-kek-lapis-sarawak/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/2/22/Concha_%28pan_dulce_mexicano%29_01.JPG" alt="Photo of Conchas (Mexican Pan Dulce)">

#### 🐚 [Conchas (Mexican Pan Dulce)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-conchas/)
Mexican · snack · medium  
**$0.67/serving** · 374 kcal · P7 · C49 · F16 · ⏱ 180 min · 🍽 12  

Classic Mexican sweet rolls: a soft, buttery, enriched yeast bread crowned with a crackly sugar-cookie paste scored in the iconic seashell pattern. Tender and lightly sweet with a sandy, snappy shell — half vanilla, half cinnamon-cocoa, just like the panadería.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 4 cups all-purpose flour, for the dough
- **Instant yeast** — 2 1/4 tsp (1 packet) instant yeast
- **Sugar** — 1/2 cup granulated sugar, for the dough
- **Salt** — 1 tsp salt
- **Whole milk** — 3/4 cup whole milk, warmed to 110F
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs, room temperature
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, softened, for the dough
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract, for the dough
- **Butter** — TOPPING: 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, softened
- **Powdered sugar** — TOPPING: 1 cup powdered sugar
- **All-purpose flour** — TOPPING: 1 cup all-purpose flour
- **Vanilla extract** — TOPPING: 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Cocoa powder** — TOPPING: 2 tbsp cocoa powder, for the chocolate half
- **Cinnamon** — TOPPING: 1 tsp ground cinnamon, for the cinnamon half _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Make the dough: In a large bowl whisk together the 4 cups flour, instant yeast, 1/2 cup granulated sugar, and salt. In a separate bowl whisk the warm milk (110F), eggs, and 2 tsp vanilla. Pour the wet into the dry and stir until a shaggy dough forms.
2. Knead in the softened 1/2 cup butter a tablespoon at a time, then knead on a lightly floured surface (or with a stand mixer dough hook) for 8-10 minutes until smooth, elastic, and slightly tacky. Shape into a ball, place in a greased bowl, cover, and let rise in a warm spot for 1 to 1 1/2 hours, until doubled.
3. Make the topping paste while the dough rises: cream the 1/2 cup softened butter with the powdered sugar until fluffy, then mix in the 1 cup flour and 1 tsp vanilla until it forms a smooth, play-dough-like paste. Divide in half: leave one half plain (vanilla), and knead the cocoa (plus cinnamon, if using) into the other half until evenly colored.
4. Divide each topping color into 6 equal balls (12 total). Flatten each between two squares of plastic wrap into a 3 1/2-inch disc, about 1/8 inch thick. Keep covered so they don't dry out.
5. Punch down the risen dough and divide into 12 equal pieces (about 3 oz each). Roll each into a tight, smooth ball and arrange on two parchment-lined baking sheets, spacing them about 3 inches apart.
6. Lay a topping disc over each dough ball, gently pressing it to cover the dome. Using a paring knife or a concha cutter, score the classic seashell or grid pattern into the topping, cutting through the paste but not into the dough.
7. Cover loosely and let proof 45-60 minutes, until puffy and nearly doubled; the topping will crack along the score lines. Meanwhile, preheat the oven to 350F (175C).
8. Bake one sheet at a time for 18-22 minutes, until the bread is cooked through and the bottoms are golden (the topping stays pale; don't wait for it to brown). Cool on a rack at least 15 minutes before eating — they are best the day they're baked.

</details>

<sub>📷 ProtoplasmaKid, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-conchas/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

---

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Concha_%28pan_dulce_mexicano%29_02.JPG/480px-Concha_%28pan_dulce_mexicano%29_02.JPG" alt="Photo of Mexican Conchas">

#### 🐚 [Mexican Conchas](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-mexican-conchas/)
Mexican · breakfast · medium  
**$0.80/serving** · 351 kcal · P8 · C43 · F16 · ⏱ 180 min · 🍽 8  

Pillowy-soft Mexican sweet bread (pan dulce) crowned with a crackly cinnamon-vanilla and cocoa sugar paste scored into the signature seashell pattern. A proper enriched dough — milk, butter, eggs, and a slow rise — gives these conchas that tender, fluffy crumb you get from a real panadería. Worth every minute of proofing.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose; ~3.5 cups split between dough and topping
- **Instant yeast** — 2 1/4 tsp (1 packet)
- **Whole milk** — 3/4 cup, warmed to lukewarm
- **Eggs** — 2 egg
- **Butter** — 1 1/4 sticks total — softened for dough and topping
- **Sugar** — granulated, for dough
- **Salt** — 1 tsp
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp, split dough/topping
- **Powdered sugar** — 1 1/2 cups, for the shell topping
- **Cocoa powder** — 2 tbsp, for the chocolate topping half
- **Cinnamon** — 1/2 tsp, for the vanilla topping half

**Steps**

1. Warm the milk to lukewarm (about 110F) and stir in the yeast with a pinch of the sugar; let it sit 5-10 minutes until foamy. In a large bowl, combine 2 3/4 cups flour, the granulated sugar, and salt.
2. Add the foamy milk, 2 eggs, 6 tbsp softened butter, and 1 tsp vanilla to the dry ingredients. Mix to a shaggy dough, then knead 10-12 minutes by hand (or 8 in a stand mixer) until smooth, elastic, and slightly tacky.
3. Place the dough in a greased bowl, cover, and let rise in a warm spot 1 to 1.5 hours, until doubled.
4. Make the topping: cream the remaining 4 tbsp softened butter with the powdered sugar, then work in 3/4 cup flour and 1 tsp vanilla until a soft, pliable paste forms. Divide in half — knead the cocoa into one half and the cinnamon into the other.
5. Punch down the dough and divide into 8 equal balls. Roll each smooth and arrange on parchment-lined baking sheets, leaving room to spread.
6. Divide each topping color into 4 portions. Flatten each into a thin disc between plastic wrap and drape one over each dough ball, gently pressing to cover the top.
7. Use a concha cutter or a paring knife to score the classic seashell or grid pattern into the topping. Cover loosely and proof 45-60 minutes, until puffy.
8. Bake in a preheated 350F oven for 18-20 minutes, until the bread is set and lightly golden at the edges and the topping is crackled. Cool on a rack at least 15 minutes before eating.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: ProtoplasmaKid — CC BY-SA 4.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-mexican-conchas/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Polvor%C3%B3n_casero_de_cacahuate.jpg/480px-Polvor%C3%B3n_casero_de_cacahuate.jpg" alt="Photo of Mexican Polvorones (Mexican Wedding Cookies)">

#### 🍪 [Mexican Polvorones (Mexican Wedding Cookies)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-mexican-polvorones/)
Mexican · snack · easy  
**$0.46/serving** · 110 kcal · P1 · C9 · F7 · ⏱ 75 min · 🍽 24  

Tender, crumbly shortbread cookies loaded with toasted pecans and warm cinnamon, rolled twice in powdered sugar so they melt the second they hit your tongue. A classic Mexican holiday cookie that comes together with pantry staples and no mixer required.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks), softened
- **Powdered sugar** — 1/2 cup in dough + ~1 cup for rolling/coating
- **All-purpose flour** — about 2 1/4 cups all-purpose
- **Pecans** — 1 cup, toasted and finely chopped/ground
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 1/2 tsp
- **Cinnamon** — 1 tsp, plus a pinch for the sugar coating
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp

**Steps**

1. Toast the pecans in a dry skillet over medium heat for 4-5 minutes until fragrant, then cool and finely chop or pulse in a blender to a coarse meal (do not over-process into paste).
2. In a large bowl, beat the softened butter with 1/2 cup of the powdered sugar until pale and fluffy, then beat in the vanilla.
3. Whisk the flour, 1 tsp cinnamon, and salt together, then add to the butter along with the ground pecans. Mix just until a soft, slightly crumbly dough forms.
4. Chill the dough for 20-30 minutes so it holds its shape, then preheat the oven to 350F (175C) and line two baking sheets with parchment.
5. Roll tablespoon-sized portions into 1-inch balls (or small crescents) and space them 1 inch apart on the sheets.
6. Bake 13-15 minutes, until the bottoms are lightly golden and the tops are set but still pale; the cookies should not brown on top.
7. Let the cookies rest on the sheet for 3-4 minutes until firm enough to handle but still warm.
8. While warm, roll each cookie in the remaining ~1 cup powdered sugar (mix in a pinch of cinnamon) to coat completely.
9. Cool fully, then roll a second time in powdered sugar for the classic snowy finish.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Petrvs III — CC BY-SA 4.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-mexican-polvorones/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/90/Kunafa%2C_Bangalore_%282025%29_03.jpg/480px-Kunafa%2C_Bangalore_%282025%29_03.jpg" alt="Photo of Kunafa">

#### 🧀 [Kunafa](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-kunafa/)
Middle Eastern (Levantine) · snack · medium  
**$0.37/serving** · 33 kcal · P1 · C2 · F2 · ⏱ 70 min · 🍽 8  

The classic Levantine cheese kunafa: a crisp, golden crown of buttery shredded kataifi pastry hiding a layer of molten stretchy cheese, drenched in fragrant lemon-cardamom sugar syrup and showered with crushed pistachios. Best served warm so the cheese pulls.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Phyllo dough** — 1 lb shredded kataifi (knafeh) dough, thawed
- **Ghee** — melted clarified butter/ghee for soaking the pastry
- **Mozzarella** — fresh low-moisture mozzarella standing in for akkawi/nabulsi cheese; soaked to desalt if using akkawi
- **Ricotta cheese** — softens and stretches the cheese layer (akin to sweet ashta)
- **Sugar** — for the attar syrup plus a little for the cheese
- **Lemon** — juice keeps the syrup from crystallizing
- **Green cardamom** — lightly crushed pods to perfume the syrup (rosewater/orange-blossom traditional)
- **Pistachios** — raw, finely crushed for garnish

**Steps**

1. Make the syrup (attar) first so it cools: combine sugar with an equal amount of water and the crushed cardamom pods in a small saucepan, bring to a boil, then add a squeeze of lemon juice and simmer 8-10 minutes until lightly syrupy. Remove from heat and let cool completely - cold syrup on hot kunafa is the secret to the crunch.
2. Preheat the oven to 400F (200C). Pull the kataifi dough apart in a large bowl, fluffing and separating the strands so they soak up butter evenly.
3. Pour the melted ghee over the kataifi and work it through with your fingers until every strand is glistening; this is what makes it shatteringly crisp and golden.
4. Tear the mozzarella into small pieces and mix with the ricotta and a spoonful of sugar to make a soft, spreadable cheese layer. If using salty akkawi, soak it in water first and pat dry.
5. Press just over half the buttered kataifi firmly into the bottom and a little up the sides of a buttered round or cast-iron pan, packing it into an even, compact base.
6. Spread the cheese mixture over the base in an even layer, leaving a small border, then cover completely with the remaining buttered kataifi and press down firmly so it holds together.
7. Bake 30-40 minutes until the top is deep golden and the edges pull away from the pan; for the classic crisp bottom, finish the last few minutes on the stovetop or broil briefly, watching closely.
8. Immediately invert the kunafa onto a serving plate while piping hot, then ladle the cooled syrup evenly over the top so it sizzles and soaks in.
9. Shower generously with crushed pistachios and serve warm at once, cutting into wedges so the cheese stretches.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Gpkp — CC BY-SA 4.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-kunafa/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e4/Fresh_batch_of_Biscochitos%2C_Albuquerque_NM.jpg/480px-Fresh_batch_of_Biscochitos%2C_Albuquerque_NM.jpg" alt="Photo of Buttermilk Biscochitos">

#### 🍪 [Buttermilk Biscochitos](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-buttermilk-biscochitos/)
New Mexican · snack · medium  
**$0.23/serving** · 163 kcal · P2 · C21 · F8 · ⏱ 95 min · 🍽 24  

New Mexico's official state cookie — a crisp, buttery anise-and-cinnamon shortbread. Buttermilk and a splash of brandy keep the crumb tender, while the warm anise seed and a cinnamon-sugar coating give these their unmistakable festive flavor.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — about 3 cups all-purpose flour
- **Butter** — 1 cup; traditionally lard, butter for richer flavor
- **White sugar** — 3/4 cup for the dough
- **Eggs** — 1 egg
- **Buttermilk** — 3 tbsp
- **White rum** — 2 tbsp brandy, traditional
- **Fennel seed** — 1.5 tsp anise seed, lightly crushed
- **Baking powder** — 1.5 tsp
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp _(optional)_
- **Cinnamon** — for the cinnamon-sugar coating
- **White sugar** — 1/4 cup for the cinnamon-sugar coating

**Steps**

1. In a large bowl, beat the butter (or lard) with the 3/4 cup sugar until very light and fluffy, 3-4 minutes. Crush the anise seed lightly in your fingers and beat it in along with the vanilla.
2. Beat in the egg until fully combined, then mix in the brandy and buttermilk. The mixture may look slightly broken; that's fine.
3. Whisk the flour, baking powder, and salt together, then add to the wet mixture in two additions, mixing just until a soft, smooth dough forms. Do not overwork it.
4. Gather the dough, flatten into a disk, wrap, and chill at least 1 hour (or up to overnight) so it firms up and the anise flavor blooms.
5. Heat the oven to 350F (175C). Roll the dough out on a lightly floured surface to about 1/4-inch thick and cut into the traditional fleur-de-lis shapes or simple rounds.
6. Stir together the 1/4 cup sugar and the cinnamon in a shallow dish. Press one side of each cookie gently into the cinnamon-sugar, or coat after baking while still warm.
7. Arrange cookies on parchment-lined sheets about 1 inch apart. Bake 10-12 minutes, until the bottoms are golden and the edges just barely color.
8. Cool on the sheet 2 minutes to set, then transfer to a rack. If you didn't coat before baking, dredge the warm cookies in the cinnamon-sugar now.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: John Phelan — CC BY-SA 3.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-buttermilk-biscochitos/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ac/Pineapple_Tarts_%2853544184751%29.jpg/480px-Pineapple_Tarts_%2853544184751%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Nyonya Pineapple Tarts (Open-Faced)">

#### 🍍 [Nyonya Pineapple Tarts (Open-Faced)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-pineapple-tarts/)
Nyonya (Peranakan) · snack · medium  
**$0.28/serving** · 102 kcal · P1 · C10 · F6 · ⏱ 150 min · 🍽 30  

The classic Southeast Asian Lunar New Year cookie: a melt-in-your-mouth, buttery shortcrust crowned with a glossy mound of thick, slow-cooked spiced pineapple jam. The pastry is enriched with egg yolks and a touch of cornstarch so it shatters tenderly, while the jam is cooked down low and slow until jammy, sticky, and deeply caramelized. Worth every minute of stirring.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Pineapple** — 4 cups fresh pineapple, finely grated or pulsed (about 1 large pineapple), for the jam
- **Sugar** — 1/2 cup sugar, for the jam
- **Cinnamon** — 1 tsp ground cinnamon (or 1 small stick), for the jam
- **Whole cloves** — 1/4 tsp ground cloves (or 2 whole cloves), for the jam
- **Bottled lemon juice** — 1 tbsp lemon juice, for the jam
- **All-purpose flour** — 2 cups all-purpose flour, for the pastry
- **Cornstarch** — 2 tbsp cornstarch, for a tender crumb
- **Powdered sugar** — 1/4 cup powdered sugar, for the pastry
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt
- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks) unsalted butter, softened
- **Eggs** — 2 large egg yolks (reserve 1 white for egg wash); 2 eggs total used
- **Vanilla extract** — 1/2 tsp vanilla extract

**Steps**

1. Make the jam first (it can be made a day ahead). Finely grate the pineapple or pulse it in a food processor to a coarse pulp, keeping all the juice. Tip everything into a wide, heavy nonstick pan with the sugar, cinnamon, cloves, and lemon juice.
2. Cook over medium heat, stirring often, for 45 to 70 minutes. As the liquid evaporates the pulp will turn from pale yellow to a deep golden-amber and become thick, sticky, and glossy. It is ready when you can drag a spoon across the pan and the jam holds a clear trail without weeping liquid. Spread it on a plate to cool completely, then roll into roughly 30 small balls (about 1 tsp each).
3. Make the pastry. Whisk together the flour, cornstarch, powdered sugar, and salt. In a separate bowl, beat the softened butter until creamy, then beat in the 2 egg yolks and vanilla until smooth and pale.
4. Add the dry ingredients to the butter mixture and mix just until a soft, slightly greasy dough comes together; do not overwork. Pat into a disc, wrap, and chill for 20 to 30 minutes so it firms up enough to shape. Meanwhile, heat the oven to 350F (175C) and line two baking sheets with parchment.
5. Roll the dough about 1/4 inch (6mm) thick on a lightly floured surface and cut out 1.5-inch rounds (or roll dough into balls and flatten). Place on the lined sheets, leaving a little space between each. Press a shallow dimple in the center of each round with your thumb or the back of a spoon.
6. Set a ball of cooled pineapple jam into each dimple, pressing gently so it adheres. Beat the reserved egg white with a teaspoon of water and lightly brush the exposed pastry (not the jam) for a golden sheen.
7. Bake for 18 to 22 minutes, rotating the sheets halfway, until the pastry edges are set and very lightly golden. They should stay pale; do not let them brown deeply or the buttery crumb turns dry.
8. Let the tarts cool on the sheet for 5 minutes, then transfer to a rack to cool completely; they firm up and become more tender as they cool. Store airtight at room temperature for up to 2 weeks.

</details>

<sub>📷 Choo Yut Shing from Singapore, Singapore, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-pineapple-tarts/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a2/Shoofly-pie-lancaster-county.jpg/480px-Shoofly-pie-lancaster-county.jpg" alt="Photo of Shoofly Pie">

#### 🥧 [Shoofly Pie](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-shoofly-pie/)
Pennsylvania Dutch · snack · medium  
**$0.96/serving** · 282 kcal · P3 · C51 · F8 · ⏱ 75 min · 🍽 8  

A classic Pennsylvania Dutch wet-bottom shoofly pie: a sticky, deeply molasses-y custard bottom under a buttery brown-sugar crumb, all baked in a flaky single crust. Old-fashioned, intensely flavored, and impossibly cozy with a cup of coffee.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Refrigerated pizza dough** — 1 unbaked 9-inch single pie crust (refrigerated pie shell)
- **Molasses** — 3/4 cup unsulphured (not blackstrap) molasses
- **Brown sugar** — about 1 1/4 cups packed, split between bottom and crumb
- **All-purpose flour** — about 1 1/2 cups all-purpose for the crumb
- **Butter** — 5 tbsp cold, cut into the crumb
- **Eggs** — 1 egg
- **Baking soda** — 1 tsp, dissolved in the hot water
- **Cinnamon** — 1/2 tsp in the crumb
- **Nutmeg** — pinch, freshly grated _(optional)_
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 375F and fit the pie crust into a 9-inch pie plate, crimping the edge; chill it in the fridge while you make the fillings.
2. Make the crumb: in a bowl, rub together the flour, about 3/4 cup of the brown sugar, the cinnamon, nutmeg, and salt with the cold butter using your fingertips until it forms loose, pea-size crumbs. Set aside.
3. Make the wet bottom: whisk the molasses, the remaining brown sugar, and the egg together in a separate bowl until smooth.
4. Bring 3/4 cup water to a boil, stir in the baking soda (it will foam), then whisk the hot soda-water into the molasses mixture; it should be thin and lightly frothy.
5. Pour the molasses liquid into the chilled crust, then gently scatter all of the crumb evenly over the top so it floats and partly sinks, sealing the surface.
6. Bake at 375F for 15 minutes, then lower to 350F and bake 25-30 minutes more, until the crumb is set and the center jiggles only slightly like a soft custard.
7. If the crust edge browns too fast, shield it with a ring of foil for the last 15 minutes.
8. Cool the pie completely on a rack, at least 2 hours, so the gooey bottom sets; serve at room temperature, plain or with coffee.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Good N Plenty — CC BY-SA 4.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-shoofly-pie/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/5/55/Kolaczki.jpg" alt="Photo of Kolaczki (Polish Cream Cheese Cookies)">

#### 🍪 [Kolaczki (Polish Cream Cheese Cookies)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-kolaczki/)
Polish · snack · medium  
**$0.28/serving** · 86 kcal · P2 · C7 · F6 · ⏱ 110 min · 🍽 30  

Tender, flaky Polish Christmas cookies made from a rich cream cheese and butter dough, cut into squares, filled with a spoonful of thick fruit jam, folded over the corners, and baked until pale gold. Finished with a snowfall of powdered sugar. The dough has no sugar in it at all — the contrast between the slightly tangy, melt-in-your-mouth pastry and the sweet jam is what makes them authentic.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 cups all-purpose flour, plus more for dusting
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, softened
- **Cream cheese** — 8 oz cream cheese, softened
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Apricot jam** — about 2/3 cup thick apricot jam (or lekvar / prune butter / raspberry), for filling
- **Powdered sugar** — 1/4 cup powdered sugar, for dusting
- **Eggs** — 1 egg, beaten, for egg wash (optional, helps corners seal and brown) _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Beat the softened butter and cream cheese together with a hand mixer until completely smooth and fluffy, 2-3 minutes. Beat in the vanilla and salt. Add the flour about half a cup at a time on low speed, mixing just until a soft, slightly sticky dough comes together — do not overwork it.
2. Divide the dough in half, pat each piece into a flat disk, wrap in plastic, and chill at least 1 hour (or overnight). The dough is too soft to roll until it is properly cold.
3. When ready to bake, preheat the oven to 375F and line two baking sheets with parchment. Working with one disk at a time and keeping the other chilled, roll the dough on a well-floured surface to about 1/8 inch thick.
4. Cut the dough into 2 to 2.5 inch squares with a knife or pizza cutter. Place a scant 1/2 teaspoon of thick jam in the center of each square — do not overfill or the cookies will leak.
5. Fold two opposite corners of each square up over the filling so they overlap slightly in the center, and press firmly to seal. For extra hold, dab the corners with a little beaten egg or water before pressing. Brush the tops lightly with egg wash if using.
6. Transfer the filled cookies to the lined sheets, spacing them about 1 inch apart. Chill the trays for 10 minutes if the dough has softened — cold dough keeps the corners from popping open.
7. Bake one sheet at a time for 12-15 minutes, until the cookies are set and just barely golden at the edges and bottom; they should stay pale on top. Watch closely after 12 minutes.
8. Cool on the pan for 5 minutes, then transfer to a rack to cool completely. Once fully cool, dust generously with powdered sugar through a fine sieve. Store airtight; dust again with fresh powdered sugar before serving.

</details>

<sub>📷 Kurman Communications LLC, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-kolaczki/)</sub>
